CYCLE ALPHA SCAN  ·  v1  ·  WHOLE-MARKET, EX-AI-INFRASTRUCTURE  ·  PAPER-ONLY RESEARCH

Fifty-six cycles are running outside the AI supercycle. Exactly one is still genuinely latent — so the edge has moved from finding undiscovered stories to re-pricing recognised ones the market walked away from.

This is the deliberate counterweight to the AI-infrastructure work: a top-down MECE sweep of 13 demand segments, 52 distinct cycles, each placed on its own clock and staged honestly against verified price action. The headline finding is uncomfortable for anyone hunting secrets: 36 of 56 cycles are already at recognition stage and only one is latent. But recognition is not the same as priced — the best-shaped setups are cycles where the fundamentals have measurably turned and the equity has not followed. Automotive semiconductors turned while NXPI trailed SOXX by roughly 70 points. Rheinmetall's backlog grew 30% while the stock fell 25%. Mid-market M&A advisers de-rated into a record deal backlog. Meanwhile the crowded end of the cycle world — refining, crude tankers, mature-node foundry, commercial aerospace — is paying peak multiples for peak earnings against supply that has already been ordered.

00

Read this first — in plain language

白話版top
What this is. Everything else in this research programme is about the AI build-out. This report deliberately looks everywhere else, and asks one question of each corner of the economy: is a cycle turning here, and has the stock market noticed yet?

Why cycles matter. Most of the economy moves in waves, not straight lines. Shops over-order, then stop ordering entirely. High prices trigger new mines and new ships, which arrive years later and crush the price. Banks lend loosely, take losses, then tighten. If you can tell where on the wave something sits, you know whether you are being paid to be patient or being set up to be the last buyer.

What we found. We mapped 52 separate waves across consumer, housing, cars, energy, uranium, metals, farming, industry, finance, healthcare, transport and non-AI chips. Almost all of them are already known stories — only one (farm chemicals) is genuinely still ignored. That sounds discouraging, but it points somewhere useful: the opportunity is no longer in finding a secret, it is in finding the handful of well-known cycles where the business has already started recovering while the share price has kept falling. Car-chip makers are the clearest example — their sales turned up this year, but their shares badly lagged the chip index.

The other half of the job. We also name the places to avoid: oil refining, oil tankers, older-generation chip factories and jet-engine servicing have all had enormous runs, and in each case the extra supply that ends such booms has already been ordered and is scheduled to arrive. Being late to a cycle is the most expensive mistake available, so those are marked as clearly as the opportunities.

What this is not. It is not advice and there is nothing to act on here. Every name is a research mention for further reading — a place to start, never a conclusion.

01

The framework — two orthogonal axes

top

Two questions decide everything in cycle investing, and they are independent of each other. Where on the clock is the cycle? and how much of the crowd already knows? A cycle can be early and crowded (dangerous), or mature and abandoned (often the better trade). Reading them separately is the whole method — and the third table shows exactly how the two combine into the score used to rank all 52.

Axis 1 — the phase (where on the clock)
PhaseWhat it meansWhat an early entrant does here
TroughThe bottom has formed but nothing has inflected yet. Volumes, orders or prices have stopped falling; the news is still bad and the sell-side still has cuts pending.This is the only phase where you are paid to be early. You buy the clock, not the earnings — and you must be able to sit through one more bad quarter.
Late downcycleStill falling, but the second derivative has turned: the rate of decline is shrinking and margins are carving a floor.Build the watchlist and define the trigger. You are close, but a trough that keeps extending is the single most expensive mistake in cycle investing.
Early upcycleThe inflection is visible in real data — orders, pricing, contract terms — but the crowd has not fully repriced it.The sweet spot when the crowd is still absent. Check honestly whether the stock has already moved: an early cycle with a late stock is not an entry.
Mid cycleThe move is recognised and running. Earnings are being revised up and the story is in the financial press.You are now paying for what everyone can see. Owning it can still work; entering fresh needs a genuine variant view, not agreement.
Late cycleThe driver is maturing. Upside needs ever-better news; incremental supply or demand destruction is being commissioned.Harvest, do not initiate. Watch for the supply answer that always arrives late in a commodity or capacity cycle.
PeakThe best case is priced. Positioning is crowded and the marginal buyer is a momentum buyer.Nothing to do on the long side. The asymmetry has flipped: the surprise that matters is now a downside one.
DowncycleRolling over, or in a correction inside a longer trend. Estimates are being cut.Distinguish a cyclical roll-over from a secular break, then wait for the trough. Falling knives look identical to bottoms until afterwards.
Axis 2 — the clock type (what drives the cycle)
Cycle typeTypical lengthWhat turns it
Inventory2–4 yearsThe bullwhip: the channel over-orders, then stops ordering entirely. It turns when distributor inventory-to-sales normalises and re-orders restart — usually before end-demand improves.
Capex / newbuild7–10 yearsHigh prices commission new capacity; capacity arrives years later and kills the price. It turns when the order book empties and replacement demand eventually forces a new round.
Credit5–8 yearsLending standards loosen, losses build, standards tighten, losses peak, then credit reopens. Charge-off rates peaking is the classic turn.
Commodity supply10 years or moreDiscovery-to-production lead times mean the supply answer cannot arrive quickly. It turns only when new mines, wells or plants actually commission — dates you can read years ahead.
Adoption S-curve10–20 yearsNot a cycle but a diffusion path, and it matters where on the S you are: the steep middle is where estimates are wrong in your favour; the flattening top is where they are wrong against you.
PolicyElection and budget cyclesLegislation, tariffs, export controls and central-bank reaction functions. Turns on dated events, which is what makes them tradable ahead of time.
Funding3–6 yearsCost of capital opens and shuts a sector's access to money. Winters end when the financing window reopens, not when the science improves.
Pricing3–6 yearsHard and soft markets in insurance and freight: pricing power passes between buyer and seller as capacity exits and re-enters.
How the Cycle Alpha Score is derived — not asserted
ComponentRangeRule
Phase earliness0-5Where the cycle sits on its own clock. trough 5.0 · early-upcycle 4.5 · late-downcycle 4.0 · early-mid-upcycle 3.5 · mid-upcycle 3.0 · mid-cycle 2.5 · downcycle 2.0 · late-cycle 1.0 · peak 0.0. A phase naming two labels scores as a weighted blend.
Awareness discount0-5How much of the crowd has already arrived, staged honestly against verified price action. latent 5.0 · early 4.0 · recognition 2.0 · euphoria 0.5 · priced 0.0.
Asymmetry0-5The analyst's payoff-shape call after weighing the bear case. high 5.0 · medium 3.0 · low 1.0.
Runway0-5Years of cycle left before the driver exhausts, read from the stated clock and duration view. Structural/multi-year 5.0 down to about-to-end 1.0. Exhaustion language is checked FIRST, and a cycle at or past its peak is capped (peak 1.5 · late-cycle 2.5 · downcycle 3.0) so no late-stage cycle can score years of runway on a stray word.

Every component is a function of a field the research agents were required to fill in, so the ranking is reproducible and comparable quarter to quarter rather than a matter of taste. Compound phases (“peak credit stress with an early-upcycle squeeze”) are scored as a weighted blend — leading label double weight — rather than being forced into one bucket. A high score is a research prior: it says the clock is early and the crowd has not arrived. It is not a recommendation, and it deliberately ignores valuation-of-the-vehicle, which the segment tables carry separately.

Glossary
Cycle vs secular trend — A cycle mean-reverts — the thing that causes the boom causes the bust. A secular trend does not. Confusing the two is how people buy the top of a cycle believing it is a trend.
Awareness stage — How much of the crowd has already arrived: latent (nobody is looking) → early → recognition (front page) → euphoria → priced. Alpha lives left of recognition.
Asymmetry — The shape of the payoff, not its direction. High asymmetry means the downside is bounded by something concrete while the upside is not yet priced.
Destocking — Customers running down inventory instead of ordering. Supplier revenue falls far more than end-demand does — and rebounds far more when it ends.
Book-to-bill — New orders divided by shipments. Above 1.0 means the order book is growing; it is the earliest clean read on an inventory-cycle turn.
Rate-lock (housing) — Owners with cheap mortgages will not sell into a higher rate, so transactions freeze. It thaws with time and life events, not only with rates.
Contango / backwardation — Futures above (contango) or below (backwardation) spot. Backwardation means physical buyers are paying up for metal or barrels now — a tightness tell.
Capitulation — Sellers exhausted: forced exits, capacity scrapped, coverage dropped. Ugly, and the necessary condition for a durable trough.
EV/EBITDA — What the whole business costs (share price plus debt, less cash) divided by its rough cash profit. Comparable across companies with different debt loads — and across a company's own history, which is how it is used here.
Forward vs TTM — TTM is the last twelve months actually reported; forward is the next twelve months estimated. A cheap-looking forward multiple is only as good as the estimate behind it.
FCF yield — Free cash flow divided by market value — the cash the business actually throws off, as a percentage of what you pay for it. A 5% FCF yield is roughly the inverse of a 20x multiple.
Book value / NAV — What the assets are worth on paper. Used for banks, shippers and miners, where 1.0x book is a natural anchor and 2-3x book for peak-cycle earnings is the classic late-cycle warning.
Basis point (bp) — One hundredth of a percentage point. 47bp = 0.47%. Used for interest rates and spreads, where 0.5% moves matter.
SAAR — Seasonally adjusted annual rate — a monthly figure scaled to a full year and stripped of seasonal noise, so months are comparable. Standard for car sales and housing starts.
FID — Final investment decision — the moment a large project is committed and money starts being spent. FIDs are dated and public, which is what makes multi-year capacity waves readable years ahead.
Orderbook-to-fleet — Ships on order as a percentage of the existing fleet — the single best supply gauge in shipping. Roughly 10% is lean; 30%+ means the supply answer is already ordered.
02

The cycle map — every non-AI cycle, placed

全景圖top

Every one of the 52 cycles, placed on the clock. Read it left to right: the left-hand columns are where an early entrant is paid to be patient, the right-hand columns are where the supply answer has already been ordered. Chip colour is the remaining asymmetry, so the ideal hunting ground is a teal chip in a left-hand column — early on the clock and still favourably shaped.

The board is deliberately MECE: 13 segments cover the non-AI economy, and every segment's cycles are placed somewhere. Compound phases are placed by their leading label; the segment cards below give the full reasoning and the dated evidence behind each placement.

03

Segment × clock type — the MECE grid

top

The second axis. The same 52 cycles arranged by what kind of clock is running, which matters because each type turns for a different reason and is read with a different indicator. A segment can run several at once — energy has a commodity-supply clock and a newbuild clock simultaneously — which is precisely why treating a sector as one cycle is the most common analytical error here.

04

Cross-segment alpha ranking

排序top

All 52 cycles ranked by the derived Cycle Alpha Score, best-shaped first. The bar is stacked so you can see why each one ranks where it does: a cycle can score well for being genuinely early, for being ignored, for payoff shape, or for having years of runway left — and the composition tells you which.

Showing the top 24 of 52; the full list with phase, stage and trigger is in the reconciliation table below. The score deliberately ignores the valuation of any particular vehicle, which the segment tables carry separately — a well-shaped cycle can still be expressed through an expensive stock.

05

Awareness curve — who already knows

覺察曲線top

The honesty check. Cycle staging is worthless if it is not tested against what prices have already done, so every placement here was cross-checked against verified year-to-date moves and valuation versus history. This is the chart that shows how thin genuine latency has become: one cycle out of 52 sits in the left-hand band, and the dashed marker sits beside it.

06

Multi-year roadmap — when each clock rings

top

When each clock actually rings. Cycles are not simultaneous, and the dates below are mostly not forecasts — they are construction schedules, contracted deliveries, orderbooks and legislated budgets that can be read years ahead. That is what makes them tradable in advance rather than in hindsight.

07

Four cycles in depth — teardowns

四大拆解top

Four teardowns in depth. Each tab shows that cycle’s computed score and rank, taken straight from the rubric rather than asserted here. Selection rule, stated so it can be argued with: high-scoring cycles that are also in different segments, expressible through listed vehicles a retail investor can actually reach, and not substantially AI-infrastructure demand wearing another label. That last filter is why uranium utility contracting, which ranks in the top five, is covered in its segment card instead — a material part of its demand narrative belongs to the programme this report is the counterweight to. Because the filters are applied after ranking, these four are not simply the top four by score; the full ranked list is in §04 and §11.

China consumer — the market has repriced the policy, not the cycle

EARLY19.0 / 20 · rank 01
The highest-scoring cycle in the scan, and the one most likely to be misread. China's consumer is at a genuine demand trough — May retail sales fell 0.6% year-on-year, the first contraction since 2023, and consumer-goods PPI is still −0.9% while headline CPI holds at +1.0%. What changed in July is the policy architecture: Beijing escalated from gadget trade-in subsidies, whose budget is actually shrinking, to a structural 15th-Five-Year-Plan consumption push. The equity complex is down in 2026 (PDD −20.5%, BABA −13.2%, KWEB −15.6%) at multi-year-low multiples. You are buying trough data at trough multiples with a policy put — and the honest caveat is that “China stimulus” is a widely covered headline, so what is early here is the demand turn, not the story.
Cycle type
policy
Clock
2-4y policy loop
Phase
trough
Anchor
PDD ~9x fwd ex-cash
① 怎麼賺錢 — how the money is made

These are the toll-collectors on Chinese household spending. PDD takes a take-rate on value-commerce GMV; BABA on marketplace GMV plus cloud; YUMC earns restaurant-level margin on dining traffic. All three convert a recovery in volume of transactions into operating leverage without needing price inflation.

② 營收結構 — revenue mix

PDD is the purest low-end domestic consumption read. BABA is deliberately mixed — a meaningful part of its 2026 story is cloud and AI, which is why it is the least clean expression of this specific cycle. YUMC is almost entirely services consumption, and is dual-listed in Hong Kong, so it carries no ADR-delisting tail.

③ 成長來源 — where growth comes from

Not population or income growth — a reversal of deflation psychology. Consumer-goods PPI crossing back above zero would end downstream price deflation; retail sales sustaining above 3% without a fresh subsidy injection would prove the turn is organic rather than purchased.

④ 終端市場 — end market

Chinese household consumption, targeted at roughly ¥60T of retail sales by 2030 under the new plan. The 2024-25 trade-in program pulled appliance and auto demand forward, which is why H1-2026 appliances fell 7.4% and autos 12.6% — that payback is the trough being measured here.

⑤ 主要客戶 — who buys

The mass-market Chinese household, currently deleveraging against a still-falling property market. This is the crux: the new consumption-loan interest subsidy adds leverage to a balance sheet that is trying to reduce it, which is a real reason the policy may not transmit.

⑥ 護城河 — the moat

Network effects and logistics density that a stimulus cycle does not change. The moat is not the reason to own these now; the moat is the reason the downside is “more stagnation at 9x” rather than impairment.

⑦ 管理層展望 — management view

Read the July State Council consumption plan and the August consumption-loan scheme as the authoritative guidance here, not company commentary — in a policy cycle the policymaker is effectively management. The plan is dated and explicit; the transmission is not.

⑧ 為何是卡點 — why this is the binding constraint

Because everything else in the Chinese economy has already reflated and consumption has not. Upstream PPI is +4.1%, the steepest since July 2022, driven by mining at +16.5% — that is the anti-involution supply cut, not demand. Consumer-goods PPI at −0.9% is the one line that has not turned, which makes it the binding constraint on the whole reflation and the cleanest single number to watch.

The information gap — why this is not priced. Consensus is trading the announcement and will not re-underwrite until the data confirms. The gap is that a policy loop of this kind historically takes two to four quarters from plan to data inflection, so the window between the July plan and a mid-2027 confirmation is exactly where an early entrant sits — and it is also where the thesis gets falsified cheaply. Set the falsifier now: if consumer-goods PPI is not back to zero or better by mid-2027, this trough was secular, not cyclical.
Bull. A single upturn in retail sales or consumer-goods PPI re-rates a complex sitting at record-low multiples; the plan targets consumption explicitly through 2030, and the policy put is now structural rather than episodic.
Bear. The consumer-facing stimulus is shrinking — the trade-in budget was cut 17% year-on-year — and May's −0.6% shows organic demand contracts the moment subsidies pause. Adding household leverage mid-deleveraging against a falling property market may simply not transmit, and the US-listed proxies carry a geopolitical and ADR discount that a consumer recovery alone will not close.

US natural gas — trough price against a contracted, dated demand step

EARLY18.8 / 20 · rank 02
This is the best-shaped setup in the scan, and the shape is the point: you are entering at trough pricing — Henry Hub at $2.76 with storage 185 Bcf above the five-year average — ahead of a demand increase that is already contracted and under construction. US LNG export capacity goes from 17 Bcf/d at end-2025 to over 19 Bcf/d during 2026 and keeps climbing into 2027-28 as Golden Pass Trains 2 and 3 and Corpus Christi Stage 3 land. The split that matters: the infrastructure owners have already been repriced (Cheniere +32.8%, Venture Global +82.3% year-to-date), while the molecule has not — EQT is flat, Expand Energy −14.2%, all sitting 20-25% below their 52-week highs.
Cycle type
commodity + newbuild
Clock
FID→cargo 4-5y
Phase
trough → early
Anchor
HH $2.76 vs EIA $3.57 Q4-26F
① 怎麼賺錢 — how the money is made

Two different businesses that people conflate. The E&Ps (EQT, Expand, Antero) sell molecules and make money on the price of gas. The liquefiers (Cheniere, Venture Global) are toll-collectors on 20-year contracts and make money on volume regardless of price. The cycle repricing ahead sits with the first group.

② 營收結構 — revenue mix

EQT is the largest US gas producer with Appalachia-to-Gulf integration through Mountain Valley Pipeline. Expand Energy is the #1/#2 producer sitting closest to Gulf demand across Haynesville and Appalachia. Antero carries the highest LNG-corridor exposure plus an NGL kicker, which makes it the torque name of the three.

③ 成長來源 — where growth comes from

Not drilling more — the gas rig count is only 127 and producers have stayed disciplined at $2.76. Growth comes from the demand side arriving on a construction schedule someone else is paying for. That asymmetry — flat supply discipline meeting contracted demand — is the entire thesis.

④ 終端市場 — end market

Global LNG buyers on long-term contracts, plus domestic power burn. The war ran the strategic-value experiment for you: losing roughly 10 Bcf/d of Qatari LNG for four months spiked European gas 63% in a week and took Asian spot above $23/MMBtu, demonstrating what US export capacity is worth when supply is disrupted.

⑤ 主要客戶 — who buys

Utilities and trading houses in Europe and Asia signing 20-year offtake, which is what makes the demand step contractual rather than forecast. The liquefiers hold those contracts; the producers sell into the resulting domestic pull.

⑥ 護城河 — the moat

For the liquefiers, permits and 20-year contracts — a genuine moat, and the reason they already re-rated. For the producers, low-cost acreage adjacent to the demand: Antero's FCF breakeven near $2.60 Henry Hub is the kind of hard anchor that defines how long you can wait.

⑦ 管理層展望 — management view

Producers are pointedly not chasing: 127 gas rigs at $2.76 is restraint, not incapacity. The EIA is the authority to disagree with here — its own outlook has Henry Hub easing below $3.50 in 2027 on record production. This trade is explicitly a bet against that complacency.

⑧ 為何是卡點 — why this is the binding constraint

Storage is the constraint that converts the demand step into price. Today's +185 Bcf surplus is what caps the market; when the LNG pull plus normal weather flips that surplus into a deficit, the repricing is mechanical. The year-on-year comparison is already −32 Bcf, so the surplus is eroding before the biggest trains arrive.

The information gap — why this is not priced. The market is anchored on the EIA's 2027 sub-$3.50 forecast and on the fact that gas has disappointed for three years. What it is under-weighting is that the 2022-23 final-investment-decision class lands on a construction schedule, not a price forecast — the cargoes get exported whether or not Henry Hub cooperates. The recognition trigger is public, free and weekly: EIA's Thursday storage report crossing from surplus into deficit. Position before that, not after.
Bull. Storage flips to deficit through 2027 as the demand step lands against a disciplined 127-rig supply response, repricing the strip toward $4.50 and re-rating E&Ps that are currently priced for $3 forever.
Bear. Record Permian associated gas — a free byproduct of oil drilling — plus a Haynesville restart swamps the LNG pull. The EIA is right, the surplus never flips, gas E&Ps stay value traps, and the 2027+ global LNG wave caps the export arbitrage. A warm winter buys another year of trough.

Crop protection — the only genuinely latent cycle in the scan

LATENT18.0 / 20 · rank 03
Of 52 cycles surveyed, this is the only one still staged latent, and it earned that stage the hard way. FMC's July print was a textbook capitulation quarter: revenue −17%, a third guidance cut, the India business divested and the dividend held at a post-cut $0.08/quarter (~3.0% yield), with the stock at $10.36 — down 75% from its 52-week high on a $1.3bn market capitalisation. Management's own words are the setup: “2026 may mark the bottom, with growth returning in 2027.” Corteva, the quality end of the same end-market, is +17.4% year-to-date, which is the proof this is an inventory-and-price cycle rather than an end-market collapse. The catch is that this is levered asymmetry, and saying so is the whole discipline.
Cycle type
inventory
Clock
~3y channel destock
Phase
trough
Anchor
FMC ~7x cut-guide EPS
① 怎麼賺錢 — how the money is made

Selling crop-protection chemistry — herbicides, insecticides, fungicides — into a distribution channel that resells to farmers. Because it moves through distributors, the revenue a manufacturer books depends on what the channel orders, not what farmers spray. That gap is what creates the cycle.

② 營收結構 — revenue mix

FMC is a pure-play crop-protection manufacturer, which is why it takes the full force of the cycle. Corteva is roughly half seed and half crop protection, and seed pricing power funds the wait — the reason its share price went the other way while it faces the same channel.

③ 成長來源 — where growth comes from

Purely from the channel refilling. Nothing needs to be invented: distributors bought heavily in 2021-22 on supply fear, spent 2023-25 running inventory down, and manufacturers' revenue fell far more than actual on-farm usage did. When ordering restarts, reported revenue rebounds far more than usage does too.

④ 終端市場 — end market

Row-crop farmers, whose budgets are set by farm income. Ex-government-payment net farm income of $109bn in 2026 is the smallest in six years — that austerity is the demand-side driver of the destock, and it is why the cycle cannot turn on inventory mechanics alone.

⑤ 主要客戶 — who buys

Agricultural distributors and retailers. Their inventory decisions, not farmer demand, set the clock — which is why the tell is organic volume growth in the manufacturers' own quarterly disclosure rather than any commodity price.

⑥ 護城河 — the moat

Weakening, and this must be said plainly. FMC's diamide insecticide patents are rolling off, and Chinese and Indian generic capacity keeps ag-chem pricing in secular deflation. The destock is cyclical; the pricing pressure is not, and the 2019-22 margin structure is not coming back.

⑦ 管理層展望 — management view

An explicit bottom call for 2026 with growth returning in 2027, echoed by the equipment makers facing the same farmer. Treat a management bottom-call as a data point on the clock, not as a promise — the same managements guided down three times this year.

⑧ 為何是卡點 — why this is the binding constraint

The binding constraint is the balance sheet racing the cycle. Roughly $3.3bn of net debt against a $650m EBITDA guide is about 5x levered, so the equity is effectively an option on the channel refilling before a refinancing or dilution. That is exactly why the stage is latent and the asymmetry is high — and exactly why position size, not conviction, is the operative decision.

The information gap — why this is not priced. Nobody is publishing on a $1.3bn market cap that just cut its dividend ~86% after three guidance cuts. The information gap is not analytical — the destock mechanics are well understood — it is attentional: coverage and institutional ownership have left, so there is no marginal buyer to price the 2027 recovery until the volume line turns positive in a public filing. The cleanest confirmation is free: two consecutive quarters of positive organic volume in FMC's release, cross-checked against Corteva's crop-protection segment volume.
Bull. Year-three destock trough plus capitulation pricing plus a company-guided 2027 volume recovery is the classic setup, and from a $1.3bn capitalisation a normal cyclical recovery is worth multiples of the equity.
Bear. The destock can end without a price recovery. Generic capacity keeps pricing deflating, the diamide patent cliff means the old margin structure never returns, and a 5x-levered balance sheet can force dilution or asset sales before the 2027 turn arrives. If grain stays below breakeven, grower austerity buys a second year of “bottom” — and value traps are the default outcome in capitulated chemicals.

Automotive semiconductors — the same restock, one cycle earlier, at half the multiple

EARLY17.5 / 20 · rank 06
This is the cleanest “fundamentals turned, price didn't” case in the entire scan, and it is measurable inside single companies: Analog Devices' industrial revenue grew 56% year-on-year in its fiscal Q2 while its automotive revenue grew 2%. The auto leg runs four to six quarters behind industrial, and it has just turned — NXP's automotive revenue rose 12% and ST's 16%. Yet NXPI is +6.5% year-to-date against SOXX +76.2%, a roughly 70-point relative deficit, at 13.5x EV/EBITDA versus Texas Instruments at 29.0x. You are offered the same restock mechanics as the crowded industrial leg, several quarters earlier, at roughly half the multiple. Note also that this is deliberately the ex-AI semiconductor cycle — the segment's contaminated corners are excluded rather than counted.
Cycle type
inventory
Clock
3-4y, lags industrial
Phase
early upcycle
Anchor
NXPI 13.5x vs TXN 29.0x
① 怎麼賺錢 — how the money is made

Selling analog and mixed-signal chips — radar, sensors, microcontrollers, power management — into vehicles through Tier-1 suppliers. Revenue is a function of content per vehicle multiplied by builds, but in the short run it is dominated by what the channel is holding.

② 營收結構 — revenue mix

NXP is the largest pure-ish automotive franchise at roughly 55% automotive revenue. Melexis is the cleanest read of all — a Belgian pure-play automotive sensor maker with no AI-datacenter revenue whatsoever. Allegro is the maximum-torque name on EV powertrain and ADAS sensing, priced accordingly at 76x EV/EBITDA on near-zero net margin.

③ 成長來源 — where growth comes from

Two stacked sources. Near term, the channel restocking after a three-year destock. Beyond that, content per vehicle rising 5-8% a year through software-defined vehicles, electrification, ADAS and ultra-wideband — which has to do all the work, because units are not growing.

④ 終端市場 — end market

Global light-vehicle production of roughly 92.6M units in 2026, essentially flat year-on-year. An independent audit of this report corrected the regional detail: the July-2026 vintage actually revised most regions up (Europe +144k, South Asia +181k, Japan +111k, North America +66k), with Greater China the notable cut. That correction cuts in the thesis's favour, but the honest weakness stands — units are not growing, so content per vehicle must carry the case.

⑤ 主要客戶 — who buys

Tier-1 suppliers and OEMs. Their inventory behaviour is the swing factor, and it is currently distorted: NXP carries 11 weeks of channel inventory versus 9 a year ago, much of it a hedge Tier-1s built after the 2025 Nexperia export shock.

⑥ 護城河 — the moat

Automotive qualification cycles and design-in periods measured in years, which make revenue sticky once won. This is a genuine moat and it is why the auto leg lags in both directions — slow to fall, slow to recover.

⑦ 管理層展望 — management view

NXP guides Q3 to $3.65-3.85bn, or +15-21% year-on-year. The more informative signal is the dissent: UBS cut NXPI to Neutral on 2026-08-03 citing Chinese automotive inventory-correction risk. A downgrade at the start of an upswing is the marker of an unloved early leg rather than a crowded one.

⑧ 為何是卡點 — why this is the binding constraint

China is the binding constraint, and it is genuinely two-sided. Chinese EV sales fell 21% year-on-year in Q1, over half of Chinese dealers are loss-making, and AI-driven memory scarcity has pushed automotive-grade memory prices up more than 150%, compressing OEM margins to about 2.9%. If the Tier-1 hedge inventory unwinds, the restock reverses into a second destock and the cheap multiple turns out to be cheap for a reason.

The information gap — why this is not priced. The market is pricing the automotive leg off the industrial leg's late-cycle position and off China headline risk, without separating the two legs that sit inside the same companies' disclosures. The disaggregation is public and free: read the segment splits in ADI's and onsemi's quarterlies, where industrial and automotive are reported separately and are 54 points apart. The specific number that resolves the debate is NXP's disclosed channel inventory in weeks — above 12 and the restock is borrowed demand, back below 9 and a genuinely clean second leg becomes possible.
Bull. The same restock that carried the industrial names for six quarters now runs through automotive, at half the multiple, with a real content-per-vehicle second leg behind it and sentiment still openly negative.
Bear. Units are flat at best, so content must carry everything. China cracks, the 11-week Tier-1 hedge inventory unwinds into a second destock, and the 55% valuation discount to the industrial leg proves to be a correct assessment of a structurally worse end market rather than an opportunity.
08

Segment by segment — the full MECE sweep

分部門top

The full sweep, segment by segment, in top-down order: the macro overlay that scales everything, then the demand segments. Each cycle carries its dated evidence with two independent sources per load-bearing fact, the listed vehicles with verified year-to-date moves and one hard valuation anchor, and the bear case. Each segment also states honestly which part of its demand is really AI infrastructure and has therefore been excluded rather than counted — in several segments that exclusion is what changes the conclusion.

Global macro overlay (Tier-0)

4 cyclesbest: Europe fiscal turn 15.5/20 · rank 10

The 2026 Tier-0 regime is "stalled easing, steep curve, dollar pause": the Fed has been frozen at 3.50-3.75% for five straight meetings with three dissenters wanting a HIKE (tariff+Iran-war CPI at 3.5%), so the long end is doing the tightening — 30Y at 5.27%, highest since 2007 — while QT is over and the balance sheet is growing again. The structural USD downcycle that began in 2025 (worst year since 2017) is in a countertrend rally (+4% YTD) that has punished consensus anti-dollar trades (gold -23.6% off its Feb peak, though a two-session rally has since taken it back to roughly flat YTD). Underneath, three real non-US cycles are running: an 18-month-old EM-vs-DM relative leg (narrow, AI-contaminated), Japan's generational reflation (year 3 wage loop, now showing yen/JGB late-cycle stress at a 40-year currency low), and Europe's contractually locked fiscal/rearmament capex ramp whose equity pricing just went through a full euphoria-and-washout inside 18 months.

Europe fiscal turn: rearmament + infrastructure capex cycle

RECOGNITIONcapex15.5/20
phase 4.5aware 2.0asym 5.0runway 4.0
phase early-upcycleclock state-led capex supercycle 7-10y: NATO ramp to 3.5% GDP by 2029, German 500B-euro infrastructure fund to ~2036; order-to-revenue lags 2-4y; equity pricing already completed one euphoria-washout loop (2025-26)asymmetry high

Where on the clock. The money is now flowing, not just announced: Germany's approved 2026 defense budget is 108.2B euros (82.7B core + 25.5B special fund, ~+29B vs prior plan), 2.8% of GDP rising to 3.5% by 2029, plus 58.9B of infra-fund borrowing in 2026 and >180B total new federal debt. Yet the flagship equities de-rated hard in 2026: Rheinmetall -25.5% YTD (-42% off its high), Stoxx A&D negative YTD vs Stoxx 600 +4.8% — pricing a peace deal that keeps stalling.

Who already knows. This was 2025's euphoria trade (Rheinmetall +1,300% over 5y); the 2026 correction has reset it to recognition — everyone knows the budgets, but positioning has been flushed by the April peace-plan scare and JPMorgan downgrades, and the sector now trades below the broad index YTD despite record backlogs.

Why the payoff is shaped this way. Rare setup: contractually locked decade-long demand (treaty targets + constitutional-grade German funds) meeting a 26-42% drawdown in the lead names. The binary ceasefire risk is partly self-hedged — a signed peace shifts spending toward reconstruction/infrastructure, the other half of the same fiscal turn.

Runway. Outlays ramp through 2029 (defense) and 2036 (infrastructure) — 3-8y of runway; ends via a genuine peace dividend plus fiscal fatigue/populist reversal, or bond-market enforcement (Bund >3.5%) crowding out the non-defense half.

Bear case. Defense multiples built for a decade-war scenario (Rheinmetall still ~30x fwd est. after the correction) would have another full de-rating leg, and the reconstruction offset arrives years later than the market can wait.

EM-vs-DM relative equity cycle

EUPHORIAcredit11.0/20
phase 4.5aware 0.5asym 3.0runway 3.0
phase early-upcycleclock EM-vs-DM relative legs run 7-10y per direction (EM led 2001-10, DM led 2011-24), driven by the USD cycle + growth differential; this EM leg is ~18 months oldasymmetry medium

Where on the clock. EEM +17.6% YTD vs SPY +11.1% (Aug-4), second consecutive year of MSCI EM outperformance and its strongest run in 5 years — classic year-2 behavior of a new relative leg after a 13-year DM regime. But the leg stalled with the 2026 dollar bounce (EEM -10% off its June high), confirming the USD cycle is its master clock.

Who already knows. LPL, Lazard, BofA and MSCI itself are all publishing 'EM on top' pieces — no longer early. But the aggregate hides a split: the recognized part is AI Asia (EWY +74.0% YTD, +132% 1Y = priced/euphoric); the unloved ex-AI half (KWEB -15.6% YTD, INDA -7.2% YTD) is still latent-to-early.

Why the payoff is shaped this way. Aggregate EM after +32% in 1Y is no bargain, and the biggest winner (Korea) is a crowded AI trade -16% off its high. The asymmetry lives in the laggards — China internet at -43% over 5y and India after 18 months of underperformance — which get a mechanical bid if the dollar downleg resumes.

Runway. If the historical 7-10y relative clock holds and the USD downcycle resumes, this leg has years left; it ends early on a dollar upcycle or a China credit event.

  • EM outperforming DM for a 2nd straight year: EEM +17.6% YTD vs SPY +11.1%; MSCI EM having its strongest run vs the S&P in 5 years — +6.5pp YTD spread as of 2026-08-04FMP quote-change (EEM/SPY · MSCI markets-in-motion + LPL
  • Breadth is narrow: Korea EWY +74.0% YTD (+132% 1Y, verified vs 12/31 close 97.22) while KWEB -15.6% and INDA -7.2% YTD — the median EM is not participating — EWY +74.0% vs KWEB -15.6% YTD as of 2026-08-04FMP quote-change + EOD chart · Fund Selector Asia

Add a resumed dollar upcycle (the 2026 rally extending) and unresolved China property/deflation, and EM gives back the entire relative leg as it did in 2011 and 2013.

Japan reflation / governance cycle

RECOGNITIONpolicy11.0/20
phase 2.0aware 2.0asym 3.0runway 4.0
phase mid-cycleclock once-a-generation deflation exit: annual shunto wage cycle (year 3 above 5%), BOJ normalization arc 3-5y (2024->), TSE governance-reform cycle ~10y (year 4); fiscal-dominance stress now compressing the clockasymmetry medium

Where on the clock. The wage-price loop is real (shunto 5.01% final, third year >=5%; BOJ at 1.00%, highest since 1995, warning inflation may exceed 2%), and TOPIX set a record 4,102 on Jul-6 led by banks/value while AI semis were sold. But the funding side is straining: Takaichi's Y21.3T stimulus triggered the biggest JGB rout since 1999 (Jan-20), 10Y hit a 29-yr high 2.88%, and MOF had to intervene at ~163/USD on Jul-30.

Who already knows. The equity reflation trade is fully recognized — TOPIX at all-time highs, Mizuho +40% YTD/+205% 3Y, every macro fund long Japan banks. The UNRECOGNIZED piece is the currency: yen at a 40-year nominal low with FXY -0.3% YTD, priced as if BOJ normalization and US cuts never resume.

Why the payoff is shaped this way. Equity longs are late (records, crowded banks); the residual asymmetry sits in yen normalization (intervention has drawn a line at ~163, BOJ has 2-3 hikes left, US 2Y sits 320bp above Japan's) and in domestic small caps — while the JGB tail risk caps how hard one can lean on the whole complex.

Runway. Wage loop and governance reform run to ~2030 (2-3 more BOJ hikes to ~1.5-1.75% est. neutral); the cycle ends early only via a disorderly yen/JGB spiral forcing violent tightening, or a global recession breaking the wage momentum.

  • Suspected MOF intervention Jul-30: yen jumped 162.8 -> ~157/USD in an hour from a 40-year low; USDJPY 157.7 on Aug-4 — USDJPY 157.7 post-intervention as of 2026-08-04Japan Times (above · FMP forex quote USDJPY

Bear case. Fiscal dominance wins: Takaichi keeps spending, the 40Y stays above 4%, the yen breaks 165 despite intervention, and the BOJ is forced into crisis hikes that crush both JGBs and the equity rally — the 'reflation trade' turns into a currency-crisis trade. Conversely, a fast yen recovery to 145 mechanically guts the exporter/DXJ leg and the yen-translation earnings that drove TOPIX records.

USD / Fed-liquidity / curve-regime cycle

RECOGNITIONpolicy10.0/20
phase 2.0aware 2.0asym 3.0runway 3.0
phase downcycleclock USD long swings ~15-17y full cycle, downlegs 6-9y (1985-92, 2002-08; upcycle 2011-2025); overlaid Fed easing cycle 3-4y (stalled mid-path); term-premium regime turns are decadalasymmetry medium

Where on the clock. Dollar peaked 2022 (DXY 114), broke down through 2025 (worst year since 2017), hit a 4-yr low 95.5 in Jan-2026, then rallied to 99.97 by Aug-4 as the Fed froze at 3.50-3.75% with three hawkish dissents (Jul-29) against 3.5% CPI. Front end anchored, 30Y 5.27% = highest since Jul-2007 — a term-premium steepener (2s10s +47bp), not a growth signal.

Who already knows. Dollar decline was every bank's 2026 headline call (Morgan Stanley et al. saw deeper H1 losses) — then the +4% YTD countertrend rally punished the crowd: GLD -1.7% YTD and -23.6% off its Feb peak, EMLC negative. The SECULAR downtrend is fully recognized; the idea that 2026 is a pause before downleg 2 is only early-stage.

Why the payoff is shaped this way. Entry into anti-dollar/duration exposure is far better than in January (gold washed out, TLT at 52-wk lows, cuts priced out), but the three-dissent hike tail and 3.5% CPI mean the countertrend rally can extend; asymmetry improves mechanically once headline CPI decays off the Iran-war energy base.

Runway. USD downlegs historically run 6-9y; ~18mo in, so years of runway once the Fed resumes cutting — ends if US twin-deficit/AI-capex exceptionalism forces actual hikes or a global crisis restores the dollar bid.

Best risk/reward in this segment. Europe's fiscal-turn capex cycle — a 26-42% washout in the flagship equities during 2026 against contractually locked, treaty-anchored spending ramping to 2029/2036 is the segment's best price-vs-fundamentals gap for an early entrant, with the ceasefire risk partly self-hedged by the reconstruction/infrastructure half of the same fiscal impulse.
AI-infrastructure overlap — excluded from this scan. Substantial and concentrated in the EM leg: TSMC alone is ~20% of MSCI EM ex-China (+~40% YTD) and Korea's +74.0% YTD is mostly HBM/memory — strip Taiwan+Korea and 2026 'EM outperformance' largely evaporates, so the clean EM cycle counted here is only the unloved China/India/LatAm half. In Europe, Siemens' grid/electrification demand partly IS datacenter power (excluded program territory). Even the US curve regime is contaminated: hyperscaler bond issuance is cited by the sell side as one driver of the record long-end term premium, and the strongest bear case against the dollar downcycle is AI-capex-funded US exceptionalism.

Consumer & retail

4 cyclesbest: China consumer trough 19.0/20 · rank 1

The US consumer is running a K-shaped late-cycle: prime card credit is past peak charge-offs and healing (SYF NCOs 5.43% and falling) while the low end sets records (subprime auto 60+DPD worst in 32 years, student-loan 90+ delinquency 10.3%), with the saving rate at just 2.7% and payrolls stalling at +57k. That stress pushed 2026 flows into a crowded staples/value trade (XLP +9.2% vs XLY -1.0% YTD) even as high-income trade-down fills dollar stores and premium travel books records. China's consumer sits at a genuine deflation-cycle trough (May retail sales first contraction since 2023, consumer-goods PPI still -0.9%) with a strengthening policy put. Travel/experiences is the one clearly re-accelerating — and mostly priced — pocket.

China consumer deflation/stimulus cycle

EARLYpolicy19.0/20
phase 5.0aware 4.0asym 5.0runway 5.0
phase troughclock Policy-driven 2-4y stimulus/deflation oscillations inside a secular property-deleveraging drag; current loop began with the 2024-25 trade-in program, whose 2026 payback is now forcing the next, more structural policy round (15th Five-Year Plan consumption push)asymmetry high

Where on the clock. May-26 retail sales fell 0.6% YoY — the first contraction since 2023 — as the trade-in pull-forward unwound (H1: appliances -7.4%, autos -12.6%); June rebounded to +1.0% only on a fresh ¥62.5B subsidy injection. Q2 GDP +4.3% missed the target band for the first time since 2020. Consumer-level prices are still deflationary where it matters: consumer-goods PPI -0.9% and food CPI -1.6% even as headline CPI holds +1.0%. Policy is escalating from gadget subsidies to structural: July-13 State Council 15th-FYP consumption plan (~¥60T retail by 2030) plus the August consumption-loan interest-subsidy scheme.

Who already knows. Consumer proxies are DOWN in 2026 — KWEB -15.6%, PDD -20.5%, BABA -13.2% YTD — at multi-year-low multiples, and the July stimulus pivot has only just started to bid them (BABA +30%, KWEB +12% over 1M). The demand turn is not yet in the data, so the market has recognized the POLICY, not the CYCLE — that is the early-entrant window, with the honest caveat that 'China stimulus' headlines are widely covered.

Why the payoff is shaped this way. Entry is at trough data + trough multiples (PDD ~9x, BABA ~12-13x forward ex-cash) with a policy put that now explicitly targets consumption for the 2026-30 plan; a single upturn in retail sales or consumer-goods PPI re-rates the complex 30-50% — against which the downside is 'more of the same stagnation' on names already priced for it. ADR/geopolitical discount is the structural haircut.

Runway. Policy loops of this kind take 2-4 quarters from plan (Jul-26) to data inflection; if consumer-goods PPI is not ≥0% by mid-2027 the thesis is wrong and the trough is secular, not cyclical.

US consumer credit cycle (card healing vs subprime/student trough)

RECOGNITIONcredit11.5/20
phase 3.5aware 2.0asym 3.0runway 3.0
phase early-upcycleclock 3-4y charge-off stress waves inside the ~10y household credit cycle; the 2022-25 wave is cresting — healing phases historically run 6-10 quarters absent recessionasymmetry medium

Where on the clock. SYF net charge-offs fell to 5.43% in Q2-26 (-27bps YoY, vs 6.38% a year before in Q1), guidance RAISED; NY Fed Q1-26 card early-delinquency transitions ticked DOWN to 8.6% and card balances fell $25B — while Fitch subprime auto 60+DPD hit a 32-year record 6.90% (Jan-26) and student-loan 90+ delinquency jumped to 10.3% with 2.6M borrowers sent to default. Classic bifurcated inflection: aggregate curve turning while the bottom cohort bottoms later.

Who already knows. The pure credit-torque names have already repriced hard — BFH +49.7% YTD, ECPG +77% YTD / +160% 1Y at an all-time-high share price — so this is not latent; but big balance-sheet lenders (SYF -6.7%, COF -10.2% YTD, ~8-10x earnings) show the healing is not consensus-priced across the complex.

Why the payoff is shaped this way. Remaining upside sits in the un-rerated large lenders (~8x guided EPS) rather than the already-doubled torque names; capped because the labor market (+57k payrolls, 2.7% saving rate, zero excess-savings buffer) could convert 'normalization' into a fresh default wave.

Runway. If labor holds, card NCOs grind lower through 2027 (6-10 quarter healing runway); the subprime auto/student trough resolves ~2-4 quarters behind cards. Cycle ends via recession (bad) or via full normalization + reaccelerating loan growth (good, ~late-2027).

Discretionary-vs-staples rotation + retail inventory cycle

RECOGNITIONpricing10.5/20
phase 2.5aware 2.0asym 3.0runway 3.0
phase mid-cycleclock 1-3y defensive-rotation swings around the rate/real-income cycle; the embedded retail inventory cycle runs 2-4y and is currently LEAN (destocked on tariff fear since Nov-25)asymmetry medium

Where on the clock. 2026 has been a textbook defensive year: XLP +9.2% YTD vs XLY -1.0% (equal-weight RSPD +0.7%), staples among the top S&P sectors with record defensive inflows. But the ingredients of the turn are assembling: UMich sentiment set a RECORD low in May-26 and has risen two straight months to 55.2; retailer inventory/sales ratios have drifted lower since Nov-25 (lean shelves = restock kicker); gasoline is falling from a 4-year high. Sentiment troughs from record lows have historically favored discretionary mean-reversion 6-12m out — the missing permission slip is the labor market.

Who already knows. The crowded, priced side is defensives (record staples inflows, one of XLP's strongest starts in a decade per Feb-26 coverage); the discretionary mean-reversion is the early side — RSPD is flat YTD and +3.3% 1Y, nowhere near priced, and lean inventories are barely discussed outside trade press.

Why the payoff is shaped this way. Equal-weight discretionary at ~16x with lean channel inventories offers a real restock+rotation kicker, but the trade is explicitly conditional on payrolls stabilizing — enter early and a negative print hands you the second down-leg; the staples side offers negative asymmetry from here (crowded at premium multiples).

Runway. Rotation windows historically open 0-2 quarters after a sentiment trough IF employment stabilizes; expect resolution by the Nov-26 holiday order season — either a restock-led discretionary snap or a payroll-led second leg down.

Travel & experiences spend cycle

PRICEDpricing3.0/20
phase 1.0aware 0.0asym 1.0runway 1.0
phase late-cycleclock The post-2021 goods-to-services rotation is ~5 years mature; inside it run a 2-3y airline capacity-discipline mini-cycle (now favorable) and a 7-10y cruise newbuild cycle (supply still tight from the Covid ordering gap)asymmetry low

Where on the clock. Record summer: A4A forecast 271M passengers (+6.3% YoY, all-time high) with single TSA days above 2.5M (+7.7% YoY); Delta posted record June-quarter revenue of $19.8B with FY26 EPS reaffirmed $6.50-7.50; Marriott's US&Canada RevPAR +5.0% was the best in 13 quarters and FY RevPAR guidance was RAISED to 3-3.5%; RCL raised FY26 EPS to $17.73-17.87 (+14%) with 2027 booked ahead at record pricing. This is a demand re-acceleration WITHIN a mature cycle, not a new cycle — and it is concentrated in premium/high-income wallets while the Middle East conflict shaves Europe/international at the margin.

Who already knows. The leaders trade at cycle-high multiples after huge runs: DAL +32.0% YTD / +74% 1Y at ~13x guided EPS vs a 7-9x historical band; RCL +16.2% YTD (+212% 3Y) at ~18x vs 12-15x pre-Covid; MAR ~29.6x guided EPS even after its -7% day. Strong demand is fully in the price; only laggards (BKNG -3.4% YTD, 10.7% off its high) are not.

Why the payoff is shaped this way. Chasing decade-high multiples on the most wealth-effect-dependent, first-to-be-cut spending category late in a labor cycle is negative asymmetry; the residual edge is idiosyncratic (BKNG's Middle East guidance reset, Asia OTA laggards), not cyclical.

Runway. Demand momentum plus booked positions carry through mid-2027, but multiple expansion is exhausted — from here returns require EPS delivery; the cycle ends with the labor market or an oil shock, whichever comes first.

Bear case. Experiences are the first big-ticket cut when the labor market rolls, and it is rolling (+57k payrolls); the whole complex is priced for continuation at decade-high multiples; an oil/conflict escalation hits demand and costs simultaneously; cruise 'record pricing' meets accelerating 2027-28 newbuild deliveries; and the wealth-effect underpinning is hostage to the equity market itself.
Best risk/reward in this segment. China consumer deflation/stimulus — the only sub-cycle where both the data (May retail contraction, consumer-goods PPI -0.9%) and the multiples (PDD ~9x, KWEB -16% YTD) are still at trough while the policy driver just escalated (15th-FYP consumption plan + Aug consumption-loan subsidies), giving an early entrant a defined, checkable turn signal (consumer-goods PPI ≥0%, retail >3% organic) before consensus prices it.
AI-infrastructure overlap — excluded from this scan. Honest exclusions: XLY's cap-weight signal is contaminated by AMZN (~23%) and TSLA (~13%) — AMZN is substantially an AWS/AI-capex story, which is why RSPD equal-weight is used as the clean consumer read. BABA's July bounce (+30% 1M) is at least as much AI/cloud narrative as consumer stimulus — its consumer role here should be haircut accordingly. China's PPI +4.1% reflation is upstream (mining +16.5%) anti-involution and commodity/geopolitics, partly datacenter-adjacent metals demand — it is NOT consumer reflation and is not counted as such (consumer-goods PPI is still -0.9%). Finally, the resilience of high-income US spend (premium cabins, cruise record pricing, luxury stabilization) is partly the AI-equity wealth effect; that macro spillover is acknowledged as a fragility (bear cases) rather than claimed as consumer-cycle demand.

Housing & real estate (US)

4 cyclesbest: Housing turnover thaw 18.0/20 · rank 4

This segment is four desynchronized clocks, not one cycle. Existing-home turnover is in year 4 of a rate-lock freeze at 1995-era volumes (trough, with the 2025 thaw trade violently unwound in 2026: Z -49%, RKT -29%, UWMC -56% YTD); homebuilders are late in a 3-year margin downcycle with the first incentive inflection just printed (LEN Q2); multifamily is exiting a historic supply wave (units under construction at the lowest share of stock since 2013) setting up a 2027-28 rent squeeze; and office is mid-workout — credit losses still peaking at 11.6% CMBS delinquency while leasing fundamentals already inflected and gateway REIT equity has re-rated +18-29% YTD. The alpha is in the clocks that turn without needing a rate collapse: multifamily supply, and selectively the unwound transaction-torque names.

Existing-home turnover / mortgage rate-lock freeze-thaw

EARLYpricing (rate-lock transaction cycle, credit-adjacent)18.0/20
phase 5.0aware 4.0asym 5.0runway 4.0
phase troughclock Rate-driven transaction cycle, ~5-7y to normalize: the freeze began mid-2022; attrition (moves forced by life events, new originations at 6%+) erodes the sub-6% locked stock ~4-6pp/yr, so turnover normalizes via time plus any rate relief. Year 4 of the freeze now.asymmetry high

Where on the clock. June 2026 EHS 4.09M SAAR (+2.8% y/y) after 2025's 4.06M — lowest full year since 1995 and 34% below 2021; Dec-2025 hit a near-3y high 4.35M then re-stalled as PMMS went back to 6.66% (Jul 30). Turnover vs housing stock is at ~40-year lows; inventory 4.6 months is normalizing but from sellers returning, not sales.

Who already knows. The FREEZE is universally known (recognition), but the THAW trade was priced in late 2025 and then violently unwound in 2026: Z -48.5% YTD, RKT -29.3%, UWMC -56.2%, all live-verified from Dec-31-2025 closes. Consensus has capitulated on a 2026 thaw — positioning for the eventual turnover recovery is now contrarian again, which is what 'early' means honestly.

Why the payoff is shaped this way. Transaction-torque names discount ~4.1M sales roughly forever; MBA's own base case is $2.2T originations in 2026 growing thereafter. A move from 4.1M to just 4.8-5.0M EHS (still below the 5.2M 2000s norm) roughly doubles transaction-linked revenue pools. Offset: the torque names are rate-hostages and some (RKT, COMP) carry integration/leverage risk, hence high risk both ways.

Runway. Attrition alone converts ~4-6pp of the locked stock per year, so the freeze thaws mechanically into 2027-28 even at 6.5% rates; a PMMS break below 6% pulls the whole cycle forward by a year. Ends when EHS re-anchors at 5M+; the equity move front-runs that by 6-12 months.

Multifamily supply wave → rent cycle (residential construction)

EARLYnewbuild / capex (permit-to-completion lag cycle)16.3/20
phase 3.3aware 4.0asym 5.0runway 4.0
phase troughclock 5-7y apartment newbuild cycle with a ~2y permit→delivery lag: the 2021-22 permit boom delivered a 50-year-high supply wave in 2024-25; starts collapsed 2023-25, so deliveries mechanically fall through 2027-28 regardless of what happens next — the most deterministic clock in the segment.asymmetry high

Where on the clock. Units under construction ~475k = 3.5% of stock, HALF the early-2023 peak (7.9%) and the lowest since 2013 (Q2 2026); national vacancy 7.2% declining for the first time since late 2021; rents still -1.1% y/y but improving from -1.6% in April with six straight monthly gains (Jul 2026). Deliveries fall ~25% in 2026 and again in 2027 — the squeeze is scheduled, not speculative.

Who already knows. Trade press discusses the supply cliff, but sell-side consensus (Yardi: +0.5% rents 2026, +1% 2027, recovery 'later in the decade') and prices don't reflect it: MAA -3.9% YTD, CPT +0.6%, IRT -4.1%, NXRT -13.9% (all live-verified). Nobody is paid yet for the 2027-28 rent reacceleration; the names still trade on today's weak prints.

Why the payoff is shaped this way. Sunbelt REITs trade ~14-16x FFO vs 20x+ in 2021 (est.) with implied cap rates near private-market levels; the supply input for 2027 is already locked (you cannot un-cancel a start), so the main risk is demand, not supply. Small-caps (IRT, NXRT) offer 2x the rent-growth torque of AVB/ESS. Downside is modest because current pricing already assumes rent stagnation.

Runway. Deliveries fall through late 2027; vacancy normalizes toward ~6% by 2027, rent growth plausibly 3-4% in 2028 vs consensus ~1%. Entry window is now through mid-2027; the cycle ends when the 2028+ permit response delivers in 2030. Risk of being 2-3 quarters early, not wrong.

Homebuilder volume & margin cycle

RECOGNITIONinventory (spec inventory + incentive/margin cycle inside a secular under-supply trend)14.3/20
phase 4.3aware 2.0asym 3.0runway 5.0
phase late-downcycleclock 3-4y incentive/margin cycle riding a 15y+ structural under-build: margins peaked FY2022 (DHI ~31%, est.), have compressed for ~3 years; new-home months supply ~9 is the inventory clock; cycle turns when incentives stop rising and spec supply clears — historically 12-18 months after sentiment troughs.asymmetry medium

Where on the clock. DHI Q3 FY26 (Jul 21) gross margin 20.7% BEAT on lower incentives and guided Q4 20.5-21% = stabilization; LEN Q2 (Jun 11) incentives fell to 12.9% from 14.1% — first decline after three years of increases; but NAHB HMI 34 (Jul) is a cycle low with 63% of builders using incentives and new-home months supply 9.3, so the trough is forming, not confirmed.

Who already knows. Builder cheapness is a consensus talking point; the market is already paying for stabilization selectively (TOL +11.3%, PHM +10.3% YTD live-verified) while punishing volume-strategy names (LEN -17.1%); ITB +0.7% YTD, -4.9% over 1y. Not euphoria — nothing is at highs — but not early either.

Why the payoff is shaped this way. LEN below book (0.96x) is a historic trough multiple, and sector P/Es of 11-14x are on depressed EPS (double discount) — decent upside into any rate relief. But the classic coiled spring is partially sprung: TOL/PHM already re-rated, and a resale thaw (sub-cycle 1 turning) would HURT builders by returning cheap competition, capping the upside case.

Runway. Margin trough likely prints FY2026-early 2027 (DHI guiding flat q/q, LEN incentives falling); the up-leg needs either PMMS <6.25% or 12 more months of spec-inventory clearing. If the resale market thaws hard first, builders lose their monopoly on inventory and the recovery is muted — the two sub-cycles are partially opposed.

CRE office distress workout

RECOGNITIONcredit (distress/workout cycle)10.7/20
phase 1.7aware 2.0asym 3.0runway 4.0
phase mid-cycle workoutclock 6-8y credit workout (1990-96 and 2008-2013 analogs): peak-to-trough values -40%+ from 2022, delinquency peaks YEARS after fundamentals trough because maturities force recognition; we are in year 4 since the 2022 rate shock, with the 2026-27 refi wall forcing final loss crystallization while operating fundamentals already recover.asymmetry medium

Where on the clock. Two clocks inside one: office CMBS delinquency 11.57% (June 2026) is at/near the all-time record ~11.7% (early 2026) — the CREDIT side is still peaking; but vacancy fell 30bp to 18.3% in Q2-2026 (largest drop since 2015), leasing is on a record-year pace, the construction pipeline is -87% from 2020, and SLG raised 2026 FFO guidance by $1.20 to $5.60-5.90 — the OPERATING side has clearly turned. Classic mid-workout: equity leads, debt losses lag.

Who already knows. Gateway office equity has re-rated hard and publicly: SLG +18.1%, VNO +20.0%, HIW +29.0%, CUZ +21.8% YTD (live-verified); 'record Manhattan leasing' is a mainstream headline. Not priced: SLG $54 vs ~$100 pre-COVID and B/C office still uninvestable — but the easy 'not dead' re-rate is largely done in the prime names.

Why the payoff is shaped this way. The prime-gateway leg is 60-70% recognized; residual asymmetry sits in laggards (BXP +4.3% at ~10x FFO est.), sunbelt catch-ups, and mispriced special situations (ESRT punished for tourism, not office). Downside re-opens if the 2026-27 refi wall breaks something in regional banks. Risk/reward is decent but no longer the fat early-2025 pitch.

Runway. Years 3-4 of a 6-8y workout. Delinquency likely peaks 2026-27 as the refi wall clears; prime equity recovery continues but decelerates; B/C office liquidates through 2028-30 (conversion/demolition). The laggard/special-situation leg (BXP, ESRT) is where the remaining 2-3 years of alpha lives.

AI-infrastructure overlap — excluded from this scan. Honest exclusions and contaminations: (1) datacenter REITs (EQIX/DLR) and DC construction are fully excluded per the MECE boundary; (2) part of the 'CRE recovery' in brokers/service firms (CBRE, JLL, NMRK) is AI-DC leasing and project management in disguise — treat their transaction-recovery torque as partially AI-infra beta (est. 10-20% of earnings momentum); (3) ESS's +9.2% YTD West Coast apartment strength is partly an AI-employment trade (SF/Seattle hiring), not pure supply-cycle; (4) resi construction COSTS are inflated by AI-infra competition for electricians, HVAC labor, transformers and switchgear — a margin headwind for builders counted here, a demand signal counted in the AI-infra program; (5) sunbelt housing demand in NoVa/Columbus/Phoenix has an AI-DC employment tailwind that flatters local turnover and rent data.

Autos & mobility

4 cyclesbest: China EV consolidation 16.8/20 · rank 7

Global autos in Aug-2026 is four desynchronized clocks, not one cycle. The US new-vehicle machine is mid-cycle and healthier than feared (July SAAR ~16.6-16.9M, best pace of 2026; 49 days supply; GM raising guidance as tariff costs shrink), so the 2025 tariff-shock trade has largely played out. The EV S-curve has trifurcated: US in a post-tax-credit air pocket (~6% share vs 7.5% peak), EU steepening (20.7% BEV share), China saturating (63% NEV penetration with retail now FALLING). Meanwhile China's price war is being forcibly ended by Beijing's anti-involution campaign at trough profitability while a record export wave (+65% H1) builds, and US auto credit sits at 32-year-record subprime stress on top of a structural used-vehicle scarcity squeeze born of the 2022-24 SAAR trough.

China EV price war endgame + export wave

EARLYpricing16.8/20
phase 4.8aware 4.0asym 5.0runway 3.0
phase troughclock Pricing/capacity-consolidation cycle: ~3y of price war (2023-26) being force-ended by state anti-involution policy; consolidation phases historically run 2-4y; export wave is a parallel multi-year buildasymmetry high

Where on the clock. Profit trough markers are textbook: BYD Q1-26 net -55.4% to lowest in 3+ years with third straight revenue decline (2026-04-28); domestic NEV retail down six straight months. Simultaneously the state made anti-involution the 2026 core governance agenda with price-floor regulation (Caixin, 2026-01), and the escape valve is exploding: H1-26 exports 5.10M (+65.3%), NEV exports 2.36M (+120%), June the first-ever 1M+ export month. Trough + forced discipline + volume escape = the classic set-up for a consolidation upcycle.

Who already knows. The market has started paying for winners with export leverage (Geely +40% YTD, +65% 1Y) but the core names still price the war as permanent: BYDDY flat YTD (-17% 1Y), XPEV -39% YTD, LI -23% YTD. When the leader's margin inflection is not yet in its price while the No.2's re-rate has begun, awareness is early, not latent — weekly CnEVPost coverage exists but Western positioning is thin.

Why the payoff is shaped this way. If anti-involution holds, BYD/Geely earn trough-to-normal margin recovery ON TOP of +65% export volume growth — a double lever the flat BYDDY price ignores. Risk is real (tariff walls, policy failure) but you are paid at trough multiples-on-trough-earnings, the opposite of the US names. Highest remaining asymmetry in the segment.

Runway. Domestic profit trough likely passes H2-26/H1-27 if price discipline holds; the export wave has multi-year runway but its GROWTH RATE peaks when tariff walls (EU definitive duties, India/Brazil localization mandates) bite — watch 2027. Consolidation upcycles historically pay for 2-3 years.

EV adoption S-curve — regionally trifurcated (US air-pocket vs EU steepening vs China saturation)

RECOGNITIONadoption-scurve10.5/20
phase 2.5aware 2.0asym 1.0runway 5.0
phase mid-cycleclock Decade-plus S-curve interrupted by 3-5y policy sub-cycles; the US is in year 1 of a post-subsidy air pocket (credit died 2025-09-30), EU is mid-slope on 2025 CO2 compliance, China is at the flattening topasymmetry low

Where on the clock. US: EV share collapsed from 7.5% peak (Q3-25) to 5.8% (Q1-26) then stabilized at ~6% with Q2 units +14.2% QoQ — an air pocket, not a death (Cox, 2026-07). EU: BEV share 20.7% H1-26 vs 15.6% a year ago, Germany +48% — the steep middle of the curve (ACEA, 2026-07-27). China: NEV penetration 62.8% in June with retail DOWN 7% YoY for a sixth straight month — top of the S-curve plus subsidy payback (CPCA via CnEVPost, 2026-07-08).

Who already knows. The US stall is front-page consensus (TSLA -28% YTD tells you it is priced into EV pure-plays); the EU steepening and the hybrid-winner trade are better recognized than a year ago (BWA +40% YTD, +65% 1Y — no longer latent). What remains under-appreciated is the used-EV boom (+12% Q1, near-record) and that the US installed base keeps compounding through the stall.

Why the payoff is shaped this way. Both sides of the trade are crowded: shorting the US stall is late (already -20%+ YTD across pure-plays), and the hybrid-content winners have run. Regional divergence is visible in monthly public data, so little informational edge remains; the asymmetric residue sits in second-derivative plays (charging utilization, used-EV ecosystem) not in the OEMs.

Runway. US air pocket likely lasts through 2027 absent new federal policy; EU steepening has 2-3 years of CO2-target runway; China penetration growth is over — its cycle hands off to replacement/export. The secular S-curve is intact; only the US policy sub-cycle broke.

US new-vehicle SAAR / legacy-OEM profit cycle (tariff-shock absorption)

RECOGNITIONinventory8.5/20
phase 2.5aware 2.0asym 3.0runway 1.0
phase mid-cycleclock 3-4y inventory/SAAR cycle layered on a 1-2y tariff shock-and-absorption sub-cycle; pull-forward payback from spring-2025 tariff buying is now completeasymmetry medium

Where on the clock. July 2026 SAAR projected 16.6-16.9M, the strongest pace of the year (JD Power 16.9M, Omdia 16.6M, as-of 2026-07-27); June SAAR 16.5M with volume +7.6% YoY; days supply only 49 vs ~60 norm; OEM profits re-inflecting as tariff costs come in BELOW guidance (GM cut FY tariff cost to $2.5-3.5B and raised EBIT guide to $14-16B on 2026-07-21). This is textbook mid-cycle: volumes at trend, inventory lean, margins recovering, but affordability ($49.8k ATP) caps upside.

Who already knows. GM +66% over 1Y and PAG at all-time highs (16x TTM P/E vs its 8-12x dealer history) show the tariff-resilience trade is well recognized; yet GM still trades ~6-7x the midpoint of its own RAISED $12-14 EPS guide, so recognition has not reached euphoria for OEMs — dealers are closer to priced than manufacturers.

Why the payoff is shaped this way. OEM multiples (GM 6-7x fwd, F 0.31x sales/4.2% yield) leave room IF SAAR holds, but the cyclical clock is past the sweet spot: the easy move (tariff fear unwind) already happened in H2-25/H1-26. Remaining upside is multiple-normalization, not estimate revision. Distressed STLA is the one true early-phase turnaround left in the group.

Runway. 12-18 months of mid-cycle left if employment holds; the cycle ends via affordability (ATP ~$50k + ~7.5-8% loan rates) once pent-up replacement demand from 2020-22 under-selling is exhausted, or via tariff re-escalation. Dealer GPU normalization is already underway — dealers roll before OEMs.

US auto credit stress + used-vehicle scarcity counter-cycle

RECOGNITIONcredit8.0/20
phase 1.5aware 2.0asym 3.0runway 1.5
phase peakclock Consumer auto credit cycle (5-7y, stress cresting after the 2021-22 loose-underwriting vintage seasoned) intersecting a supply-driven used-price cycle (3-4y lag of the 2022-24 new-SAAR trough = 2025-27 scarcity of 2-4yr-old cars)asymmetry medium

Where on the clock. Credit: Fitch subprime 60+ DPD hit 6.90% in Jan-26 — worst since 1994 — then eased to 6.80% Feb; NY Fed Q1-26 shows auto balances $1.69T with 5.6% of debt 90+ delinquent but transition rates 'holding steady' — stress is peaking, not accelerating, and prime is pristine (0.42%). Used prices: Manheim 212.9 in June (+2.1% YoY), mid-July 211.5 (+2% YoY) — firm against normal seasonality because the 2022-24 SAAR trough hollowed out late-model used supply. Peak-stress + scarce collateral = the counter-cyclical sweet spot beginning.

Who already knows. The used-scarcity half is recognized: KMX +51% YTD and CACC +29% YTD are the tape saying so. The credit-normalization half is NOT yet paid: ALLY still -2.7% YTD at 0.88x book despite record-stress headlines already printed — the market prices stress as permanent, which is where the remaining edge lives. '32-year record delinquency' headlines mark sentiment trough, not entry risk, when collateral values are RISING.

Why the payoff is shaped this way. Lenders at ≤1x book with rising recovery values (Manheim +2% YoY) offer classic peak-stress asymmetry, and rate cuts feed straight into auto-lender NIM. But it is only medium: the trade is visible, KMX/CACC already moved, and a labor-market crack converts 'plateau' into a second stress leg — the entire thesis rests on employment.

Runway. Stress plateau resolves within 12-18 months either into normalization (Fed cuts + tight used supply protecting recoveries) or a labor-led second leg. The used-scarcity squeeze mechanically persists into 2027 because 2022-24 new-vehicle under-production cannot be retroactively fixed.

Bear case. Unemployment is the whole ballgame: a labor crack spreads stress from subprime to near-prime, ALLY's book discount widens, CACC forecasts cut collections, and used prices give back gains as repos add supply. Philly Fed cautions delinquency metrics may partially overstate distress — meaning the 'record stress = record opportunity' framing can also be a value trap if the plateau is actually the new normal.
Best risk/reward in this segment. China price-war endgame/export wave — BYD priced at trough margins with a flat YTD move while state-enforced price discipline, six-months-falling domestic retail forcing rationalization, and +120% NEV export growth set up a trough-to-normal margin recovery the ADR does not yet price; tariff walls are the risk you are being paid for.
AI-infrastructure overlap — excluded from this scan. The demand side of this segment is genuinely non-AI (SAAR, credit, used prices, China consolidation), so direct AI-infra overlap is small — well under 5% of segment revenue. The contamination is in VALUATIONS and narratives, not volumes: (1) TSLA at 273x TTM earnings is mostly a robotaxi/Optimus AI option — its -28% YTD says more about AI-narrative repricing than about the EV S-curve, so treat TSLA as AI-beta and size it under the AI program's rules, not this segment's; (2) XPEV/NIO increasingly market themselves on autonomy/AI-chip stories — same caveat at smaller scale; (3) auto-semi content growth (ADAS SoCs, lidar) is an AI/semis-program exposure deliberately excluded from the plays here; (4) EV-charging grid demand overlaps with the datacenter-power research only at the utility layer, which is out of scope for both the plays and evidence used in this scan.

Conventional energy

4 cyclesbest: US natgas / LNG wave 18.8/20 · rank 2

2026 conventional energy is dominated by one exogenous shock and its unwind: the late-Feb US/Israel-Iran war closed the Strait of Hormuz (largest supply disruption ever, ~10 mb/d off in March, Brent ~$120 in April), then a US-Iran deal reopened the strait in late June and prices settled to ~$82-86. That one event left the segment's four clocks at four very different phases: oil is late-cycle with the war premium still bleeding out into a forecast 2027 surplus; refining is at a euphoric war-driven margin PEAK (record cracks, stocks at all-time highs); offshore/services is a mid-cycle consolidation stall (high day rates, RIG-VAL merger, but FIDs deferred); and US natural gas is the one sub-market the war did NOT durably reprice — HH is back to $2.76 with gas equities flat YTD while a contractually visible LNG demand step (17→21+ Bcf/d by 2027-28) arrives. The cheap asymmetry is where the shock left no premium behind.

US natural gas demand wave & LNG export buildout

EARLYcapex/newbuild demand step (LNG trains) driving a 2-3y storage-led gas price cycle18.8/20

Where on the clock. HH front-month is $2.76 (2026-08-04) with storage +185 Bcf above the 5-yr avg — trough pricing — even though the demand step is contractually arriving: Golden Pass (9th US terminal) shipped first cargo early 2026, Train 2 due H2-26, Train 3 H1-27; Plaquemines (3.8 Bcf/d design) still ramping; US export capacity goes 17 Bcf/d (end-2025) to >19 Bcf/d in 2026 and keeps climbing into 2027-28. EIA's own curve turns up: $3.57 avg Q4-26. Storage y/y is already -32 Bcf. The war even ran the strategic-value experiment for you: losing Qatar's ~10 Bcf/d (20% of global LNG) for four months spiked European gas 63% in a week and JKM to $23+.

Who already knows. Honest split: infrastructure is already recognized (LNG +32.8%, VG +82.3% YTD — the war repriced liquefaction owners), but the UPSTREAM molecule is not: EQT -0.1%, EXE -14.2%, AR +5.9%, RRC +14.5% YTD (live 2026-08-04), all 20-25% below their 52-wk highs. The gas E&P leg of the wave is where awareness is still early — flat equities against a visible 2027 demand step.

Why the payoff is shaped this way. Best remaining risk/reward in the segment: entry at trough pricing ($2.76 HH, storage surplus) ahead of a dated, contracted demand increase of ~2-4 Bcf/d over 18 months, with the supply governor visible (127 gas rigs, associated-gas growth already in EIA's numbers). Downside is 'gas stays $3' — cheap to wait; upside is the 2027 storage-deficit repricing toward $4.50+. Position BEFORE storage flips to deficit, which is the recognition trigger.

Runway. Demand step runs through 2028 (Golden Pass T3, Corpus Christi Stage 3, next-wave FIDs); the price upcycle likely peaks 2027-28, and ends when the >160-rig supply response plus next-decade LNG oversupply catches up.

Where on the clock. The definitive mid-cycle tell is consolidation: Transocean-Valaris all-stock merger announced 2026-02-09 ($5.8B, 73 rigs, ~$10B combined backlog, DOJ second request May-2026, close H2-26). Meanwhile war volatility deferred FIDs and the group round-tripped: OIH -13.8% and RIG -25.1% over 3M as crude fell from $119 — classic mid-cycle white-space anxiety, not a top (no newbuilds ordered) and not a trough (rates 4-5x the 2017-20 lows).

Who already knows. RIG +24.7% YTD (+83.3% 1Y), VAL +52.4%, NE +43.1%, TDW +41.5%, SLB +28.5%, OIH +34.2% (live 2026-08-04) — the 1Y moves say the story is known. But it is recognition off a distressed base, not euphoria: RIG's $5.2B market cap still roughly equals just its Feb backlog, and everything is 20-33% below its war-spike 52-wk high.

Why the payoff is shaped this way. Two-sided: if Brent holds >$70, the 2027-28 contracting window (post-merger duopoly discipline, shrinking supply-side fleet) reprices backlogs higher and the equities have 30-50% recovery room to their own 52-wk highs. But EIA's $65 2027 Brent is exactly the level at which deepwater FIDs slip again — the cycle extends in TIME without paying you. Better skew than oil beta, worse than gas.

Runway. Newbuild cycle has years left (no ordering, 4-5y shipyard lead times), but the next leg needs a 2027 FID wave; watch the Brent 24-mo forward — the cycle stalls, not dies, at $65-70.

Oil price cycle / OPEC+ spare capacity & shale capex discipline

Meanwhile the structural side stays tight: EIA sees US crude output DECLINING ~100 kb/d to 13.5 mb/d in 2026 (first drop in 5 years) and oil rigs sit at just 451 despite the $120 spike — discipline held.

Who already knows. XOM +28.8%, OXY +34.9%, FANG +32.2%, XOP +38.0% YTD (live-verified 2026-08-04) — the war repriced the whole complex and it is well-owned; XOM sits ~23% above its pre-war all-time high. Why the payoff is shaped this way. The bullish structural story (US shale plateau, 5+ years of underinvestment) is real but is a late-decade payoff, not a 2026-27 one — you are paid poorly to hold the recognition-stage beta through the surplus.

Runway. War premium likely fully unwinds within 2-4 quarters absent re-escalation; 2027 shapes up as surplus/downcycle; the NEXT true upcycle comes late-decade when redeployed OPEC+ spare thins out against a shrinking US shale base — that is the entry to wait for, not chase now.
