Refining margin super-spike

EUPHORIApricing/margin cycle (capacity rationalization + war-outage overlay)3.0/20
phase 0.0aware 0.5asym 1.0runway 1.5
phase peakclock Margin cycles off capacity closures run 2-4y; the war-outage component (~6 mb/d of refinery runs offline) mean-reverts in quarters as plants restartasymmetry low

Where on the clock. US 3-2-1 crack hit a RECORD ~$69.66/bbl in July (normal $10-20) and diesel cracks >$91/bbl; IEA confirms cracks/margins at multi-year highs in early July precisely because crude FELL while products stayed tight. The tightness is war-made and structural at once: global refinery runs were -6 mb/d y/y in June (Middle East export refineries still down, Russian throughput curtailed by drone strikes) on top of permanent US closures (LyondellBasell Houston 264 kb/d in 2025; Phillips 66 LA ~139 kb/d end-2025; Valero Benicia ~145 kb/d Apr-2026 — California lost ~18% of state capacity). Record margins on temporarily destroyed supply = textbook peak.

Who already knows. VLO +88.9%, MPC +88.8%, DINO +92.4%, PSX +59.8%, PARR +99.0% YTD (live 2026-08-04), essentially all at all-time highs (VLO $307 vs $130.78 52-wk low); mainstream press running record-crack and $12-California-gasoline headlines; Forbes covering crack spreads. This is as recognized as a cycle gets.

Why the payoff is shaped this way. Negatively skewed: you are being offered peak multiples on peak margins. When Middle East export refineries and Russian units restart over the next 2-4 quarters, cracks can halve and still be historically strong; the equities would derate faster. The only durable residual is the higher mid-cycle floor from permanent US/EU closures — worth owning at the NEXT trough, not this peak.

Runway. War-damaged capacity restarts drive normalization over ~2-4 quarters; structurally higher floor persists (US/California closures are permanent), so the next normalized trough should bottom above pre-2026 troughs — the re-entry point.

AI-infrastructure overlap — excluded from this scan. Refining, oil, and offshore have negligible AI-infra content (datacenter diesel gensets and construction fuel are rounding errors).

Nuclear & uranium

4 cyclesbest: Uranium utility contracting 17.5/20 · rank 5

This segment is two markets wearing one narrative. The PHYSICAL fuel cycle keeps tightening: spot U3O8 $86.6/lb (+6% YTD), the long-term price at a record-nominal $95.50/lb (+10% YTD), conversion near all-time highs, Kazatomprom holding a 10% cut to 2026 output, and a 13th straight year of below-replacement utility contracting that mathematically must reverse before 2028-30 delivery windows. The EQUITY/narrative layer peaked in Oct-2025 euphoria and has de-rated all of 2026 (URA -4.5% YTD but -34% off highs; pre-revenue SMR names -40% to -84% off highs) as investors demanded proof over AI hype. The interesting setup in Aug-2026 is precisely this divergence: fuel-cycle fundamentals strengthening while sentiment washed out — with physical uranium buyable at a 13% discount to NAV via SPUT.

Utility long-term contracting (procurement) cycle

EARLYpricing/contracting cycle17.5/20
phase 4.5aware 4.0asym 5.0runway 4.0
phase early-upcycleclock ~10y: utilities contract in generational waves (last one 2005-2012); replacement rate ~150M lb/yr; 13 consecutive years (2013-2025) contracted BELOW replacement — the deferred-demand spring is the clockasymmetry high

Where on the clock. Inflection is dated and visible: 2025 total term contracting ~116M lb of which ~72M lb came in Q4-25 alone; Cameco's Q2-26 (Jul-31) reports LT price strengthening on 'increased on- and off-market contracting' in H1-26; the LT price printed a record $95.50 while spot sits $9 lower — term demand is leading spot for the first time this cycle. Still below the 150M lb replacement rate, so the wave is starting, not mature.

Who already knows. This is the least-followed data series in the segment — retail tracks spot price, not term volume, and 2026's equity de-rate happened DESPITE the contracting inflection. Physical (SRUUF -3.2% YTD at a 13% NAV discount, YCA -5.7% YTD at a discount) is priced as if the term cycle weren't turning. Genuinely early on the awareness curve even though the uranium story broadly is recognized.

Why the payoff is shaped this way. 13 years of deferral is mathematically finite: EIA shows US operators' maximum contracted deliveries 2026-2035 total only 174M lb vs ~45-50M lb/yr of requirements — the uncovered wedge post-2028 is enormous and can only be closed by contracting at prices that incentivize new supply. Expression via physical at a 13% NAV discount means you're paid to wait; the trigger (replacement-rate contracting) is observable quarters before spot reacts.

Runway. Contracting waves run 5-7 years once started (2005-2012 precedent); if 2026 confirms >130M lb, this wave has ~5y of runway and ends when utilities are re-covered into the mid-2030s

Western enrichment & conversion rebuild

EARLYcapex (policy-forced), 2028 hard deadline15.5/20
phase 4.5aware 4.0asym 3.0runway 4.0
phase early-upcycleclock 7-10y capex cycle: centrifuge/conversion plants take 5-8y to permit+build; clock started with the Aug-2024 Russian LEU import ban and runs to the Jan-1-2028 waiver termination, capacity landing 2027-2031asymmetry medium

Where on the clock. Prices at records (conversion spot $64-66/kgU, ~$52 LT; SWU spot ~$190 at last public print) but the CAPEX is only now being committed: Centrus signed its $900M DOE HALEU/LEU expansion contract Jun-30-26 and named Fluor EPC Feb-26; Solstice is de-bottlenecking Metropolis +20% to >10,000 tU UF6 in 2026 and studying a sister plant; Urenco US expansion continuing. Revenue/capacity harvest is 2027-2031 — textbook early capex upcycle with a statutory demand cliff (waivers end Jan-1-2028).

Who already knows. The 2028 Russian cliff is the most telegraphed event in the fuel cycle — LEU ran +337% over 3y into Oct-25 euphoria ($464 high). But 2026 knocked LEU -24% YTD (-60% from high) and SILXY -40% YTD, so the euphoria premium is out while the physical bottleneck prices (conversion/SWU) sit at records. Recognition, no longer euphoric.

Why the payoff is shaped this way. Upside: only 464,183 kg of Russian LEU waivers allowed for all of CY2026 and zero after 2027 — any western capacity slip in 2027-28 forces panic SWU/EUP buying, and LEU/SILXY just de-rated 40-60%. Downside: the cliff is fully known, Urenco/Orano capacity lands 2027-28, and a Russia re-entry on any settlement would crack record SWU pricing overnight; LEU itself still depends on TENEX deliveries pre-2028.

Runway. Bottleneck pricing likely holds through 2028-29; the cycle ends 2030-32 when new western SWU/conversion capacity overshoots — classic capex-cycle overbuild risk on the far side

Uranium mine-supply deficit cycle

RECOGNITIONcommodity-supply11.5/20
phase 2.5aware 2.0asym 3.0runway 4.0
phase mid-cycleclock 10-15y commodity-supply cycle: last trough 2016-20 ($18/lb), price discovery 2021-24 (spot peaked $107 Jan-24), supply response now underway; greenfield lead times 7-10y (Arrow ~2030) set the clockasymmetry medium

Where on the clock. Price discovery done; spot has consolidated $78-90 for ~18 months while the LT price grinds to record nominal highs ($95.50 Jun-30-26 vs $86.50 end-25). Supply response is real but disciplined and slipping: Kazatomprom cut 2026 nominal plan ~10% to 29,697 tU (Aug-25), Cameco held 19.5-21.5M lb guidance through Q2-26 road-condition disruptions, restart ramps (Boss/Paladin/enCore) have disappointed. Classic mid-cycle: incentive price confirmed, new supply not yet arriving.

Who already knows. Not latent — CCJ +167% over 3y and the story is on every podcast. But not euphoria either: CCJ is -1.9% YTD 2026 and -34% below its Oct-25 all-time high, UEC -14.6% YTD, URA -4.5% YTD, and SPUT trades at a -12.9% NAV discount (Jul-31-26) — the marginal financial buyer left during the 2026 de-rate even as LT price set records.

Why the payoff is shaped this way. The easy 5-10x from the 2020 trough is gone; remaining upside is the gap between record LT prices/records-tight physical and equities that just de-rated 25-35%, plus the SPUT discount as a coiled spring. Downside cushioned by producer discipline; capped by 13% NAV-discount overhang and Kazatomprom's ability to revert to 100% subsoil levels (+~8M lb/yr).

Runway. Deficit persists to ~2029-31 (Arrow/expansion pounds arrive then); cycle ends when LT>$100 pulls the ~60M lb/yr of latent Kazakh+greenfield supply into FID — likely 2-3y more of tight physical before the supply wave is visible

SMR / advanced-reactor adoption S-curve — funding sub-cycle

RECOGNITIONadoption-scurve with a 3-4y funding winter/thaw overlay8.0/20
phase 2.0aware 2.0asym 1.0runway 3.0
phase downcycleclock 15-20y S-curve (FOAK builds 2025-2030, scale 2030s); the tradeable clock is the 3-4y funding cycle: thaw 2023 -> euphoria 2025 -> winter/digestion 2026asymmetry low

Where on the clock. The funding sub-cycle rolled over in H1-26: OKLO -42.6% YTD (-73% from $193.84 high), NuScale -36.3% YTD (-84% from high), press narrative flipped to 'investors demand real results over AI hype' (Jul-26). Meanwhile the SECULAR S-curve advanced: Darlington BWRX-300 under construction (first N.American SMR, May-25), TVA Clinch River CP decision due ~end-26, Oklo broke ground at INL Sep-25 (Kiewit, COD target late-27/early-28), Centrus's 12 tU/yr HALEU cascade contracted Jun-26. Even the simple Palisades RESTART slipped from late-25 into 2026 — proof that milestones, not narratives, now set prices.

Who already knows. Euphoria demonstrably broke: names 73-84% off highs, YTD -36% to -43%. But this is NOT latent — OKLO still carries a $7.2B market cap on zero revenue and every hyperscaler deal is front-page news. The market recognizes the S-curve; it is repricing the timeline.

Why the payoff is shaped this way. Even post-crash, valuations embed flawless FOAK execution (OKLO $7.2B pre-revenue; NuScale $2.7B with no firm US order). The winter can deepen — dilution, milestone slips, NRC timelines — before first power in 2027-28 proves anything. Asymmetry improves only at genuinely distressed prices or on a verified first criticality; today it is a milestone-timing trade, not a cycle entry.

Runway. Funding winter likely persists until a hard proof-point (Darlington completion ~2029 or Oklo INL criticality 2027-28); the secular S-curve then has 15+ years — but the equity cycle will thaw on proof, not on time

NuScale's -84% shows how far 'recognition' can still fall.
Best risk/reward in this segment. Utility long-term contracting cycle — 13 years of below-replacement procurement is a finite spring, the Q4-25 (72M lb) and H1-26 inflection is verifiable in free data quarters before spot reacts, and it is expressible through physical uranium at a 13% discount to NAV, giving the rare combination of an early awareness stage, a dated trigger, and a valuation cushion.
AI-infrastructure overlap — excluded from this scan. Honest split: the physical fuel-cycle deficit stands on its own — it is driven by ~440 operating reactors, China's ~30 units under construction, and life extensions, none of which is AI demand. AI-datacenter linkage enters three ways. (1) Physical demand: hyperscaler-tied restarts (Crane-Microsoft 835MW, Duane Arnold-Google ~615MW) add only ~0.7-0.8M lb/yr U3O8 at full run — under 0.5% of ~180M lb global demand; AI-driven new-build/SMR fuel demand before ~2032 is negligible (est. AI adds perhaps 5-10% to cumulative 2035 uranium demand). (2) Equity beta: this is the BIG overlap — the entire H1-26 nuclear drawdown (OKLO -43%, NuScale -36%, even UEC/UUUU) was the AI-proof reckoning, not fuel fundamentals; SMR sub-cycle valuations remain ~70-90% AI-narrative-driven and that slice belongs to the AI-infra program, excluded here. (3) Policy tailwind (DOE loans, quadrupling goal) is partly AI-motivated but benefits the standalone fuel cycle regardless. Sub-cycles 1-3 above are counted as standalone fuel-cycle stories; sub-cycle 4's demand narrative is largely AI-infra in disguise and is flagged accordingly.

Metals & mining

4 cyclesbest: Copper deficit 14.5/20 · rank 13

2026 is a year of violent divergence inside metals. Copper is in a genuine supply-deficit upcycle — 2026 treatment charges settled at literally $0, Grasberg's force majeure removed ~591kt, and COMEX printed an all-time high $6.71/lb in May — with equities lagging the metal. Precious metals are in a post-euphoria hangover: gold topped $5,500 and silver $121.62 in January, then crashed (worst gold quarter since 2013) as 10Y real yields hit 2.47% and the Fed leans toward hiking, even while central banks bought a record 289t in Q2. Rare earths are mid-washout in a policy oscillation cycle (China's June counter-controls on MP/USAR; truce expires Nov 2026), and China steel/aluminium is in an early supply-discipline upcycle (crude steel −3.0% H1, aluminium at 4-year highs near the ~45Mt cap) that consensus still reads as a US-tariff story.

China steel/aluminium supply-discipline (anti-involution) cycle

RECOGNITIONcapex/policy (China supply-side reform 2.0)13.5/20
phase 4.5aware 2.0asym 3.0runway 4.0
phase early-upcycleclock 5-10y capacity cycle; reform 1.0 (2016-18) delivered 2-3 years of margin expansion; reform 2.0 escalated from warnings (2024) to 'deep rectification' (2026); aluminium has a hard ~45Mt capacity cap now effectively bindingasymmetry medium

Where on the clock. The supply side is finally moving: H1 2026 crude steel 499.95Mt (-3.0% y/y) with an outright ban on new steel capacity; steel exports -5.6%; aluminium at 4-year highs ($3,325 Jan 29 peak, fresh 4-yr peak May 26 on China cut concerns, live $3,454 = +16.2% YTD) with output pinned near the cap; record US Midwest premium $2,182/t; iron ore back above $100 in August — discipline is tightening supply faster than property-dragged demand is falling.

Who already knows. For the China-discipline leg, genuinely early — Western coverage is fixated on US tariffs, and the aluminium equities badly lag the metal (AA -15.6% YTD vs LME +16.2%). The US-mill tariff leg is the opposite: NUE +68.4% YTD at 20.8x TTM P/E vs ~10-12x its own mid-cycle history — that part is priced.

Why the payoff is shaped this way. The metal-vs-equity divergence in aluminium (AA at 9.1x TTM P/E and 0.87x sales while LME sits near 4-year highs) is a real mispricing if China's cap holds — but the whole leg rests on Beijing's enforcement follow-through, which reversed in 2021, and on demand not rolling over.

Gold/silver monetary cycle & the miners-vs-bullion gap

RECOGNITIONpolicy (real-rate cycle layered on secular central-bank reserve shift)8.0/20
phase 2.0aware 2.0asym 1.0runway 3.0
phase downcycleclock 8-12y monetary cycle with 2-4y bull legs and 6-18mo corrections (2008: -30%; 2011-13 analog); current leg began 2024, blow-off top Jan 2026 — the correction is ~6 months oldasymmetry low

Where on the clock. Gold printed >$5,500/oz intraday in Jan 2026 then fell below $4,000 late June — worst quarter since 2013 (-16% in Q2) — and sits at $4,346 live (roughly flat YTD, +0.1%); silver's Jan 29 record $121.62 collapsed 52% to $57.79 by Jul 30 after CME margin hikes forced a ~30% single-day liquidation; the cyclical macro is hostile (10Y TIPS 2.47%, futures pricing Fed funds ~4% by year-end) while the structural bid strengthened — record 289t of central-bank buying in Q2, INTO the dip.

Who already knows. January was textbook euphoria (retail silver squeeze, ATH headlines); the 25-52% crash, Q2 gold-ETF outflows and -35% GDX drawdown have reset positioning back to recognition — no longer crowded, nowhere near latent. The genuinely under-appreciated piece is miners printing record FCF at single-digit-to-low-teens multiples.

Why the payoff is shaped this way. The gap trade has margin of safety (NEM 7.9x P/FCF with record $2.2B quarterly FCF; KGC 8.9x P/E) IF gold holds ~$4,000, and silver carries a 6th consecutive annual deficit (cumulative ~762Moz stock draw since 2021, est.) — but a live Fed hiking bias with 10Y real yields at 2.47% caps upside and the WGC's own fair-value model sits at ~$4,100, so this is timing risk, not information edge.

Bear case. WGC's own base-case model (one Fed hike, inflation peaking ~3.9%) puts fair value at ~$4,100 ±5% — i.e., gold is at fair value, not cheap. Best risk/reward in this segment. Copper supply-deficit — the only sub-cycle where verified physics (TCs at $0, ~591kt Grasberg loss, 3-decade-low ex-US inventories, no new supply before 2028) guarantees years of runway while the equities (COPX +10.5% YTD, TECK 6.9x EV/EBITDA, washed-out IVN/ERO) still lag the metal and a dated Jan-2027 refined-tariff catalyst sits on the calendar; awareness is 'recognition', but duration plus laggard selection beats the crowded monetary trades and the policy coin-flips.
AI-infrastructure overlap — excluded from this scan. The AI-shaped slice here is copper and grid metal: datacenter + AI-driven grid buildout adds roughly 400-600kt/yr of incremental copper demand, about 1.5-2% of the ~28Mt refined market (est.) — material to the deficit narrative's margins but NOT this scan's thesis, which rests on supply (TCs, Grasberg, pipeline). Aluminium HV cable/busbar and datacenter-structural steel are low-single-digit % of their markets; silver's industrial driver is solar (thrifting silver use -19% in 2026), essentially not AI; rare-earth NdFeB into robotics/drones is the fastest AI-adjacent slice but EV/wind/defense dominate volumes. Any grid/power-equipment equity angle (transformers, HV cable makers) was deliberately excluded as AI-infra territory.

Agriculture & food

4 cyclesbest: Crop-protection destock 18.0/20 · rank 3

US agriculture is exiting its worst income downturn since the 1980s: 2024-25 was the trough, and in 2026 the commodity leg is turning first — a genuine wheat supply shock (smallest US crop since 1970/71, KC wheat +40% YTD), a China soybean-purchase deal (>=25 MMT/yr through 2028), and corn demand strong enough that USDA keeps cutting ending stocks — while corn still trades at/below cost of production and ~30% of 2026 farm income is government checks. Downstream, the equipment and crop-protection destock cycles are both at guided bottoms ("FY26 = bottom, recovery 2027" from Deere and FMC alike), but the market has already paid up for the bellwether (DE +30% YTD, ~34x trough EPS) while laggards (AGCO flat, MOS -9.5%, FMC -25%) sit unpriced. Nitrogen fertilizer is the one leg in a late-stage supply-shock upcycle (CF +53% YTD) that retail urea prices say is already cresting. Farmland is a plateau, not a cycle turn: +3% YoY nominal, falling in real terms, with 56% of ag lenders calling it overvalued.

Crop-protection channel destocking cycle

LATENTinventory18.0/20
phase 5.0aware 5.0asym 5.0runway 3.0
phase troughclock ~3y channel-inventory cycle: 2021-22 COVID/supply-fear overbuy → 2023-25 global distributor destock + generic price war → 2026 trough; volume recovery historically in year 4-5asymmetry high

Where on the clock. FMC's Q2-26 (rep. 2026-07-30) is a textbook trough print: revenue -17% YoY to $867M, FY26 guidance cut again (EBITDA to $620-680M, EPS to $1.19-1.49), the dividend held at a post-cut $0.08/quarter, India business divested — while management explicitly guides '2026 may mark the bottom, growth returns 2027'. The driver is cyclical grower austerity (ex-govt-payment farm income at a 6-yr low) layered on generic pricing. Corteva's relative resilience (+17.4% YTD) shows the pain is concentrated in legacy/generic-exposed portfolios, i.e. inventory + price cycle, partly secular for FMC specifically.

Who already knows. Nobody wants this: FMC -25.3% YTD (verified from $13.87 12/31/25 close) to $10.36, -75% from its 52w high, $1.3B market cap, dividend cut ~86% in Feb-2025 and held since, three guidance cuts — positioning and sentiment are washed out and no recovery is priced anywhere in the generic-exposed complex. This is the only sub-cycle in the segment where the KEY plays are at multi-year lows rather than highs. (Corteva itself is recognition-stage, ~24x fwd EPS est.)

Why the payoff is shaped this way. Classic year-3 destock trough + capitulation pricing + both company and peer guidance pointing to 2027 volume recovery = high asymmetry — BUT it is levered asymmetry: FMC's ~$3.3B net debt vs $650M EBITDA guide (~5x, est.) means the equity is an option that could be diluted before the cycle pays; the honest play is small sizing or the quality end (CTVA) at a fair-not-cheap price.

Runway. Destock ends 2026 (distributor inventories normalizing per both FMC and Corteva commentary); volume recovery 2027-28 as grower incomes turn; the generic-pricing and FMC diamide-patent-cliff components are secular and do NOT mean-revert.

Grain/oilseed price & farm income cycle

EARLYcommodity-supply15.7/20
phase 4.7aware 4.0asym 3.0runway 4.0
phase early-upcycleclock ~4-6y peak-to-trough commodity cycle (2012 peak→2019 trough→2022 peak→2024-25 trough); supply-shock legs run 12-24m until the next acreage/weather response; farm income lags price by ~1y, farmland lags income by 1-2yasymmetry medium

Where on the clock. Trough confirmed 2024-25 (2025 NFI revised DOWN $25B to $154.6B; ex-govt-payment 2026 income at a 6-yr low). Turn evidence dated 2026: KC wheat +40.3% YTD on the smallest US wheat crop since 1970/71 (Jul-26 USDA); China resumed soy buying under the 25 MMT/yr deal with beans +13.6% YTD to $11.90 (Jul-26); corn +7.6% YTD but at $4.74 still below the ~$4.75-5.40/bu breakeven — the classic below-cost-of-production trough from which upcycles start. Farmland is the lagging plateau: +3% YoY, -1% QoQ (Chicago Fed Q1-26).

Who already knows. Split-honest: the wheat leg hit recognition in July (16% monthly rally, front pages of ag press); soy recognition is building (contract highs Jul-24). But the corn/farm-income leg is still early-to-latent — corn is below breakeven, MOO is only +11.3% YTD, farmland REITs are AT or NEAR 52-week lows (LAND -11.9%, FPI -1.2%), and grain-handler moves (ADM +35.8%) are mostly self-help recovery, not cycle pricing. No key play is +80-100% 1y or at decade-high multiples.

Why the payoff is shaped this way. Corn below cost of production with demand strengthening and stocks being revised down is a favorable base; but the easy wheat money is made, the soy rally is one political decision from reversing, and record Brazilian supply caps upside. Best remaining asymmetry is in the un-repriced derivatives of the cycle (farmland REITs at ~30-45% NAV discounts, irrigation) rather than the futures themselves.

Runway. 12-24 months of runway on the price leg (wheat replant + Russia export normalization ends the shock in 2027; corn needs a weather or demand kicker); the income leg recovers into 2027 if prices hold — ended by the 2027 global acreage response and any China-deal breakdown.

Ag equipment inventory/replacement cycle (Deere/CNH/AGCO)

RECOGNITIONinventory14.0/20
phase 5.0aware 2.0asym 3.0runway 4.0
phase troughclock 3-4y dealer-inventory destock/restock cycle nested in a 7-10y fleet-replacement capex cycle; 2022-23 peak shipments → 2024-26 destock → OEMs guide FY26 as the bottom, restock FY27+asymmetry medium

Where on the clock. Deere Q2 FY26 (rep. 2026-05-21): NA large-ag industry volumes guided -15/-20% for FY26, used hi-HP tractor inventory down mid-teens from peak, management explicitly calling FY26 the cycle bottom with recovery in 2027. AEM June-2026 retail data shows the destock aging: tractors still -18.4% YoY but combines +3.9% — the FIRST YoY combine gain after 50%+ declines in 2025. CNH (rep. 2026-08-03) beat and narrowed FY26 guidance to the high end with ag sales ~flat — downgrades have stopped. Third straight year of decline = textbook year-3 inventory-cycle bottom.

Who already knows. The bottom is consensus and the bellwether is paid for it: DE +30.0% YTD (verified from 465.57 12/31/25 close) at $605, ~34x FY26E trough EPS (est.) vs ~15x 10y median — a decade-high multiple, i.e. FY27-28 recovery already in the price. Why the payoff is shaped this way. Low at the bellwether (DE already carries the recovery multiple); medium-high in the laggard spread — AGCO/CNH/TITN price a much shallower recovery, and dealer TITN gives torque to used-equipment pricing normalization. The cycle timing is de-risked (OEM guidance + AEM inflection) but the price you pay for it varies enormously across names.

Runway. Bottom FY26, restock and replacement recovery FY27-29 — fleet age is elevated after 3 down years; ends when the fleet is renewed or the grain cycle rolls over again (~2029-30).

TITNTitan MachineryLargest listed CaseIH dealer — direct torque to used-inventory normalization and the retail restock+20.6%~0.7x tangible book (est.), a level seen only at prior cycle troughs (2016, 2020)
Bear case. At 34x trough EPS the bellwether prices a V-shaped FY27-28 recovery; if ex-government-payment farm income stays at 6-year lows farmers defer another year and 'bottom' becomes an L; tariff-inflated steel/component costs and high floorplan rates squeeze both OEM and dealer margins; AGCO/CNH cheapness partly reflects genuinely weaker EU/South-America books (Brazil risk costs rising per CNH), not just neglect; and a grain-rally failure in 2027 would restart the downgrade cycle from a higher multiple.

Fertilizer cycle (nitrogen-led; N vs K/P split)

RECOGNITIONcommodity-supply / pricing7.0/20
phase 1.5aware 2.0asym 1.0runway 2.5
phase late-cycleclock Nitrogen: 2-3y pricing swings driven by gas spreads + export policy + geopolitics; potash/phosphate: 7-10y capacity cycles. Last N peak 2022, trough 2023-24, new squeeze 2025-26asymmetry low

Where on the clock. Nitrogen is in a supply-shock upcycle — CF Q1-26 sales +19% on 'tight global nitrogen supply-demand further strained by the Iran conflict' plus Chinese export restrictions, Q1 EPS $3.98 smashed $2.50 consensus, and Q2 consensus (reports 2026-08-05) is +141% YoY. But the leading edge has turned: DTN retail urea $714/t was -6% m/m in early July with FIVE straight weeks of declines and UAN32 leading drops into late July. Potash sits flat at $494/t retail — a plateau, no squeeze. Classic late-cycle: peak earnings prints arriving just as spot rolls over.

Who already knows. CF +53.0% YTD (verified from $77.34 12/31/25 close) with the Street already writing 'tight nitrogen' notes and consensus modeling +141% EPS growth — the squeeze is known and substantially priced, though the multiple (~6.5x 2026E, est.) shows the market rightly treats these as peak earnings. UAN +22.5% YTD. The K/P side is the opposite: MOS -9.5% YTD at ~0.56x book (est.) is closer to latent.

Why the payoff is shaped this way. Low on the nitrogen leg — you would be paying after a +53% YTD move for shock earnings whose spot driver is already declining weekly; the honest residual asymmetry is in the unloved potash/phosphate laggards (MOS near decade-low P/B) where any 2027 capacity discipline or India/China contract strength is unpriced, and in nitrogen SHORT-cycle reversal awareness (knowing when to leave).

Runway. N squeeze likely has quarters, not years — ends with Middle-East supply normalization, Chinese export quota resumption, or simply northern-hemisphere demand seasonality (urea already -6% m/m); K/P grinds sideways until the next capacity cycle tightens ~2027-28.

  • CF Q1 2026 earnings shock on tight global nitrogen — net earnings $615M, EPS $3.98 vs $2.50 est (+59% surprise); net sales +19% to $1.99B on higher prices across all segments (Iran-conflict disruption + China export limits) as of 2026-05-07Investing.com Q1-26 slides recap · iGrowNews · Morningstar
  • Street expects a peak-cycle Q2 print from CF — Q2-26 consensus EPS $5.71, +141% YoY; reports after close 2026-08-05 as of 2026-08-04MSN/Barchart earnings preview · Nasdaq.com CF coverage
  • Retail nitrogen prices are rolling over at the leading edge — urea $714/t first-week July, -6% m/m, 5th straight week of declines; UAN32 leading 4-nutrient drops late July; urea still far above the ~$400-500 pre-2021 norm as of 2026-07-29DTN retail fertilizer trends · FarmWeekNow weekly recap
  • Potash is a plateau, not a squeeze — retail potash $494/t, roughly flat m/m through July as of 2026-07-15DTN (Jul-15 and Jul-8 weekly articles · FarmWeekNow
  • Equity tape confirms this is nitrogen-specific, not a broad fertilizer bull — CF +53.0% YTD, UAN +22.5% vs NTR +6.9%, MOS -9.5% (all from verified 12/31/25 closes) as of 2026-08-03FMP live quotes · FMP EOD history 2025-12-31

CFCF IndustriesTHE pure NA nitrogen leverage: low-cost US gas vs shocked global urea/ammonia prices+53.0%~6.5x 2026E EPS (est.) — optically cheap but on peak-shock earnings; -5.5% into the Aug-5 print shows sell-the-news risk

Bear case. Supply-shock nitrogen pricing reverses as fast as it arrives: urea is already -6% m/m with five straight down weeks, the Aug-5 Q2 print is plausibly the peak-EPS moment of the whole cycle, and either Middle-East normalization or a Chinese export-quota resumption breaks the price overnight; meanwhile farmer economics (corn below breakeven) cap how much of the input inflation growers will absorb into 2027 spring, and K/P stay capped by ample capacity — so the laggard trade (MOS) can stay cheap for years.
Best risk/reward in this segment. Crop-protection destocking (sub-cycle 4) has the best remaining risk/reward for an early entrant: it is the only leg still at LATENT awareness with textbook capitulation markers (FMC's ~86% dividend cut, -75% from highs, kitchen-sink guide) while both company guidance and 3-year destock-cycle history point to a 2027 volume recovery — provided it is sized for FMC's balance-sheet risk (or taken via CTVA); runner-up is the corn/farm-income leg of sub-cycle 1, where price still sits below cost of production while demand quietly strengthens.
AI-infrastructure overlap — excluded from this scan. Genuine AI-infra demand inside this segment is negligible (<5% of any sub-cycle's economics), but three honest touchpoints: (1) AI-datacenter gas/power demand is a COST headwind to nitrogen producers (raises CF/UAN's Henry Hub input), not demand — though CF's low-carbon-ammonia/power-adjacency narrative occasionally borrows AI-multiple energy; (2) farmland REITs (FPI, LAND) carry embedded solar/datacenter land-conversion optionality that quietly supports Corn Belt NAVs — a real but small AI-adjacent kicker I have NOT counted in the farmland thesis; (3) Deere's precision-ag/autonomy story is AI APPLICATION (a margin/mix story), not AI-infra demand, and is excluded from the cycle diagnosis. No part of grain, fertilizer, or crop-protection demand is AI in disguise.

Industrials & capex (ex-AI)

4 cyclesbest: NATO rearmament 14.3/20 · rank 15

This segment is not one cycle in 2026 but four running badly out of phase. Commercial aerospace is the hottest and most crowded leg — Safran's H1-2026 civil spare-parts sales rose 27.9% and GE Aerospace's Q2 services revenue 27% — but GE now trades at 33x EV/EBITDA versus 9x in FY2023, so the re-rating leg is finished and earnings must do all future work. European rearmament is the mirror image: Rheinmetall's backlog grew 30% to EUR73bn while the stock fell 25% YTD and sits ~40% below its 52-week high, i.e. the spending clock has ~9 years left while the equity cycle already took its washout. The US short-cycle industrial upturn is genuine and only ~7 months old (ISM 55.6 in July, highest since May-2022) but the liquid proxies have already re-rated 20-100%; meanwhile US non-residential construction ex-datacenter is in a real two-year downcycle with the design pipeline at a 41-month low — a trough to watch, not yet a trough to own.

European/NATO rearmament — defence procurement capex

RECOGNITIONpolicy/budget capex cycle14.3/20
phase 3.3aware 2.0asym 5.0runway 4.0
phase early-to-mid upcycle on the spending clock; the equity cycle is mid-correctionclock 10-15 years: NATO's 5%-of-GDP-by-2035 path (3.5% core + 1.5% security) set at The Hague in 2025 and reaffirmed at Ankara in July 2026, with a 2-5 year lag from budget to contract to delivered revenue. The equity AWARENESS cycle inside it is only ~2-3 years and has already completed one full swing.asymmetry high

Where on the clock. Spending is still accelerating. NATO's Ankara Summit (7-8 July 2026) reaffirmed 5% by 2035 and Secretary General commentary put European allies plus Canada at roughly 4% of GDP on defence-and-security one year into a ten-year plan; European allies added >USD139bn of core defence investment in 2025 and all 32 allies cleared the old 2% floor for the first time. SIPRI puts Europe at ~USD563bn in 2025, +12.6% in real terms. Company-level confirmation: Rheinmetall's order backlog hit EUR73bn at 31-Mar-2026, +30.3% from EUR56bn a year earlier, with FY26 sales guided to EUR14-14.5bn (~+43%) at ~19% margin. Meanwhile the shares have done the opposite: RHM -25.0% YTD and ~40% below its EUR2,008 52w high, RENK -11.9% YTD and -29.5% over 1Y, Hensoldt at EUR87.5 vs a EUR117.7 high, and in the US AeroVironment -30.5% YTD, Kratos -27.1% YTD, Northrop -2.2% YTD. Order books compounding, multiples compressing.

Who already knows. Not latent — the theme is universally known and was the market's most crowded long in 2024-25. But it is no longer euphoria: 2026 has been an execution-scrutiny de-rating year. Rheinmetall's EV/EBITDA fell from 32.1x at FY2025 to 26.1x TTM while backlog grew 30%; Germany's June-2026 cancellation of the F126 frigate programme took the stock down 18% in a session. Dispersion has replaced beta — BAE +25.8% YTD at 17.7x vs Rheinmetall -25.0% YTD at 26.1x, Saab +11.6% at 28.7x vs Leonardo +12.1% at 15.5x. Positioning has been washed out; the fundamentals have not.

Why the payoff is shaped this way. The clock has roughly nine years left on a treaty-level commitment while the equity cycle has already delivered a 25-46% drawdown in the pure plays. Ceasefire risk is now partly priced (public prediction markets around 36.5% odds of a 2026 deal), and a ceasefire arguably does not reverse the 3.5% core-defence floor — replenishing depleted European stockpiles is a contracted multi-year obligation rather than a war-duration bet. The specific edge is that the market is treating budget headlines as spent and execution as unproven, which is exactly the point in a policy capex cycle where delivered revenue and margin start to arrive. Cheapest expression: the cash-generative primes rather than the German pure-play beta.

Runway. Contract awards and delivered revenue should run through at least 2030-2032 given a ten-year plan with 2-5 year lags. The most likely path is 12-24 months of range-bound, dispersion-heavy trading while execution is proven, then a second leg driven by recognised revenue and margin rather than order headlines. What ends it: a peace settlement combined with a European fiscal squeeze, or industry-wide failure to convert backlog into cash.

Bear case. Budgets are pledges, not cash, and the Ankara 5%/2035 path is deliberately back-loaded into years when Europe's fiscal room is smaller. The June-2026 F126 cancellation proved governments will kill a EUR12bn programme overnight and that the market no longer rewards order intake on its own. Execution is now the binding constraint rather than demand: Rheinmetall's 416-day cash conversion cycle and ~51% capex/OCF mean growth consumes cash, so backlog can compound while free cash flow does not. A credible Ukraine settlement — roughly 36.5% priced for 2026 — would very likely trigger a second de-rating leg even if procurement continues. And 'de-rated' still leaves Rheinmetall at ~26x EV/EBITDA, over four times its pre-2022 multiple. The US primes are the cautionary control group: budget growth and shareholder returns are not the same thing, as Northrop (-2.2% YTD, trading 28% below its 52w high) and L3Harris (below its 200-day) demonstrate.

US non-residential construction & reshoring factory capex (ex-datacenter)

EARLYcapex cycle, policy-pulsed13.0/20
phase 3.0aware 4.0asym 3.0runway 3.0
phase downcycle, roughly two years in, trough-formingclock 7-10 year non-residential building capex cycle. Superimposed on it, the 2021-24 CHIPS/IRA factory pulse was a ~3-year policy spike that is now unwinding into a 2026-27 trough; the design pipeline (ABI) leads spending by roughly 9-12 months and the Dodge Momentum Index by 12-18 months.asymmetry medium

Where on the clock. Manufacturing construction peaked at ~USD249.8bn SAAR in August 2024 and had fallen to ~USD175bn by May 2026, about -30% from peak. June-2026 total US construction was USD2,166.5bn SAAR, -0.1% m/m, with manufacturing -1.2% and commercial -1.2% m/m, and first-half 2026 total spending -3.5% y/y. The AIA's 20-July-2026 consensus panel projects manufacturing -11.6% in 2026 but only -0.6% in 2027 — the rate of decline flattening is what a trough looks like from a year out. The design pipeline is at its worst on record: ABI 47.3 in June 2026 (from 44.5 in May), 41 consecutive months without a majority of firms reporting billings growth, with average backlog down to 6.3 months from 6.6. Nothing here has bottomed yet, but the second derivative is turning.

Who already knows. The downcycle is fully recognised; the trough is not being positioned for at all. The tell is participation: in a year when the AI-linked contractors ran hard — Comfort Systems +86.1% YTD, Sterling Infrastructure +75.0%, Quanta +61.8%, EMCOR +34.4% — the traditional heavy-materials complex went nowhere: Vulcan +0.5% YTD, Martin Marietta -11.1%, Knife River -3.6% (-21.2% over 1Y), Construction Partners -3.2%, Eagle Materials +5.6%. That divergence is the whole opportunity and it is not a consensus trade. What stops it being 'latent' is that the aggregates-versus-datacenter valuation-gap argument does circulate, weakly, in value circles.

Why the payoff is shaped this way. The valuation and performance dispersion between AI-linked contractors and traditional heavy materials is at an extreme, and the sector's own forecasting body has manufacturing construction flattening in 2027. Real multiple support exists at the cheap end — Eagle Materials at 11.2x EV/EBITDA and Knife River at 12.5x, with Vulcan at 16.3x versus 16.5-21.6x over the prior five years. But there is no catalyst yet: ABI has to cross 50 first, and because the 2021-24 level was a policy artifact rather than organic demand, the recovery could be a long flat L rather than a V. This is a 12-24 month asymmetry, not a 6-month one.

Runway. On the AIA panel's own numbers, manufacturing construction declines flatten from -11.6% in 2026 to -0.6% in 2027, so the spending trough plausibly lands in late 2027. Because ABI leads by 9-12 months and Dodge by 12-18, the equity turn should precede that by two to four quarters — i.e. a first credible entry window opens once ABI holds above 50 for a quarter, most likely somewhere in 2027. What ends the downcycle: a highway reauthorisation with real multi-year funding, plus a rate path that lets private commercial projects out of the drawer. What extends it: a recession, or tariffs keeping construction cost inflation running ahead of rent and revenue growth.

Bear case. The CHIPS/IRA pulse may simply not come back — the 2021-24 level was a policy artifact that tripled manufacturing construction in three years, so mean reversion could take the series well below any 'normal', making this a long flat L rather than a V. Tariffs and elevated construction costs keep private projects shelved, and the ABI's 41-month sub-50 streak says the design pipeline that feeds 2027-28 spending is still emptying, not refilling. Public work carries its own funding cliff: the IIJA surface-transportation authorisation runs through FY2026 (est.), so a lapse or a thin reauthorisation would hit exactly the highway names (Knife River, Granite, Construction Partners) that look cheapest. Valuations are not distressed either — Martin Marietta is still 20.8x EV/EBITDA and Vulcan 16.3x, so this is 'unloved', not 'cheap'. And a recession would extend the downcycle by years rather than months, with aggregates volumes falling before pricing gives way.

US short-cycle manufacturing & factory-automation order cycle

RECOGNITIONinventory / short-cycle capital-goods order cycle12.5/20
phase 4.5aware 2.0asym 3.0runway 3.0
phase early-upcycleclock 3-4 years peak-to-peak. Last peak 2021-22; ISM sat below 50 for most of 2022-2025; trough in 2025. The current expansion is roughly 7 months old and upturns of this shape historically run 18-30 months.asymmetry medium

Where on the clock. The turn is confirmed across three independent datasets. ISM Manufacturing PMI printed 55.6 in July 2026, +2.3pt from June's 53.3 and the highest since May 2022, with New Orders at 56.7 (seventh consecutive month of expansion after a four-month contraction), Production 58.5 (best since November 2021) and Backlog of Orders jumping to 55.0 from 50.5. Capital-equipment orders confirm it: USMTO metalworking machinery orders were USD583.4m in May-2026, +47.8% y/y, with January-May at USD2.77bn, +31.9% y/y. Crucially, the robot leg has NOT turned — A3 North America Q1-2026 orders were 9,055 units, -0.1% y/y and -6.4% in dollars, with Automotive OEM down 35.1% in units. That missing leg is what keeps this early rather than mid.

Who already knows. The market is pricing the turn quickly and in some cases violently: Cognex +96.5% YTD at 42.3x EV/EBITDA and 10.6x EV/Sales, Zebra +52.0%, MSC Industrial +49.3% and at a 52-week high, Hurco +41.7%, Honeywell +27.2%, Emerson +22.4%, Applied Industrial at a 52-week high, Kennametal +20.2%. Rockwell is a relative laggard at +15.7% but trades at 29.8x EV/EBITDA on still-depressed margins. Nothing in this complex is latent; the open question is whether earnings can catch multiples that already discount a full-cycle recovery.

Why the payoff is shaped this way. Genuinely early on the ORDER clock — month seven of an expansion that typically runs 18-30 months — with the auto and robot leg still in outright recession, which is real remaining fuel rather than hope. But the liquid proxies have re-rated 20-100% already, so the asymmetry no longer lives in the automation platforms. It sits in the cheap, consumable, machining-hours-levered tier that gets paid on volume rather than on project capex, and in whatever turns when auto OEM capex finally inflects.

Runway. If this is a genuine inventory cycle, roughly 11-23 months of order growth remain, with the auto/robot leg the natural second-half fuel. It ends when ISM New Orders roll back under 50 or when the tariff pre-buy reverses — watch the New Orders minus Customers' Inventories spread rather than the headline PMI, because the headline will keep printing well after the marginal order has already been placed.

Bear case. ISM diffusion indices measure direction, not volume, and 2026's readings are plausibly inflated by tariff-driven price pass-through and inventory pre-buying: firms reporting 'more expensive, ordering ahead' push New Orders above 50 without any real unit growth. The A3 data is the tell — robot units were flat and dollars actually fell in Q1-2026, meaning nobody is installing incremental capacity yet, and auto OEM capex is in outright recession at -35% units. If ISM is a tariff pull-forward, it unwinds inside two to three quarters and the 'early-cycle' framing was a mirage. Meanwhile the market has already paid for the recovery: Cognex at 42x EV/EBITDA and Rockwell at 30x on depressed margins price in the full cycle before a single robot order has inflected.

Commercial aerospace: OEM delivery ramp + engine aftermarket/MRO

EUPHORIAcapex (OEM ramp) layered on an installed-base adoption S-curve (aftermarket shop-visit wave)7.0/20
phase 2.5aware 0.5asym 1.0runway 3.0
phase mid-cycle on units, late-mid-cycle on aftermarket earnings and multiplesclock 8-12y OEM recovery off the 2020-21 trough (737 rate 42->47->53, A320 toward 75); the engine aftermarket is a separate ~15y wave — IATA sees LEAP shop visits going from 600-800/yr in 2025 to >5,000/yr by 2040asymmetry low

Where on the clock. Units are still climbing: Boeing delivered 314 aircraft in H1-2026, +12% y/y, with Q2 at 171 (+14%), and the FAA capstone review for 737 rate 47/mo was passed 27-May-2026 (target 53/mo by end-2026, 787 stable at 8/mo). Backlogs are records — Boeing USD715.3bn total, >6,200 commercial jets; Airbus ~9,000+ jets, ~10.6 years of output. But the aftermarket earnings leg is already running white-hot: Safran H1-2026 civil spare parts +27.9% in USD, civil services >+40%, guidance raised; GE Aerospace Q2-2026 CES revenue +27% with orders +17% and raised FY guidance. That combination — record backlog, constrained supply, accelerating spares pricing — is what a late-mid-cycle aerospace print looks like, not an early one.

Who already knows. The engine complex is at or near all-time highs on decade-high multiples: GE +23.8% YTD and +40.1% 1Y at 33.2x EV/EBITDA TTM vs 27.9x FY2025, 19.2x FY2024 and 9.3x FY2023; Rolls-Royce +36.6% YTD at an all-time high (1,570p vs a 990p 52w low); Safran +20.5% YTD at its 52w high; Howmet +42.1% YTD, +62.4% 1Y; RTX +21.2% YTD trading at its 52w high. That is not latent by any definition. The honest nuance is dispersion: the high-multiple aftermarket compounders are de-rating even as fundamentals accelerate — TransDigm -5.7% YTD and -11.4% over 1Y, HEICO only +13.4% YTD at 39.1x EV/EBITDA. The market is paying for engine OEM economics and selling paying-up-for-quality.

Why the payoff is shaped this way. The cycle has runway but the multiple does not. GE re-rated ~3.5x in three years, so from here returns require earnings to compound into a 33x multiple with no accident. Whatever asymmetry remains sits outside the megacaps — in the de-rated aftermarket compounders, in the capacity-constrained independent MRO tier, and in the levered-but-unloved RRSP holders — not in the names that have already made new highs.

Runway. The unit cycle has ~2-3 more years of growth (737 47->53->57, A320 toward 75) and the shop-visit wave runs deep into the 2030s, so the CYCLE is far from over. What is over is the re-rating: from here the trade is a de-rating race against earnings compounding. Practically, expect the sector to keep making highs while individual multiples compress — the TransDigm/HEICO pattern of 2026 spreading to the engine OEMs is the likely way this ends, not a demand collapse.

HEIHEICO CorporationPMA replacement parts and used serviceable material — the deflationary counter-play to OEM spares pricing+13.4%EV/EBITDA 39.1x TTM, EV/Sales 10.9x, earnings yield 1.54% (~65x P/E) — the top of its historic band while lagging the engine OEMs
Bear case. Much of the aftermarket surge is price and scarcity rather than volume: OEM spares list escalations plus a used-serviceable-material shortage. The cure is already in motion — Safran's LEAP deliveries rose 41% in H1-2026, and as spare-engine availability improves, AOG falls, green time returns and shop-visit urgency (and pricing power) normalise. GE at 33x EV/EBITDA and HEICO at 39x leave zero room for that. Boeing still posted a USD322m operating loss in Q2-2026, so the delivery ramp is not yet earnings. Add crowded consensus positioning at 52-week highs, and a single demand shock (fuel spike, recession, a China order freeze) or a technical event on a LEAP/GTF fleet would hit both the OEM and aftermarket legs simultaneously.
Best risk/reward in this segment. European/NATO rearmament — it is the only sub-cycle where the fundamental clock still has roughly nine years to run (Ankara reaffirmed 5% of GDP by 2035; Rheinmetall's backlog +30.3% to EUR73bn) while the equity cycle has ALREADY completed a full euphoria-to-washout swing, with the pure plays down 25-46% from their highs and multiples compressing against a rising order book — the rare setup where you get a recognised theme at post-drawdown prices rather than having to be early on the idea itself.
AI-infrastructure overlap — excluded from this scan. Three of the four sub-cycles have measurable AI-infrastructure contamination, and one is nearly clean. (1) Non-residential construction — the largest and most quantifiable overlap. The AIA's July-2026 consensus panel puts commercial construction at +4.8% in 2026 and +5.8% in 2027, but states that excluding data centers those figures become -1% and +1%; data centers alone are forecast at +33% and +25%. So essentially ALL of the growth in US commercial construction is AI-datacenter demand, and the ex-AI segment is flat to negative. The June-2026 Dodge Momentum Index decline (-1.9% m/m to 271.7) was likewise driven specifically by slowing data-center planning. This is why I excluded the contractors that have actually performed in 2026 — Comfort Systems +86.1% YTD, Sterling Infrastructure +75.0%, Quanta +61.8%, EMCOR +34.4%, Argan (AGX) and MasTec — from the plays: their 2026 moves are AI-infra earnings, not a non-residential construction cycle. Everything I list for that sub-cycle (Vulcan, Martin Marietta, Eagle Materials, Knife River, Granite, Construction Partners, Astec) is heavy-materials or public-works exposure with negligible datacenter revenue, which is exactly why it is flat while the hybrids doubled. (2) Automation/short-cycle industrials — a moderate, harder-to-isolate overlap I estimate at roughly 15-30% of the observed 'automation recovery' (est., not directly measurable from public data). Concretely: Teradyne reported its first-ever USD100m robotics quarter in July 2026 explicitly attributed to AI factory buildout, which is why I excluded TER despite its optical fit; Zebra (+52.0% YTD) and Cognex (+96.5%) carry electronics- and semiconductor-assembly inspection exposure that is partly AI-server manufacturing rather than general factory automation — I kept Cognex as a play but flagged it as the most expensive and most AI-contaminated of the four; and Rockwell, Emerson and Honeywell all have datacenter power, cooling and controls revenue lines. Even the USMTO metalworking series has a precision/semiconductor component inside its +31.9% YTD. The cleanest reads in this sub-cycle are the ISM New Orders index, the A3 automotive-OEM robot line (in outright recession at -35.1%, i.e. definitionally not AI demand) and Kennametal's machining-hours consumables — which is why I anchored the phase call on those rather than on the share prices. (3) Reshoring factory buildout — a definitional overlap worth naming honestly. The Census 'manufacturing construction' series that peaked at ~USD249.8bn in August 2024 and has fallen ~30% was substantially computer/electronic/electrical plant, i.e. CHIPS-funded semiconductor fabs. Some of that is upstream of AI compute. But it is fab construction, not datacenter construction, and critically it is the DECLINE that I am analysing, so treating it as AI-infra demand would not change the diagnosis: the policy pulse is unwinding either way. I have counted none of it as AI-infra upside. (4) Aerospace and defence — essentially clean. Two minor indirect linkages worth noting rather than counting: aero-engine castings and forgings capacity (Howmet, Hexcel, Precision-type suppliers) competes for the same specialty-alloy and large-forging capacity as the heavy-duty gas turbines being built for datacenter power, which tightens aerospace supply chains as a second-order effect; and some defence-electronics names (Mercury Systems, Leonardo DRS, Kratos) attract AI/edge-compute narrative flow that is not budget-driven. Neither is material to the cycle diagnosis. Note also that GE Aerospace is a separate company from GE Vernova, so the GE ticker used here carries no datacenter-power exposure.

Financials & credit

4 cyclesbest: Crypto post-halving 15.0/20 · rank 12

Financials in Aug-2026 are four cycles running on different clocks, and the market is pricing them as one. Banks are in an early-mid NIM upcycle (FDIC industry NIM 3.31% in Q1-26, -8bp QoQ, but Q2-26 regional prints inflected up on a steep curve with fed funds parked at 3.50-3.75%) — and that is already recognised, with KRE +19.3% YTD at a 52-week high. Capital markets are violently K-shaped: record $2.8T H1 M&A value on a six-year-low deal COUNT, so bulge-bracket and mega-IPO names re-rated while mid-market advisers de-rated 25-35%. Private credit is in the first genuine downcycle of its life — Fitch's US private-credit default rate hit a record 6.0% in April, listed BDCs trade at the widest NAV discounts since Oct-2020, and non-traded BDCs saw their first-ever net outflow quarter. Crypto is ~10 months into a -49% post-halving drawdown with record ETF outflows and miner capitulation. A fifth cycle I had to compress for space: the P&C underwriting cycle has definitively turned soft (CIAB composite -1.2% in Q1-26, ending a 33-quarter run of increases; Guy Carpenter global property-cat rate-on-line -12% at 1/1/26) while TRV trades at ~2.8x tangible book and +31.9% YTD — a downcycle being paid a peak multiple, which is the lowest-asymmetry corner of the whole segment.

Digital-asset cycle (post-halving drawdown)

EARLY4-year halving + liquidity/adoption cycle15.0/20
phase 3.0aware 4.0asym 5.0runway 3.0
phase downcycle, late-ishclock ~4y halving clock: April-2024 halving → cycle top Oct-2025 (~18 months post-halving, on schedule) → bear typically 12-14 months → next halving April-2028. We are ~28 months post-halving and ~10 months into the bearasymmetry high

Where on the clock. BTC is $64,574 as of 2026-08-05, -48.9% from the $126,296 all-time high set 2025-10-06 — 10 months into a drawdown that historically runs 12-14 months. Capitulation markers are present: US spot BTC ETFs posted -$5.4B in H1-26, the first negative half-year since the Jan-2024 launch, with June's ~-$4.5B the largest single month on record and an eight-week streak from mid-May to early-July exceeding -$8B. Listed miners sold >32,000 BTC in Q1-26, a single-quarter record exceeding all of 2025, with hashprice at post-halving lows in the high-$20s/PH/day against ~$35 breakeven for older rigs — and network hashrate is now falling. Strategy's basic mNAV fell to 0.68x on 2026-08-03, inverting the treasury-company flywheel. What is NOT present is a resolved regulatory catalyst: the CLARITY Act still has not reached a Senate floor vote.

Who already knows. Nobody wants this. COIN -33.7% YTD and -49.7% over 1Y; IBIT -26.0% YTD and -43.1% over 1Y; MSTR -35.3% YTD and -73.8% over 1Y; CRCL -58.9% over 1Y; ETH $1,908 vs a $4,954 52-week high; SOL $74 vs $254. Sentiment is at extreme-fear readings and institutional holders have de-risked (Jane Street cut ETF holdings ~70% in Q1). That is the opposite of euphoria — but calling it 'latent' would be dishonest, because the four-year clock is the single most-discussed framework in the space. It is early-in-the-recovery, not undiscovered.

Why the payoff is shaped this way. High expected asymmetry with an unusually fat loss tail — both must be stated. Prior post-halving bears ran 77-85% peak-to-trough; this one is at -49%, so a historically-typical bottom implies $19k-$29k, another 55-70% below spot. Against that: the institutional-rails argument (ETFs, mNAV-constrained treasuries, regulated custody) has genuinely changed the marginal holder; MVRV peaked at only 2.524 this cycle versus 3.5+ in 2017 and 2021, so no euphoric top was ever made; and April-2028 halving supply mechanics are unchanged. Framing this as a small, staged, wide-stop option on a 2027 upcycle carries far better geometry than a conviction-sized position.

Runway. On the four-year clock, 2-6 more months of bear (a 12-14 month drawdown from Oct-2025 lands Oct-2026 to Dec-2026), then a 12-18 month base before the April-2028 halving becomes the narrative. Miner capitulation and record ETF outflows are late-bear, not early-bear, markers. What ends the bear early: a CLARITY Act Senate floor vote, or four straight weeks of >$1B ETF inflows. What extends it: a Fed hike (three dissents already wanted one) draining the liquidity this asset is a pure duration bet on, or continued capital rotation into AI assets.

Bear case. The four-year clock may simply be dead. This cycle already broke pattern — BTC made an all-time high BEFORE the April-2024 halving, which had never happened — and if ETFs turned bitcoin into a liquidity-beta asset rather than a supply-schedule asset, the relevant clock is the Fed's, not the halving's, and the Fed is on hold with hike dissents. Prior bears ran 77-85% peak-to-trough; -49% is not obviously enough. The structural bid is visibly impaired: the treasury-company flywheel is inverted at 0.68x mNAV, so a cohort that bought hundreds of thousands of coins on the way up is now a potential seller; miners are already record sellers; ETF holders have shown they redeem. And the regulatory catalyst everyone underwrote has ~15% odds of landing in 2026. A -49% drawdown extending to -75% before the trough is entirely consistent with every prior cycle.

Capital-markets revival cycle (M&A / ECM / advisory fee pool)

RECOGNITIONcapital-markets activity cycle (5-7y), currently bifurcated11.8/20
phase 2.3aware 2.0asym 5.0runway 2.5
phase K-shaped: mega-cap leg late-cycle/peak, mid-market leg trough-to-early-upcycleclock 5-7y from trough to trough (2000, 2007, 2015, 2021 peaks). The fee pool follows deal COUNT, not deal VALUE — advisory fees are a declining % of value, so 24,000 deals at $2.8T pays boutiques far less than 30,000 deals at $2.0Tasymmetry high

Where on the clock. H1-26 announced M&A hit a record $2.8T (+48% YoY, highest since LSEG records began in 1980) — but deal COUNT fell 9% to ~24,000, the weakest first half in six years, and 47 deals above $10B accounted for >$1.3T (~50% of value, an all-time concentration record). US IPO proceeds hit a record $104.9B in Q2 on 48 deals, but $75B of that was SpaceX alone. GS Q2-26 IB fees +55% YoY were driven by equity underwriting +130% and debt underwriting +75% — underwriting, not advisory. Meanwhile the PE exit backlog is >13,000 US portfolio companies (~8 years of inventory at the current exit pace), 30% aged 7+ years: the mid-market has not cycled at all.

Who already knows. Honest split within one label. The mega-cap leg is at euphoria: GS +20.6% YTD at ~2.5x tangible book (est.: $313B cap / $123B tangible equity), roughly double its 2015-23 average; MS +22.9% YTD and +55.4% over 1Y at ~17x TTM earnings. The mid-market leg is barely at early: HLI -26.4% YTD and -33.4% over 1Y, EVR -6.3% YTD and only +6.7% over 1Y, JEF -9.3% YTD, PWP flat YTD and -21.9% over 1Y. The pure mid-market fee-pool proxies have gone backwards through a record M&A tape — that is the definition of an un-priced leg, and the blended stage is recognition only because the headline number is famous.

Why the payoff is shaped this way. The asymmetry lives entirely in the laggard leg. HLI at 18.3x FY-Mar-2027E EPS ($7.00 consensus) versus its 25-30x post-2021 range, and EVR at 14.0x 2027E EPS ($22.73 consensus) versus 18.7x TTM, price in a mid-market that never recovers. But the forcing function is mechanical: 13,000 unsold portfolio companies against LPs who now name DPI as their top metric (21% cite it, up from 8% three years ago). That inventory must clear through sale, IPO or continuation vehicle — and every route pays a mid-market adviser. Buying the leg that has NOT moved while the aggregate tape sets records is the cleanest early-entry setup in the segment.

Runway. The mega-deal/ECM leg is late-cycle — record concentration, record proceeds and a $2.8T print are peak-shaped, so assume 2-4 quarters of runway there. The mid-market leg has 2-4 YEARS of runway if it turns, because it has not participated at all and the 13,000-company backlog is roughly eight years of inventory. What ends it: a high-yield spread blowout above ~450bp, or an equity drawdown that shuts the IPO window and refreezes sponsor exits — both would hit the laggard leg hardest first.

Bear case. The bifurcation may not be a lag — it may be structural. If AI-driven and power/grid strategic consolidation is genuinely the only deal theme with financing, the mid-market never broadens, and HLI/EVR/PWP are cheap for a permanent reason rather than a cyclical one. Renaissance itself notes the IPO window is concentrated in AI and defence. Meanwhile the mid-market's natural buyer — the private-credit-funded sponsor LBO — is exactly the channel now in stress (record 6.0% private-credit defaults, first-ever non-traded BDC net outflows). If sponsors cannot finance, the exit backlog clears through continuation vehicles and NAV loans instead, which pay secondaries advisers, not M&A boutiques. And the boutiques' cost base is people: a three-year fee drought with flat comp destroys operating leverage in both directions.

Rate cycle → bank NIM & deposit-franchise cycle

RECOGNITIONrate/credit (bank margin cycle)10.5/20
phase 3.5aware 2.0asym 1.0runway 4.0
phase early-mid upcycleclock 5-7y policy-rate cycle; NIM lags policy by 4-8 quarters because ~40% of bank earning assets are fixed-rate paper booked in 2020-21 that reprices on a 3-5y schedule regardless of what the Fed doesasymmetry low

Where on the clock. FDIC industry NIM was still falling in Q1-26 (3.31%, -8bp QoQ; asset yields -21bp vs cost of funds -13bp), but Q2-26 regional prints turned: Hanover 2.96→3.10%, Hope 2.90→2.96%, NB Bancorp 3.93→4.00%, California BanCorp 4.47→4.71%. The driver is the curve, not cuts — fed funds held at 3.50-3.75% on 2026-07-29 with three dissents wanting a HIKE, while 10Y sits at 4.63% and 30Y at 5.17%, giving 2s10s ~+45bp. Industry NIM at 3.31% is still ~30-50bp below the 2015-19 norm, so there is runway; the easy multiple move is not.

Who already knows. KRE +19.3% YTD and +29.2% 1Y, sitting at its 52-week high (77.34 vs 78.21). The regional-bank group traded at 1.30x book in Q1-26 versus a 1.14x recent average; $10-100B-asset banks at ~1.6x TBVPS. FITB is +23.1% YTD, +39.3% 1Y, at ~2.25x tangible book (est.). This is a well-owned, well-understood 2026 consensus trade — not latent.

Why the payoff is shaped this way. The NIM tailwind is real and multi-year, but it is already in the multiple: the group re-rated ~14% above its own recent average book multiple BEFORE NIM confirmed the turn. Upside now needs both NIM expansion AND benign credit; the deregulatory/M&A kicker (181 bank deals in 2025, approval times cut to 117 days) is also widely flagged. Downside is asymmetric because a credit-cost surprise hits earnings and the multiple simultaneously.

Runway. 12-24 months of NIM runway from fixed-asset repricing alone (industry NIM 3.31% vs a ~3.6-3.8% pre-2020 norm), largely independent of Fed action. What ends it: a credit-cost cycle that outruns margin gains (see the private-credit sub-cycle), or a genuine hike that re-inverts the curve — three FOMC dissents already wanted one.

Bear case. The market has bought the NIM story at 1.30x book before credit has spoken. Consumer credit is already at stress readings (card delinquency 13.1%, 16-year high; record auto delinquency), CRE non-performers are rising at select super-regionals even as headline charge-offs fall, and banks hold hundreds of billions of direct and indirect private-credit exposure per the FSB. A hike — which three FOMC voters wanted on 2026-07-29 — would re-flatten the curve and hit funding costs, compressing the exact spread the trade is paid for. Regionals also carry a permanent tail: a 2023-style deposit event turns a margin story into a solvency story overnight.

Credit cycle & private-credit maturation

RECOGNITIONcredit cycle + funding/redemption cycle in a $2T asset class experiencing its first full turn10.0/20
phase 2.0aware 2.0asym 3.0runway 3.0
phase early downcycleclock 7-10y credit cycle; the private-credit-specific overlay is a maturity-wall clock — 2021-22 vintage unitranches hit refinancing in 2026-27, and the retail evergreen wrapper adds a 1-2y redemption cycle that has never been testedasymmetry medium

Where on the clock. Fitch's US private-credit default rate hit a record 6.0% in April-2026, with 81 first-time defaulters — the most since tracking began in Aug-2024 — and more than half of defaults driven by interest deferral or PIK conversion, i.e. borrowers stopping cash payment rather than filing. Non-traded BDCs recorded the first-ever quarter where redemptions exceeded fundraising ($6.9B met vs $4.9B raised, Q1-26), and Moody's cut the ~$400B BDC sector outlook to negative in April. Listed BDCs trade at a median ~0.74x forward NAV, the deepest since Oct-2020. On the bank side credit is still benign — 10 of 11 super-regionals posted lower charge-offs and provisions in Q2-26 — which is exactly what an EARLY downcycle looks like: the non-bank lane cracks first.

Who already knows. This is now a headline story, not a hidden one: the FSB published a private-credit vulnerabilities report on 2026-05-06, Moody's cut the sector outlook, and the trade press is saturated. Prices agree — OWL -22.7% YTD and -40.3% over 1Y, ARES -25.6% over 1Y, KKR -25.9% over 1Y, BXSL -25.1% over 1Y, OBDC at 0.77x NAV. But it is NOT capitulation: BDC NAVs themselves have barely moved (the discount, not the mark, has done the work), PIK income is still rising as a share of investment income, and ARCC still trades at ~1.0x NAV. The marks have not confessed yet.

Why the payoff is shaped this way. Two-sided and honest. Near term the asymmetry points DOWN: prices are at recognition but NAVs are not marked, so the second leg comes from write-downs rather than sentiment. The medium-term asymmetry points up — a 0.74x median forward NAV on a floating-rate senior loan book has historically been a good entry once defaults ROLL OVER, and the asset class's structural growth is intact. This is an option on the default peak, not on today's yield. Sizing matters more than direction: a 12% distribution rate on an asset bought at 0.77x that then marks down 15% is a losing trade.

Runway. Credit downcycles from a defined default-rate peak typically run 18-30 months to trough valuations. Defaults peaked (so far) at 6.0% in April-2026 and the 2021-22 unitranche maturity wall lands through 2027, so the plausible trough for BDC marks is H2-2027 to 2028. What ends it: the default rate rolling over for a full quarter AND NAVs actually marking down — historically the entry signal comes AFTER the write-downs, not before. What extends it: a hike (three FOMC dissents already wanted one) keeping floating-rate borrower coverage ratios below 1.5x.

BXSLBlackstone Secured Lending FundSenior-secured, low-PIK end of listed private credit — the 'quality' leg of the downcycle-11.4%≈0.89x NAV ($5.43B cap vs $6.10B net assets); -25.1% over 1Y despite a first-lien-heavy book
ARCCAres Capital CorporationThe scale incumbent; its premium to peers is the market's measure of who is trusted on marks-4.5%≈1.00x NAV ($13.87B cap vs $13.89B net assets) vs a ~0.74x listed-BDC median — the trust premium is the whole thesis
OWLBlue Owl Capital Inc.Fee-stream manager most exposed to retail non-traded/evergreen flows — the funding side of the cycle rather than the credit side-22.7%~19x TTM EV/EBITDA after a 44% fall from its $20.58 52-week high; down from >30x in 2024 (est.)
BXBlackstone Inc.Diversified alt manager — shows how much of the alts de-rate is credit-specific vs a whole-complex multiple reset-11.8%$136.02 vs a $190.09 52-week high (-28%); -18.9% over 1Y while the S&P made highs
Bear case. The bull case for BDCs is 'the discount is the margin of safety'. That fails if the NAVs are wrong. More than half of April's record defaults were interest deferrals and PIK conversions — a structure that lets a lender keep accruing income on a loan that is not paying cash, and PIK income is still rising as a share of BDC investment income. The FSB flagged in May that global banks hold at least hundreds of billions of direct and indirect private-credit exposure, so the loss does not stay in the non-bank lane. Non-traded vehicles have already crossed into net outflow; Blue Owl's proposed merger of a non-traded BDC into a listed one is what a manager does when the liquidity promise breaks. If redemptions force asset sales into a market with no bid, the marks converge down to the discount rather than the discount converging up to the marks — and the alt managers lose the fee stream at the same time.
Best risk/reward in this segment. The mid-market leg of the capital-markets cycle (HLI, EVR, PWP, plus the secondaries complex): global M&A value set an all-time record in H1-26 while deal COUNT hit a six-year low, so the fee pool that actually pays mid-market advisers has not cycled at all — and those names are down 6-26% YTD at 14-18x forward earnings while a >13,000-company PE exit backlog (~8 years of inventory) has to clear through somebody's advisory desk.
AI-infrastructure overlap — excluded from this scan. Material and concentrated in the capital-markets sub-cycle. (1) M&A: technology was the largest H1-26 sector at $649B of the $2.8T (23%), and the record $1.3T mega-deal bucket is visibly AI/power-shaped — NextEra/Dominion at $66.8B and SpaceX/Cursor at ~$60B sit among the 47 deals >$10B, with LSEG-sourced commentary naming AI infrastructure, grid modernisation and defence as the pull. I estimate 15-25% of H1-26 announced VALUE is AI-infra/power/datacenter-linked (est.), which is exactly why my phase call rests on deal COUNT (-9% YoY, six-year low) — a metric AI mega-deals cannot flatter. (2) IPO: Renaissance explicitly notes the window is concentrated in AI and defence and wants it to widen; the next flagged mega-listing is SK hynix (HBM), pure AI-infra. SpaceX's $75B is space/defence rather than AI-infra, but it distorts every H1 proceeds statistic, so I use ex-SpaceX figures throughout. (3) Crypto: I deliberately excluded MARA/RIOT/CLSK/IREN from the plays because their equity is now substantially an AI-datacenter conversion story, not a crypto-cycle story, and I flagged the same contamination inside the Galaxy Digital (Helios) latent-watch entry. (4) Private credit: datacenter and asset-based-finance lending is a growing share of direct-lending origination (not separately quantified in free sources); in the reverse direction, a named driver of BDC NAV pressure is exposure to SOFTWARE borrowers being disrupted by AI — an AI effect, but a credit-negative one that legitimately belongs inside this segment. (5) Banks: datacenter and power project lending contributes to the loan growth in the FDIC data, so part of the bank NIM sub-cycle's volume leg is AI-capex demand in disguise (est., not separately disclosed).

Insurance & underwriting

4 cyclesbest: Insurance brokers 12.5/20 · rank 24

The US P&C hard market that began around 2018 is turning: CIAB's composite commercial rate index went negative for the first time in 33 quarters in Q1 2026 (-1.2%), and Guy Carpenter's global property-cat reinsurance rate-on-line fell ~12% at the 1/1/26 renewal against a record $790bn of global reinsurance capital. But 'the cycle is turning' does not mean the trade is early: flagship carriers Travelers (+31.9% YTD, ~2.6x tangible book, above its own 10-year high of 2.51x) and Allstate (+27.1% YTD) are already being paid euphoria-stage prices for a downcycle. The genuinely under-priced signal sits one layer downstream — a first-ever negative organic-growth quarter at Brown & Brown and a guidance cut at Baldwin Group, while most broker stocks are still marginally up YTD. Four sub-cycles with different clocks are unwinding at different speeds: carrier underwriting margin (late-cycle, priced), reinsurance/ILS capital (recognition, but margin cushion still wide), personal lines/Florida reform (dispersed, partly priced), and broker commission economics (earliest-stage, least repriced).

Insurance brokers — commission & organic-growth cycle

EARLYpricing12.5/20
phase 1.0aware 4.0asym 5.0runway 2.5
phase late-cycle organic deceleration, still masked by M&Aclock Distinct from underwriting-margin cycles: brokers earn roughly 10-20% commission on gross premium, so revenue tracks (exposure growth + rate) x retention with a natural 2-4 quarter lag behind carrier pricing as renewals roll through. An active M&A roll-up sub-cycle (Brown & Brown, Gallagher, Baldwin, Goosehead as serial acquirers) can mask organic deceleration for several quarters via reported (not organic) revenue growth.asymmetry high

Where on the clock. Q2 2026 organic growth was still positive across the majors (Gallagher +5-6%, Marsh/MRSH +3-5%, Aon +5%, WTW +7%) but decelerating from prior-cycle highs. Brown & Brown's Q2 2026 organic revenue DECLINED -0.7% even as total revenue rose 30.4% via acquisitions — the first negative organic print among the majors this cycle — and Baldwin Insurance Group cut its FY26 organic growth guidance to mid-single digits. The softening carrier rate base (CIAB -1.2%) is starting to flow through to broker commission economics.

Who already knows. Staged early (analyst wrote “early/latent”). Broker equities have not materially repriced despite the first hard negative-organic data point: Aon +2.0%, Marsh/MRSH +3.2% and WTW +2.9% YTD, still trading near multi-year-high multiples pricing continued high-single-digit organic growth. Only Gallagher (-3.0%) and Brown & Brown (-11.0%) show the start of a re-rating, so most of the group hasn't moved even as the underlying data cracked in July 2026.

Why the payoff is shaped this way. This is the only sub-cycle where the leading data (Brown & Brown's first negative organic quarter, Baldwin's guidance cut) has moved meaningfully ahead of the average broker's stock price and multiple. AON, Marsh (MRSH) and WTW are still up YTD near unchanged valuations pricing continued high-single-digit organic growth — that gap between the newest data and what most of the group's prices reflect is the largest information edge of the four sub-cycles, in either direction.

Runway. Further quarters of decelerating organic growth are likely as the CIAB rate index stays negative through 2026-2027; the sub-cycle ends either when underlying commercial rates re-harden (a new cat/reserve shock) or when the M&A-driven 'reported vs. organic' growth gap becomes impossible for the market to ignore and multiples compress broadly.

Global property-catastrophe reinsurance & ILS/cat-bond capital cycle

RECOGNITIONfunding10.0/20
phase 2.0aware 2.0asym 3.0runway 3.0
phase downcycle in rate, but underwriting margins still wideclock Renews mainly at 1/1 (also 4/1 Japan, 6/1-7/1 US wind), driven by dedicated reinsurance and ILS/cat-bond capital chasing return after loss years; multi-year capital-in/capital-out swings of roughly 3-6 years.asymmetry medium

Where on the clock. Guy Carpenter's Global Property Cat Rate-on-Line index fell -12% at 1/1/26 (US -12%, Europe -15%, APAC -12%), independently corroborated by Howden Re (-14.7% cat reinsurance, -16.5% retro). Aon reports record $790bn global reinsurance capital as of 3/31/26, with alternative capital at a record $141bn. Yet reinsurer margins remain excellent (RenaissanceRe CR 72.8%, Everest Treaty CR 88.5%), so the softening is a capital-glut/price story that hasn't yet destroyed profitability.

Who already knows. Staged recognition (analyst wrote “recognition (well covered in trade press) but not fully priced into equities”). Cat bond risk premia have compressed sharply (average multiple 3.14x to 2.29x YoY, Q2 2026 spread over expected loss the lowest on record for a quarter) and Guy Carpenter/Howden data has been headline reinsurance-press news all year. Yet reinsurer stocks are still up YTD (RNR +15.1%, EG +10.0%, ACGL +3.3%), a mismatch between well-known softening data and equity prices that haven't chased it lower.

Why the payoff is shaped this way. Reinsurer combined ratios (73-90%) still carry 10-25 points of cushion versus break-even, so a 12-15% rate decline doesn't yet threaten profitability. The setup rewards patience over urgency: still-excellent margins persist while price normalizes, and equities haven't chased the ROL declines lower, leaving a modest gap in either direction.

Runway. Rate declines likely continue through the 6/1, 7/1 and 1/1/27 renewals absent a major loss event; the leg ends when a $50bn+ single event, or an accumulation of soft years, forces capital withdrawal and re-hardening.

ACGLArch Capital GroupDiversified (re)insurer spanning P&C, mortgage insurance and reinsurance, a lower-beta way to hold the cycle+3.3% (verified: $95.92 to $99.09)Smallest YTD gain of the three majors despite a still-strong 83.5% CR — the market is already discounting further softening here
Bear case. A single severe 2026 Atlantic hurricane season or Japan quake could sharply reverse capital flows and re-harden pricing overnight; conversely, continued softening compresses reinsurer ROE toward cost of capital by 2027-28, capping upside from here even without a loss event.

Personal lines (auto & homeowners) claims-cost & reform cycle

RECOGNITIONpricing6.8/20
phase 1.3aware 2.0asym 1.0runway 2.5
phase auto: mid-to-late cycleclock A 3-5 year mini-cycle inside the broader P&C clock, driven by loss-cost inflation (used-car/parts/medical/litigation) racing against regulatory rate-filing approval lag (state DOI approval can take 6-18 months); Florida homeowners has additionally been reshaped by a discrete 2022-23 legislative tort-reform shock still working through the system in 2026.asymmetry low

Where on the clock. Progressive's Q1 2026 combined ratio was 86.4% and Allstate's underlying auto CR was 89.5% — both show margin fully restored via 2022-24 rate hikes. In Florida, Citizens approved its first rate DECREASE since 2015 (avg -2.6% statewide, up to -13% in Miami-Dade), Citizens' policy count fell to a 25-year low of 293,465 from a 1.42M peak, and 17 new insurers entered the market — the reform cycle is already well advanced.

Who already knows. Staged recognition (analyst wrote “recognition/priced, with real within-theme dispersion”). Allstate is +27.1% YTD and Universal Insurance Holdings (a pure-play FL homeowners specialist) is +30.8% YTD — both look priced for the recovery story. Progressive, by contrast, is -6.6% YTD after a ~9% single-day drop on 2026-07-15 when policy-in-force growth decelerated from 8% to 7% YoY, showing the market actively re-rating growth deceleration in real time. HCI Group, another FL specialist, is -6.9% YTD despite the same bullish FL narrative — a name-level divergence this scan cannot fully explain.

Why the payoff is shaped this way. The big, easy trade — auto/home margin restoration via 2022-24 rate hikes — is already reflected in Allstate's and Universal's prices; Progressive's post-earnings selloff shows the market now punishes growth deceleration immediately, so what remains is stock-specific dispersion (e.g., HCI vs UVE) rather than a cycle-level opportunity, further complicated by renewed CPI-driven loss-cost risk.

Runway. Ends either when loss-cost inflation reaccelerates enough to force a new hardening leg (bear case) or margins hold and growth reaccelerates (bull case); Florida homeowners still has multi-year room to normalize further toward non-crisis pricing.
