Bear case. If renewed severe-weather/hail loss activity combines with reaccelerating parts/labor/medical inflation (CPI already at 4.2%), auto margins could get squeezed again just as rate-fatigue and regulatory pushback limit further pricing power; Florida could also see a hurricane-driven reversal of the reform narrative in any active storm season.

Commercial & specialty (E&S) P&C carrier underwriting-margin cycle

EUPHORIApricing5.3/20
phase 1.3aware 0.5asym 1.0runway 2.5
phase late-cycle transitioning to downcycleclock Full hard-to-soft-to-hard underwriting cycle typically runs 8-12 years, driven by capital chasing ROE: high margins attract capacity, capacity competes on price, price cuts erode margin until a capital-depleting shock (mega-cat, reserve charge) restarts hardening. The current hardening leg began ~2018-19 (post reserve strengthening, Harvey/Irma/Maria, CA wildfires) and ran 33 straight CIAB quarters of rate increases before turning in Q1 2026.asymmetry low

Where on the clock. CIAB's composite index printed -1.2% in Q1 2026, ending the 33-quarter hardening streak, with large accounts down -2.7% (2nd straight quarterly decline). Yet underwriting margins remain strong (Travelers CR 83.6%, Arch 83.5%, Everest Wholesale/Specialty CR 95.2%) — a classic late-cycle pattern where price turns down before losses catch up.

Who already knows. Staged euphoria (analyst wrote “priced/euphoria for large-cap carriers, recognition for E&S specialists”). Travelers is +31.9% YTD at ~2.6x tangible book (TBVPS $147.41 as of 2026-06-30, price $382.45 as of 2026-08-05) — above its own 10-year P/TBV high of 2.51x (10-yr median 1.83x) — a decade-record multiple being paid for a peaking margin. Allstate is +27.1% YTD. By contrast E&S pure-plays have already partly repriced on growth-deceleration fears: Markel -12.6% YTD, Kinsale -5.2% YTD, RLI -1.8% YTD.

Why the payoff is shaped this way. The most liquid, highest-profile names (TRV, ALL, CB) have already re-rated to multi-year-high valuations while margins are only just beginning to soften, skewing risk to normalization rather than further upside for new capital; E&S names have partly repriced but aren't statistically cheap.

Runway. Headline pricing likely keeps softening for 2-4 more quarters before finding a floor; the leg ends via a major cat loss, a reserve-adequacy shock (casualty/social inflation), or a capital-withdrawal event.

Bear case. Reserve deficiencies can stay hidden for 2-3 years and only surface once a soft-market loss year forces reserve strengthening (as in 2000-01); social inflation and litigation funding keep eroding casualty lines even as property softens, so the downturn is not uniform across lines.
Best risk/reward in this segment. Insurance brokers (AON/MRSH/AJG/WTW/BRO) — the underlying carrier rate cycle has already turned negative and the first hard evidence (Brown & Brown's first-ever negative organic-growth quarter, Baldwin's guidance cut) just landed in July 2026, yet most broker stocks and multiples haven't moved, making this the sub-cycle with the largest gap between fresh data and price of the four.
AI-infrastructure overlap — excluded from this scan. Minimal genuine overlap. The only plausible AI-infra read-through is (a) rising data-center property/casualty and cyber-exposure accumulation inside commercial and E&S books (Chubb, AIG, W.R. Berkley and Lloyd's/E&S markets increasingly underwrite hyperscaler and colocation property, business-interruption and cyber risk) — a real but still low-single-digit-percent-of-book demand driver embedded inside the commercial/specialty sub-cycle above, not separately broken out in CIAB or company disclosure; and (b) carriers' and brokers' own AI/automation initiatives in claims and underwriting, which are a margin/opex lever, not a demand driver, and are excluded here. Neither is large enough to change the cycle-level conclusions above, and both are already covered by the separate AI-infrastructure programme, not this insurance-cycle lane.

Healthcare & biopharma

4 cyclesbest: ACA subsidy cliff 13.1/20 · rank 21

Healthcare in 2026 is NOT one cycle — it is two cycles going up and one going down, and the index masks all three. XLV is +6.0% YTD vs SPY +12.9% (live, 2025-12-31 close base), because SMID biotech (XBI +25.5%) and payers (UNH +25.0%) are re-rating while medtech and hospitals de-rate hard (IHI -13.6%, ISRG -33.8%, HCA -12.3%) on the December-2025 expiry of the enhanced ACA premium tax credits. The biotech funding winter has genuinely thawed (H1-26 VC $9.1B, 18 IPOs vs 8, M&A already past the full-2025 total) but is now a consensus trade — BofA's July FMS shows a net 32% overweight healthcare vs 14% in June. The one leg where the cycle has actually turned DOWN in 2026, and where a hard-dated binary catalyst sits in front of the 2027 open-enrollment window, is the coverage/utilization cycle.

Coverage & medical-cost (payer underwriting) cycle — payers up-leg, providers/medtech down-leg

RECOGNITIONpolicy + pricing (payer underwriting cycle)13.1/20
phase 3.1aware 2.0asym 5.0runway 3.0
phase downcycle for providers/medtechclock 3-5 year payer underwriting cycle: utilization surprise → MLR blowout → repricing and benefit cuts → margin recovery → competitive re-expansion → repeat. 2024-25 was the blowout; 2026-27 is the repricing recovery. Overlaid in 2026 by a POLICY step-change (enhanced ACA premium tax credit expiry) that shrank the insured pool. Provider and medtech volumes follow insured lives with a 2-4 quarter lag.asymmetry high

Where on the clock. Payer leg: UnitedHealth's Q2-2026 medical loss ratio was 86.7%, 180bp better than consensus, FY26 EPS guidance was raised to $19.50-20.00, and CMS finalized a CY2027 Medicare Advantage rate of +2.48% net (+4.98% including risk-score trend) on 2026-04-06 versus just +0.09% in the Advance Notice. Provider/medtech leg: the enhanced premium tax credits expired 2025-12-31, February-2026 effectuated exchange enrollment fell to 19.2M from 21.8M (-12%), and the volume damage is landing now — HCA's Q2-26 same-facility inpatient surgeries fell 2.3% and outpatient surgeries 3.4%, with a ~$400M pre-tax quarterly drag and a $1.0-1.2B FY26 EBITDA hit guided from exchanges. Intuitive guided 2026 procedure growth to 13.5-15.5% with the US moderating to ~12%, and fell 12% on 2026-07-17 to a 52-week low.

Who already knows. The payer up-leg is close to priced: UNH +25.0% YTD, CVS +24.9%, CNC +62.8%, ELV +11.6%, with UNH at ~20.9x the midpoint of its OWN raised FY26 guide. The provider/medtech down-leg is at early recognition — IHI -13.6% YTD, ISRG -33.8%, MDT -10.5%, HCA -12.3%, GEHC -14.4% — but consensus has now largely re-based numbers to assume permanent coverage loss, which is precisely what makes the reversal catalyst asymmetric rather than the price alone.

Why the payoff is shaped this way. This is the only leg of healthcare where the cycle has actually turned DOWN in 2026, so the plays are cheaper than a year ago rather than more expensive — and it carries a hard-dated, binary, poorly-modelled catalyst. The House already passed a three-year enhanced-PTC extension 230-196 on 2026-01-08 with 17 Republicans crossing over; the Senate is negotiating a scaled-back two-year version with income caps; and 2027 open enrollment starts 2026-11-01, two days before the midterms. Honest caveat: this is a catalyst setup, not a deep-value one — ISRG at ~42x TTM earnings AFTER a 34% drawdown shows the de-rating has not reached valuation support.

Bear case. The bear case is that the ACA coverage loss is permanent policy, not a cycle. If the Senate does not act before 2026-11-01, 2027 enrollment falls again, uninsured volumes compound, and hospital and medtech numbers reset lower for a second consecutive year — while ISRG at ~42x TTM earnings still has a great deal of multiple left to lose, and GLP-1 substitution independently keeps compressing bariatric and sleep-apnea volumes regardless of coverage. On the payer side the risk is symmetric: a +2.48% CY2027 Medicare Advantage rate against a cost trend still running near or above 4% means the margin recovery is one bad utilization quarter from breaking, UNH management itself has said commercial margin recovery may not complete until 2027 or later, and at ~21x its own guide the stock no longer discounts a stumble. In the sour scenario both legs lose: providers never recover volume and payers give back the repricing gains.

Pharma patent cliff 2026-2030 → forced external-innovation capex (M&A) cycle, under an MFN policy tax

RECOGNITIONcapex (replacement-innovation capital cycle) + policy11.5/20
phase 2.5aware 2.0asym 3.0runway 4.0
phase mid-cycle on M&A activity, early-cycle on the actual revenue lossclock ~10-12y between industry-wide LOE waves (2010-14 small-molecule cliff → 2026-2030 biologics cliff). The M&A response LEADS the revenue loss by 2-4 years because acquired assets need 3-5 years to reach the P&L — so the buying peak comes before the revenue hole, not during it.asymmetry medium

Where on the clock. Deal-making is already hot: 2026 biopharma M&A passed the FULL-YEAR 2025 total by 2026-06-22 ($123B), with H1-26 at 49 control deals / ~$149B disclosed and IQVIA modelling $140-160B for FY26. But the revenue cliff has barely begun — Keytruda's ~$30B franchise does not lose US exclusivity until 2028, Eliquis 2027-29, Ozempic (US) 2031. We are in the pre-emption phase: acquirers buying before the hole opens, exactly when target premiums are richest.

Who already knows. The 'big pharma is too cheap and the cliff is discounted' trade has already been run: MRK +21.9% YTD, AMGN +24.6% (sitting at its 52-wk high of $416.22 on 2026-08-04), BMY +18.0%. The other side of the cliff is also partly discovered — VTRS +41.8% YTD, and OGN +89.3% purely because Sun Pharma is buying it at ~$11.75B EV. Effectively priced on the acquirers; still recognition-stage on the toll-collectors. What is NOT priced: the 2028-2030 revenue hole itself, and MFN/tariffs as a permanent margin tax rather than a headline.

Why the payoff is shaped this way. You are paid to own the target pool and the toll-collectors, not the acquirers — acquirer multiples have already re-rated on a cliff whose P&L damage is still 2+ years out. Bolt-ons in the $1-5B band rarely replace $10-30B franchises on schedule, which is the structural reason this cycle keeps forcing more deals. The genuinely underweighted risk is policy: 17 MFN agreements covering ~86% of the US branded market plus a 100% tariff threat on non-participants is a durable price ceiling consensus has largely waved through.

VTRSViatrisSecond generics/biosimilar toll-collector; shows how fast this leg re-rates once the cliff narrative is understood+41.8%$17.65 against a 52-wk low of $8.74 — already +102% off the low, i.e. the 'other side of the cliff' idea is no longer undiscovered
Bear case. The acquirers have already re-rated on the premise that the cliff is 'manageable', but three things are not in numbers. First, bolt-ons at $1-5B have a poor historical record of replacing $10-30B franchises on schedule — the deals are being done at pre-emptive, elevated premiums precisely because the hole is visible. Second, MFN plus a 100% tariff threat is a permanent price ceiling whose full P&L effect only begins to show in 2027; Novo has already named it as a guidance driver. Third, the entire target-pool premium in SMID biotech is contingent on deal flow staying near $10B+/month — if antitrust, financing, or the political climate slows that, the mechanism currently supporting XBI valuations disappears and both legs deflate together.

Biotech funding winter → thaw (capital-markets reopening)

RECOGNITIONfunding11.0/20
phase 3.0aware 2.0asym 3.0runway 3.0
phase mid-upcycleclock ~6-8y capital cycle: IPO/follow-on window reopens → VC redeploys → company formation → 18-30 month lag before it lands as R&D spend in tools/CRO revenue. Gated by cost of capital and exit visibility (M&A). Last peak Feb-2021, trough 2023-24, thaw began H2-2025.asymmetry medium

Where on the clock. H1-2026 biotech VC hit $9.1B across 68 companies, the highest first half since 2022, and Q2-26 was the strongest funding quarter since Q4-2021 (TTM $95B, +69% YoY). 18 IPOs in H1-26 vs 8 in H1-25, with two all-time-record biotech IPOs (Kailera $625M Apr-26; Parabilis $670M on 2026-06-10). But this is still ~18 IPOs vs ~100 in 2021, seed/Series-A remains starved, and the downstream spend has not arrived — ICLR is -10.3% YTD and pharma R&D spend growth was cut to ~3% for 2026.

Who already knows. Honest read: no longer contrarian. XBI is +25.5% YTD 2026 and +76.2% from its 2025-08-05 close of $86.85; BofA's Global FMS shows institutions at a net 32% overweight healthcare in July 2026, up from 14% in June. That is consensus. It is not euphoria either: XBI at $153.01 is still -12.1% below its 2021-02-08 peak close of $173.99, NASDAQ biotech industry P/E is 14.5x, and the services/tools transmission leg has not happened at all.

Why the payoff is shaped this way. The headline vehicle has captured most of the easy re-rating (+81% off its 52-wk low of $84.52). Remaining asymmetry sits in the LAGGING second derivative — late-stage CROs, lab consumables, lab real estate — where funding has recovered but the spend has not yet landed and multiples are still trough-like. Second unpriced kicker: the entire rally happened with the 10Y at 4.63%, so a genuine easing cycle is an unspent call option rather than an already-consumed driver.

Bear case. This is an M&A-and-fund-flows thaw, not an innovation thaw. Early-stage formation is still starved (76% of H1-26 dollars in $100M+ megarounds, ~2/3 of rounds to already-clinical companies) and NIH basic-research cuts are shrinking 2029-32 pipeline feedstock. Critically, the entire +25% happened with the 10Y at 4.63% and with institutions already at a net 32% healthcare overweight — the marginal buyer is largely spent, and a rotation back into AI leadership can retrace it fast. The tools/CRO 'laggards' may be lagging for a real reason: total biopharma R&D spend growth was cut to ~3% for 2026 and ~2% for 2027, so the funding→spend transmission could be far weaker than the 2021 analogue implies.

GLP-1/obesity adoption S-curve maturing into a price/commoditization cycle

RECOGNITIONadoption-scurve → pricing10.3/20
phase 2.3aware 2.0asym 3.0runway 3.0
phase mid-cycle on volume, early-downcycle on priceclock Volume S-curve runs ~10-12 years from launch (Wegovy 2021) toward saturation. The PRICE cycle runs far faster: cash channels, oral formulations and ex-US loss of exclusivity started net-price deflation in 2025-26, roughly five years ahead of the 2031 US semaglutide expiry.asymmetry medium

Where on the clock. Volume is still on the steep part: Lilly's Q2-2026 revenue rose 48% YoY to $23.0B, Mounjaro $9.9B (+91%) and US Zepbound $4.9B (+44%), FY26 revenue guidance raised to $85-87B. But price has turned: Lilly's own release says Zepbound growth was 'partially offset by lower realized prices'; Novo guided 2026 sales AND profit DOWN 5-13% on 2026-02-03 and fell 18% the next day; semaglutide lost protection in Canada (2026-01-04), India (2026-03-20), plus Brazil and China in 2026; cash prices sit at $499/month across LillyDirect, NovoCare, Costco and Walmart. Volume up, price down, share concentrating in one winner.

Who already knows. The 2023-24 'infinite TAM' euphoria has already deflated. LLY is only +8.9% YTD while growing revenue 48% — a live multiple de-rating to ~32x the midpoint of its own raised FY26 non-GAAP EPS guide, versus 60x+ in 2024. NVO is -12.5% YTD at 8.05x EV/EBITDA TTM, a decade-low absolute multiple, -30.6% below its 52-wk high of $64.16. The market has moved from pricing infinity to pricing a price war — neither latent nor euphoric.

Why the payoff is shaped this way. First-order names are now fairly priced for the new regime, with the leader compounding into a compressing multiple — decent but not asymmetric. Asymmetry sits in two places consensus is not modelling: (a) the Medicare GLP-1 Bridge, which put eligible Part D beneficiaries on $50/month GLP-1s from 2026-07-01 through 2027-12-31 and which nobody has sized; and (b) the 'GLP-1 collateral damage' cohort, de-rated on a 2023-24 substitution narrative, where real-world adherence and durability data can unwind the discount (INSP is -34.9% YTD and -59.1% off its 52-wk high).

DXCMDexComThe counter-example that tests the substitution thesis: continuous glucose monitoring was supposed to be GLP-1 roadkill and is instead compounding — evidence that 'GLP-1 kills X' is often over-extrapolated+24.5%$82.66, within 5.7% of its 52-wk high of $87.63 and well above its 200-day average of $67.85 — the substitution discount has already unwound here
Bear case. The bear case is that this was never a cycle — it is one-way commoditization. Orals, four simultaneous ex-US generic markets, $499 cash pricing and $50 Medicare pricing can compress net price faster than volume grows, so the dollar TAM peaks well below the $100B-by-2030 forecasts even as patient counts keep rising. Lilly at ~32x forward is not a trough multiple and would look expensive if 2027 growth halves; Novo at 8x EV/EBITDA is a classic value trap if semaglutide erodes on four continents while CagriSema underdelivers against tirzepatide. And the collateral-damage reversal leg only works if substitution proves reversible — if adherence data holds up, names like INSP stay permanently impaired and 'cheap' never converts.
Best risk/reward in this segment. The coverage/medical-cost cycle's DOWN-leg (medtech and hospitals: IHI -13.6%, ISRG -33.8%, MDT -10.5%, HCA -12.3% YTD) — it is the only part of healthcare that has actually de-rated in 2026, and it carries a hard-dated binary catalyst consensus is not modelling: whether the Senate restores the enhanced premium tax credits before 2027 open enrollment opens on 2026-11-01, two days before the midterms, after the House already passed a three-year extension 230-196.
AI-infrastructure overlap — excluded from this scan. Direct AI-infrastructure demand inside this segment is genuinely small and I have excluded rather than counted it. AI-drug-discovery pure-plays are roughly 1-2% of XBI's weight (est.), and large-pharma AI compute contracts are well under 1% of a $10B+ annual R&D line (est.) — nowhere near enough to move any of the four cycles above. Life-science tools and CRO revenue exposure to datacenter capex is effectively nil; the shared input is scientific compute, under ~5% of tools revenue (est.). Device-embedded AI (GE HealthCare, Philips imaging algorithms) is software running on installed hardware, not datacenter buildout, so GEHC is treated purely as a hospital-capex-cycle name. The one overlap that materially matters is a FLOW linkage, not a demand linkage, and it contaminates every awareness-stage call above: BofA's Global Fund Manager Survey shows institutions at a net 32% overweight healthcare in July 2026, up from just 14% in June, coincident with roughly $7.18B of outflows from US growth funds in late July/early August 2026 — meaning a meaningful part of healthcare's 2026 relative bid is AI-de-risking money rather than healthcare fundamentals. That makes the biotech thaw's price leg partly a mirror image of the AI cycle, and it would reverse if AI leadership resumes. One asset-level crossover to flag rather than count: life-science REITs including Alexandria (ARE) have been redirecting some lab and land-bank capacity toward data-center use, so any 'lab demand recovery' thesis there is partly an AI real-estate call and should not be scored as biotech-cycle exposure.

Transport & logistics (US trucking, shipping sub-cycles, airlines, rail, parcel)

4 cyclesbest: Dry bulk 13.9/20 · rank 17

Four genuinely distinct clocks are running here and they are NOT synchronized. US truckload has just turned out of a ~3.5-year freight recession on a REGULATORY capacity shock (CDL/ELP enforcement), with spot topping contract for the first time since Feb 2022 — the contract repricing is still ahead. Crude tankers are at a rate PEAK driven by the Strait of Hormuz closure (shut since 2026-03-02) and are simultaneously at the top of the capital cycle: record VLCC ordering has pushed the orderbook to 31-35% of fleet with 83% of H1-26 orders landing 2028-29. Dry bulk is the cleanest supply-constrained upcycle (orderbook only 10.6% of fleet, Simandou tonne-miles ramping, BDI 3,063 and +53.6% YTD). Containers are the mirror image — a record 38.3% orderbook with 8.5m TEU delivering 2027-28, rates propped only by the Red Sea diversion absorbing 8-12% of capacity. Outside the four: airlines are mid-to-late cycle on capacity discipline but absorbing a large Hormuz fuel shock (UAL flagged ~$6bn fuel headwind, DAL Q2-26 net $1.6bn, +19% revenue; DAL +34.2%/UAL +18.7% YTD); rail is in a volume recovery (US intermodal +3.8% YTD through wk30, carloads +2.7%; CSX +41.0%, UNP +27.8% YTD) that is more merger/efficiency story than cycle; parcel is a pricing/restructuring story, not a cycle (UPS +8.5% YTD, still -43% over 5y). Nothing in this segment is latent — the cheapest thing here is the part of the truckload cycle that has not hit the P&L yet.

Dry bulk shipping cycle (Capesize/Newcastlemax)

RECOGNITIONnewbuild (shipping capital cycle) + commodity tonne-mile13.9/20
phase 2.9aware 2.0asym 5.0runway 4.0
phase mid-cycleclock 7-10y newbuild cycle, but this leg is supply-LED rather than demand-led: the orderbook is only 10.6% of fleet versus 27-31% for crude tankers and 38.3% for containers. The demand clock is unusually datable — Simandou ramps from 20-40Mt in 2026 to a 120Mt/yr steady state by end-2027/mid-2028, so the tonne-mile impulse has a two-year runway with a known end.asymmetry high

Where on the clock. BDI was 3,063 on 2026-08-05, up 53.6% YTD, +9.5% on the month and at its highest since June 3 after five straight up sessions — but still far below the 2021 peak above 5,000, so this is not a blow-off. Capesize 182 5TC was $37,181/day on 2026-07-13 with the BCI at 4,296 in late July. Corporate results confirm mid-cycle rather than peak: Star Bulk's Q2-2026 was its most profitable quarter since Q2-2022 (net income $144.9m, EBITDA $194.8m, TCE $24,486/day) and Genco's Q2 dividend rose 433% YoY to $0.80 — strong, but Star Bulk's $24,486/day TCE is roughly a third of what VLCCs are earning, i.e. this segment has not spiked. Crucially, the market absorbed ~40m dwt of 2026 deliveries (the heaviest since 2020) without breaking rates.

Who already knows. Large caps are up but not doubled: GNK +38.7% YTD, SBLK +45.5%, SB +58.5%, CMBT +64.9%, HSHP +70.0%. Micro caps ARE euphoric — EDRY +108.6% YTD and +156.1% over 1Y — which is the usual late-recognition tell in this segment. But the valuation anchors are the honest counterweight: Genco trades at ~0.99x the $25.80 consensus analyst NAV ($25.57 price) and Star Bulk at 1.28x book, versus tankers at 2.0-3.0x book. Paying about NAV for a segment with a 10.6% orderbook is not what euphoria looks like.

Why the payoff is shaped this way. This is the only sub-cycle in the segment where the supply side, the demand catalyst and the valuation all point the same way. Orderbook is 10.6% of fleet (Capesize ~8-11%), roughly a third of the crude tanker ratio and a quarter of the container ratio, and 33.5% of even that delivers after 2026 — so there is no near-term supply wall. Simandou is a dated, contracted, physically-committed tonne-mile impulse: 120Mt/yr on a route roughly 3x the Australia-China distance implies ~180 Capesizes versus ~64 for the Pilbara equivalent, a net +116 vessel-equivalent of demand, ~+10% to iron-ore tonne-miles and ~+3.5% to total dry-bulk tonne-miles at full ramp. And you are paying ~1x NAV to own it, with Genco's dividend up 433% YoY and Star Bulk paying out full operating cash flow after capex and debt service. Compare the tanker setup: 3x book for peak earnings against a 31% orderbook.

CMBTCMB.TECH (formerly Golden Ocean)Post-merger hybrid holding both a large Capesize fleet and crude tankers — deliberately flagged because it is NOT a clean dry bulk play+64.9%+64.9% YTD but the move double-counts the tanker peak; owning it as dry bulk exposure imports the 31% VLCC orderbook risk described in the tanker sub-cycle
Bear case. Dry bulk demand is fundamentally a Chinese property and steel derivative, and Chinese steel output has been flat-to-declining — a 2027 China stimulus disappointment breaks the thesis regardless of the orderbook. The Simandou tonne-mile case rests on an assumption that Guinean ore ADDS to global seaborne volume rather than DISPLACING Australian and Brazilian tonnes; Splash247 has argued explicitly that the market is overestimating it, and if Simandou substitutes for shorter-haul Pilbara cargoes the net Capesize demand is a fraction of the +116-vessel figure. Separately, a Red Sea normalization would SHORTEN dry bulk voyage distances — BIMCO names this as a direct negative for the segment, so dry bulk and containers are on opposite sides of the same geopolitical trade. 2026 deliveries at ~40m dwt are already the heaviest since 2020, and if owners respond to strong rates the way tanker owners just did, the 10.6% orderbook advantage can be ordered away within twelve months. Finally, BDI is a spot index and is already +53.6% YTD, so a meaningful amount of the good news is in the price even at ~1x NAV.

US truckload freight cycle (dry van capacity)

RECOGNITIONcapacity/inventory13.8/20
phase 3.8aware 2.0asym 5.0runway 3.0
phase early-upcycleclock 3-4y truckload cycle set by carrier formation vs exit against goods demand; order-to-seat lag ~2-3 quarters. The 2022-2025 down-leg ran ~3.5y, unusually long, which deepened the capacity hole. Contract rates lag spot by 2-3 quarters, so the P&L clock trails the rate clock.asymmetry high

Where on the clock. Spot inflected hard in H1-2026 and topped contract in June 2026 for the first time since Feb 2022 — the canonical truckload cycle-turn marker. Dry van spot linehaul was $2.38/mi week ending 2026-07-24, +45.6% YoY, with load-to-truck 10.23 (+74% YoY). But the Cass Truckload Linehaul Index (contract-weighted) was only 149.4 in June 2026, +5.5% YoY — i.e. the contract book has barely repriced. Carrier P&Ls confirm early-cycle: KNX Q2-26 adj EPS $0.63 (+80% YoY) yet TTM ROE is 0.6% and WERN is still TTM loss-making. Earnings have not normalized, let alone peaked.

Who already knows. Not latent — the equities front-ran the data: KNX +35.4% YTD / +67.1% 1Y, HTLD +39.0% YTD / +58.9% 1Y, SNDR +37.7% YTD, CVLG +62.3% YTD. But it is not euphoria either: KNX trades at 14.1x TTM EV/EBITDA on TROUGH EBITDA with 0.6% ROE, WERN at 11.5x on a TTM net loss. That is the classic early-cycle 'high multiple on depressed earnings' signature, not a decade-high multiple on peak earnings. Consensus recognizes the rate move; it has not yet marked the 2027 contract book.

Why the payoff is shaped this way. The un-harvested leg is the 2027 bid season. Historically OTRI sustained >12% for 8+ weeks precedes 5-10% contract increases within 1-2 quarters; OTRI was ~15.4% in mid-May 2026 with truck utilization at 69.6, the highest since Nov 2021. Spot is +45.6% YoY while contract (Cass linehaul) is +5.5% YoY — a gap that historically closes through contract, not by spot collapsing back. Truckload operating leverage is extreme: KNX TL adjusted operating income rose 69.4% YoY on only +5.5% revenue per loaded mile. A normal contract repricing of 8-15% would multiply that. The offsetting risk is real (see bear case) but the earnings have not been booked yet, which is what distinguishes this from the shipping sub-cycles where peak earnings are already in the TTM.

Bear case. The capacity exit here is REGULATORY, not economic — the March 2026 CDL immigration rule and ELP enforcement removed drivers by fiat, and fiat can be litigated, softened, or absorbed as displaced drivers re-enter through compliant channels. The supply response has already begun: Class 8 orders were +159% YoY in Feb 2026 and FMCSA logged a net inflow of carriers in Q1-2026, the first since Q2 2025. If contract rates reprice +8-15% into 2027, carrier economics are restored and re-entry accelerates within 2-3 quarters, capping the cycle early. Meanwhile the DEMAND side is not participating — Cass volumes and truckload tonnage are not driving this, capacity is; a goods-demand rollover (tariffs, consumer weakness) would collapse demand while supply returns, the worst combination. Finally the equities already discount a full margin recovery (KNX 14.1x EV/EBITDA), so the earnings must actually show up — a 2027 bid season that delivers only mid-single-digit contract increases would de-rate the group even as rates technically rise.

Container liner & charter-owner cycle

RECOGNITIONnewbuild (shipping capital cycle) with a geopolitical capacity-absorption prop6.8/20
phase 1.3aware 2.0asym 1.0runway 2.5
phase late-cycle / pre-downcycleclock 7-10y newbuild cycle. The 2021-22 pandemic-profit ordering boom delivers 2027-28: ~1,000 ships / 8.5m TEU across those two years, over 4x a normal two-year intake, peaking at ~2.2m TEU in 2027. Order-to-delivery lag ~3 years, so the supply is already built and unstoppable.asymmetry low

Where on the clock. Spot rates look healthy but are decaying from a peak that was itself artificial. The Drewry WCI was $4,255/FEU on 2026-07-30, down 3% w/w, after topping out at $4,639 on 2026-07-09 (highest since September 2024) and printing +61% YoY in early July. The strength is a capacity illusion: the Red Sea/Cape of Good Hope diversion is absorbing 8-12% of global container capacity, and the February 2026 partial return via Suez was aborted after US/Israel strikes on Iran. Meanwhile the orderbook has reached an all-time record of 12.98m TEU across 1,592 vessels = 38.3% of the cellular fleet, and both shock absorbers are gone — idle fleet is ~0.5% of capacity (near record low) and demolition is at a 20-year low (just 12 ships / 8,172 TEU scrapped in 2025). Fleet growth of ~3.6% against ~3% demand growth is already negative before the 2027 wave.

Who already knows. The market has correctly recognized the structural problem and is pricing it in the equities — which is precisely why there is no long asymmetry left. Liners and lessors trade at deep discounts to book: DAC at 0.62x book and 4.7x earnings, GSL at 0.82x book and 4.1x earnings, ZIM at 0.84x book with EV/EBITDA of 4.0x, and CMRE is the only decliner in the entire segment at -7.0% YTD. Share prices did rise on the rate spike (DAC +47.2%, ZIM +25.2% YTD / +67.1% 1Y, MATX +66.3% YTD / +84.1% 1Y) but the multiples never re-rated. That combination — rising prices, static sub-1x multiples — is the signature of a market that believes the earnings are temporary.

Why the payoff is shaped this way. For the liners, asymmetry is negative: you are buying peak-ish earnings ahead of the largest known supply wave in the segment's history, with the rate support resting on a geopolitical diversion that can end without notice. ZIM's TTM P/E of 32x on a 0.84x book already shows earnings collapsing while the orderbook is still at a record, and its 7.5% TTM dividend yield sits on a 6.4x payout ratio — a melting-ice-cube distribution. The one place modest asymmetry survives is the contracted charter-owners: Costamare has ~$6.1bn of contracted charter revenue with 96% of 2026 and 92% of 2027 fleet employment already fixed, so its cash flows do not track spot for two-plus years — but even there the discount can persist indefinitely without a corporate catalyst.

Shanghai-Genoa -6% to $5,630; Shanghai-Rotterdam -3% to $4,677 as of 2026-07-30Drewry WCI · Cyprus Shipping News / theDCN weekly WCI reprints
  • Containership orderbook at an all-time record share of fleet — 12.98m TEU across 1,592 vessels = 38.3% of the existing cellular fleet (up from 11.8m TEU / 34% in March 2026) as of mid-2026Alphaliner via Splash247 · BIMCO via Maritime Executive
  • The 2027-28 delivery wave — supply already built and undeferrable — ~1,000 newbuilds / 8.5m TEU delivering across 2027-28, over 4x the normal two-year intake; 2027 is the peak delivery year at ~2.2m TEU as of 2026 forecast for 2027-28Alphaliner via Maritime Executive · BIMCO
  • Both supply-side shock absorbers are exhausted before the wave arrives — Idle fleet ~0.5% of total liner capacity (near record low); demolition at a 20-year low — only 12 ships totalling 8,172 TEU scrapped in 2025 as of 2025 full year / mid-2026Alphaliner via The Loadstar · Maritime Executive

  • Supply-demand balance already negative, with overcapacity projected to peak in 2027 — Fleet growth ~3.6% vs demand growth ~3.0% in 2026; Sea-Intelligence projects cyclical overcapacity peaking in 2027 at levels comparable to 2016, with rate pressure persisting beyond 2026 (est.) as of 2026 estimates for 2026-27Drewry via IndexBox · Sea-Intelligence via Maritime Gateway

ZIMZIM Integrated ShippingThe most spot-exposed listed liner and therefore the segment's cleanest downcycle instrument — it earns and loses on the WCI directly+25.2%0.84x book and 4.0x EV/EBITDA but a 32x TTM P/E — earnings are already collapsing while the orderbook is at a record; the 7.5% TTM dividend sits on a 6.4x payout ratio and is not repeatable

Bear case. For the liners the bear case IS the base case: a record 38.3% orderbook, 8.5m TEU landing in 2027-28 at over 4x normal intake, zero idle capacity and 20-year-low scrapping as buffers, and spot rates held up only by a diversion that absorbs 8-12% of the fleet and could end on a single ceasefire headline. ZIM at a 32x TTM P/E on collapsing earnings with a 6.4x-covered dividend is the sharp end of that. The risk to positioning FOR the downcycle is symmetrical and real: the Red Sea has now failed to reopen twice (February and the July 7 escalation), tariff-driven front-loading keeps demand lumpy and occasionally explosive, carriers have shown they will defend rates with blank sailings, and the charter-owners' backlogs (CMRE ~$6.1bn fixed to 2027, DAC and GSL similar) mean their cash flows simply do not track spot for two to three more years — so 0.6-0.8x book can persist indefinitely without a catalyst in either direction. The honest conclusion is that this sub-cycle offers no attractive long entry and is dangerous to short, because the timing depends on geopolitics rather than on the supply schedule that is otherwise perfectly knowable.

Crude tanker cycle (VLCC/Suezmax)

EUPHORIAnewbuild / commodity-supply, with a geopolitical overlay3.3/20
phase 0.3aware 0.5asym 1.0runway 1.5
phase peakclock 7-10y shipping newbuild cycle; order-to-delivery lag ~3 years. 83% of H1-2026 orders deliver in 2028-29, which dates the next supply wall precisely. Overlaid on this is an event clock (Hormuz) that is measured in months, not years — the two must not be confused.asymmetry low

Where on the clock. Rates are at or through all-time highs: VLCC spot earnings averaged ~$175k/day in Q1-2026 and ~$140k/day in Q2 even with Hormuz shut, MEG-China TCE pushed past $200k/day (+440% YoY) and the index printed above $420k/day at the spike. Frontline booked 82% of Q2 VLCC days at $181,700/day. That is a rate peak. Simultaneously the capital cycle has flipped to its most dangerous phase: the VLCC orderbook went from ~2% of fleet in 2023 to 11.5% in 2025 to 31.4-35% by mid-2026, with 291 VLCCs on order and a record 60m dwt / 234 crude tankers contracted in 2026. And the single most reliable top signal in shipping has fired — a 5-year-old VLCC now costs ~$9m MORE than a newbuild, with resale premiums up to $45.5m, per Clarksons.

Who already knows. Honest read: the physical and asset markets are in outright euphoria (record ordering, secondhand priced above newbuild, >$10bn of VLCC asset investment in a single quarter — the highest ever for one sector per Clarksons). The equities are one notch behind at recognition-to-euphoria: INSW +86.8% YTD / +109.6% over 1Y, NAT +78.2% / +110.7%, FRO +74.0%, TEN +69.5%, ASC +55.2%. Key plays doubling over 1Y disqualifies any 'latent' label. The counter-evidence is that headline P/Es look low (FRO 9.4x, INSW 8.2x, DHT 8.7x) — but that is peak-cycle earnings in the denominator, and price-to-book of 2.0-3.0x is historically RICH for tankers, which spent most of the last two decades at 0.5-1.2x.

Why the payoff is shaped this way. You are being asked to pay 2-3x depreciated book for peak-of-cycle, war-premium earnings against a 27-31% orderbook that lands in 2028-29. Both the E and the multiple compress on normalization. The genuine bull points — ~44% of the VLCC fleet is over 15 years old, ~20% over 20, and ~18% of VLCCs are sanctioned and effectively out of the compliant fleet — are real and well known, and are precisely what is being used to justify the record ordering. Sanctions relief would return that ~18% to the compliant market on top of the newbuild wave. The dividends (FRO 8.2%, INSW 9.2%, DHT 8.2% TTM) are payout-ratio-linked to peak earnings and will fall with them, so they are not a floor.

  • VLCC spot earnings at/through record levels — ~$175,000/day average Q1-2026; ~$140,000/day Q2-2026; MEG-China TCE >$200,000/day (+440% YoY); index peaked >$420,000/day as of Q1-Q2 2026Veson Nautical Shipping Market Outlook Q3 2026 · Lloyd's List
  • VLCC orderbook-to-fleet ratio — the capital-cycle top signal — 31.4% (from 11.5% in 2025 and ~2% in 2023), orderbook nearly tripled to 291 vessels; MSI independently puts it at ~35% as of June 2026 as of 2026-07-13 (Xclusiv); June 2026 (MSI)Xclusiv Shipbrokers via Hellenic Shipping News · Maritime Strategies International via Safety4Sea
  • Total crude tanker orderbook and 2026 contracting, with delivery timing — 130m dwt orderbook = 27% of operational fleet; record 60m dwt / 234 vessels contracted in 2026; 83% of H1-26 orders deliver 2028-29 as of H1 2026BIMCO via Container News · Splash247
  • Secondhand-above-newbuild inversion — the classic cycle-top marker — A 5-year-old VLCC costs ~$9m MORE than a newbuild; resale premiums up to $45.5m over newbuild price; 10-year-old VLCC ~$115m, highest since 2008; first such inversion in the 60-year history of the VLCC as of Q1-Q2 2026Clarksons Research via Splash247 · Seatrade Maritime
  • The rate spike is an EVENT, not a cycle: Strait of Hormuz effectively closed to commercial shipping — Closed since 2026-03-02; ~2 escorted transits on 2026-08-02 vs ~73/day normally; Brent peaked near $118/bbl; Baker Hughes flagged no full reopening until H2-2026 as of 2026-08-05 (status); 2026-04-24 (Baker Hughes)CNBC · Hormuz transit trackers straits.live and
  • Fleet age and sanctioned tonnage — the structural bull case being used to justify the ordering — ~44% of the VLCC fleet is >15 years old, ~20% is >20 years; ~18% of VLCCs are sanctioned and effectively outside the compliant fleet as of H1 2026Lloyd's List fleet analysis · Tankers International 2025 review and 2026 outlook

INSWInternational SeawaysMixed crude + product fleet, lowest leverage of the large tanker names; the group's best-performing large cap this cycle+86.8%2.04x book and 4.5x EV/EBITDA on peak earnings; up +109.6% over 1Y — the clearest 'already recognized' marker in the segment
DHTDHT HoldingsVLCC pure-play with a conservative balance sheet (debt/equity 0.41x) and a fixed payout policy; the lowest-risk way to hold the peak+46.4%2.33x book, 8.7x TTM P/E, 8.2% TTM yield; interest coverage 21x — the balance sheet is not the risk, the E is
TNKTeekay TankersSuezmax/Aframax mid-size crude exposure with a historically large net cash position, so more of the peak cash is retained rather than distributed+39.3%$2.58bn market cap against a 52-week range of $42.88-$83.99 — has lagged FRO/INSW despite comparable fleet economics
TRMDTORM plcProduct tanker pure-play — the relative-value leg if crude routing normalizes but refined-product tonne-miles stay dislocated+46.2%$2.93bn cap, -17.9% over 3 months — the only large tanker name already rolling over, which is either an early warning or the entry
Bear case. 2026 tanker earnings are a war premium wearing a cycle costume. The Hormuz closure removed effective tonnage and inflated ton-mile demand; when it reopens, that unwinds fast — and it will unwind INTO a 130m dwt orderbook (27% of the fleet) with 83% of H1-26 orders delivering 2028-29 and the VLCC orderbook at 31-35% of fleet versus 2% three years ago. Owners are paying record prices for secondhand tonnage above newbuild cost, which in six decades of VLCC history has never been a mid-cycle signal. Sanctions relief on Iranian or Russian trade would additionally return ~18% of currently sanctioned VLCC capacity to the compliant fleet, compounding the newbuild wave. Equities at 2.0-3.0x depreciated book are capitalizing both peak earnings and peak asset values; a normalization toward $40-50k/day TCEs compresses the E and the multiple together, and the 8-9% dividend yields fall with the payout because they are ratio-linked, not fixed. The bull rebuttal — an old fleet and a shrinking compliant pool — is exactly the argument being used to justify the ordering that will end the cycle.
Best risk/reward in this segment. Dry bulk — it is the only sub-cycle where lean supply (10.6% orderbook vs 27-31% for crude tankers and 38.3% for containers), a dated and physically-committed demand catalyst (Simandou ramping to 120Mt/yr by 2028), and undemanding valuation (Genco at ~0.99x its $25.80 consensus NAV, Star Bulk at 1.28x book) all line up at once, with BDI at 3,063 still far below its 2021 peak — versus tankers where you pay 2-3x book for war-premium earnings ahead of a 31% orderbook. US truckload is the close second and the better pure-alpha idea, because the 2027 contract repricing (spot +45.6% YoY vs Cass contract linehaul +5.5% YoY) has not hit any P&L yet, but its capacity exit is regulatory and reversible and Class 8 orders are already +159% YoY.
AI-infrastructure overlap — excluded from this scan. Genuinely small, and concentrated in exactly one place: FLATBED trucking. DAT explicitly attributes part of flatbed's strength to data-center construction; flatbed spot hit an all-time high in June 2026 and was $3.05/mi in August, up roughly 40% YoY. Flatbed is on the order of 15-20% of US truckload spot volume (est., not separately disclosed), so if data-center and associated power construction drive perhaps a quarter to a third of that surge, it accounts for roughly 1-3pp of the headline truckload rate move (est.). I have deliberately built the truckload thesis on the DRY VAN leg — regulatory driver exit, spot topping contract, OTRI at 15.4% — none of which is AI-infra demand; the flatbed/data-center leg is excluded from the asymmetry case and belongs to the separate AI-infra program. Elsewhere the overlap is immaterial: rail intermodal +3.8% YTD is consumer and import freight, and while carload growth includes aggregates and steel that partly serve data-center builds, AAR does not break this out. Air cargo for GPUs and servers is real but its pure-play (Atlas Air) is private, so no listed name in this scan captures it. Dry bulk (iron ore, coal, grain, bauxite), crude and product tankers, and container liners have effectively zero AI-infra demand content — containers move some server racks but it is a rounding error against consumer goods. The more honest framing is that AI-infra is a COST headwind to this segment rather than a demand tailwind: data-center power draw contributes to the electricity and diesel cost base that airlines, truckers and railroads pay. Net: under 5% of this segment's 2026 cycle move is AI-infra demand in disguise (est.).

Semis ex-AI (analog / MCU / power)

4 cyclesbest: Auto semis (lagging leg) 17.5/20 · rank 6

The 2023-25 auto/industrial destock is over and the restock is loud: TI analog +26% YoY, Microchip declared the correction complete with distributor inventory at 26 days, Vishay just printed a 1.32 book-to-bill, and ECIA component sentiment hit a five-year high in June 2026. But the equities already paid for it — TXN +60%, STM +103%, UMC +145%, VSH +129% YTD — and TXN's 29x EV/EBITDA is above every year-end multiple of the past decade. Crucially, the segment is NOT one cycle: the industrial/broad-market leg is mid-cycle and richly priced, the automotive leg has barely turned and is the only genuinely under-owned piece (NXPI +6.5% vs SOXX +76%), the mature-node foundry pricing cycle is euphoric and largely an AI-supply-squeeze artifact, and the ex-AI SiC/power leg is still in outright downcycle. July 2026's 22% SOX crash reset price without resetting the fundamental cycle — that dislocation is the whole opportunity set.

Automotive semiconductor cycle — the lagging leg

EARLYinventory17.5/20
phase 4.5aware 4.0asym 5.0runway 4.0
phase early-upcycleclock 3-4y auto-chip inventory cycle running roughly 4-6 quarters BEHIND industrial, superimposed on a flat ~92M-unit global build. Tier-1 and OEM safety stock, not vehicle volume, sets the clock; content-per-vehicle is the secular overlay.asymmetry high

Where on the clock. The split is measurable inside single companies: ADI's fiscal Q2 2026 industrial revenue grew 56% YoY while automotive grew just 2%; onsemi's analog/mixed-signal segment (auto+industrial) was still -2% YoY in Q2 2026. The turn is now visible but young — NXP's Q2 2026 automotive revenue rose 12% YoY and STM's automotive rose 16% YoY / 14% QoQ in Q2 2026. This is quarter one or two of an upswing, versus quarter six for industrial.

Who already knows. The equities have not re-rated. NXPI is +6.5% YTD 2026 against SOXX +76.2% — a ~70pt relative deficit — while growing revenue 19% YoY, and trades at 19.6x TTM P/E with a 5.1% FCF yield. Melexis, the purest listed auto-sensor play, is +24.4%; indie Semiconductor is -4.0%. UBS cut NXPI to Neutral on 2026-08-03 citing China auto inventory-correction risk, which is the classic marker of an unloved early leg rather than a crowded one.

Why the payoff is shaped this way. You get the same restock mechanics as the industrial leg at roughly half the multiple, with a real second leg (content-per-vehicle in SDV, electrification, ADAS, UWB) still ahead. NXPI at 13.5x EV/EBITDA versus TXN at 29.0x is a ~55% valuation gap between two companies both growing revenue ~19-23%. The risk is honest and specific — China — but it is priced in a way the industrial leg's risk is not.

Bear case. Auto units are not growing — 92.6M builds in 2026 is -0.4% YoY with only Greater China revised down, so all of the semiconductor growth must come from content-per-vehicle at ~5-8% a year. That is thin cover if China cracks: Chinese EV sales fell 21% YoY in Q1 2026, January passenger-car volumes fell ~20%, over half of dealers are loss-making, and auto-grade memory prices are up over 150% (an AI-memory spillover) squeezing OEM bills of material to a 2.9% industry margin. NXP already carries 11 weeks of channel inventory versus 9 a year ago — much of which Tier-1s built as a hedge after the 2025 Nexperia export shock. If that hedge unwinds, the 'restock' reverses into a second destock, exactly the scenario UBS flagged on 2026-08-03, and the cheap multiple turns out to be cheap for a reason.

Wide-bandgap power (SiC/GaN) capacity-overhang shakeout, ex-AI

RECOGNITIONcapex11.0/20
phase 3.0aware 2.0asym 3.0runway 3.0
phase downcycleclock 5-7y capex/overbuild digestion cycle: the 2019-2024 SiC capex boom created substrate and device capacity sized for an EV S-curve that decelerated. Yole's base case has the glut clearing only in 2027-2028; the clock is set by capacity retirement and Chinese substrate pricing, not by EV demand alone.asymmetry medium

Where on the clock. Wolfspeed's fiscal Q3 2026 revenue was $150.2M, down 19% YoY and 10.6% sequentially, with fiscal Q4 guided to $140-160M — still falling in mid-2026. STMicroelectronics' Power & Discrete revenue grew just 3.7% in Q2 2026 while its Analog/MEMS/Sensors segment grew 26%. onsemi's Analog & Mixed-Signal segment was -2% YoY. Yole's December 2025 work put 2025 upstream substrate utilisation near 50% and device lines near 70%, with overcapacity persisting to 2027-2028. Every ex-AI power line is still contracting or flat.

Who already knows. The CYCLE is under-recognised but the listed VEHICLES are not cheap, because they have been re-rated on AI datacenter power instead: WOLF +44.7%, NVTS +73.0%, POWI +74.3%, ROHM +111.4% and AEHR +389.3% YTD 2026 (live-verified). That is the trap — you cannot currently buy the ex-AI SiC trough through the obvious names without paying for an AI-power story that is out of scope. The genuinely early expressions are X-FAB (+31.2%, 57% below its 2026 high) and Wolfspeed's post-restructuring equity stub.

Why the payoff is shaped this way. On a 2-3 year view the setup is classic deep-cycle: capacity retirement is underway (Wolfspeed cut capex ~90% and opex ~$200M annualised), the demand driver still grows (global EV +11% to >23M units in 2026), and pricing is at levels that force consolidation. But there is no trough confirmation, Yole says 6-10 more quarters of glut, and the listed proxies already carry AI-power premiums. High long-run asymmetry, low 6-12 month asymmetry — net medium.

Bear case. This may be a margin-destruction event rather than a cycle. Chinese substrate makers at ~$200 per 6-inch wafer and ~40% of global wafer/epi capacity are running the solar and LED playbook: volume recovers, price never does, and western incumbents keep share only in the low-volume niches. Yole's own base case is 6-10 more quarters of glut, which is a long time to hold a loss-making asset. Wolfspeed is still shrinking 19% YoY post-restructuring with a levered capital structure. And the cruelest part for an ex-AI thesis: the listed proxies have already been bid up 45-390% in 2026 on AI datacenter power demand that is explicitly outside this scope — so if AI capex digests, these stocks fall on the AI leg while the EV leg is still in its own trough, and you take both drawdowns at once.

Broad analog / MCU / passive channel-inventory restock (industrial-led)

RECOGNITIONinventory10.5/20
phase 2.5aware 2.0asym 3.0runway 3.0
phase mid-cycleclock 3-4y channel-inventory cycle: destock 2H2022-1H2025 (~11 quarters, unusually long), restock 2H2025-2027. Clock is driven by distributor weeks-of-supply and OEM safety-stock policy, not end demand.asymmetry medium

Where on the clock. Every restock marker has already fired: Microchip declared the inventory correction complete on 2026-05-07 with days-of-inventory down to 185 from a 266 peak and distributor inventory at just 26 days; TI's analog segment grew 26% YoY in Q2 2026 with Q3 guided to $5.65-6.15B; Vishay's Q2 2026 book-to-bill was 1.32. But TI's own days-of-inventory is still ~207 (TTM) vs 117-161 in 2021-22, so the industry is refilling, not depleted — mid-cycle, not early.

Who already knows. Not latent by any measure: TXN +60.1%, LFUS +82.3%, DIOD +73.5%, VSH +129.0%, POWI +74.3% YTD 2026 (live-verified). TXN trades at 29.0x TTM EV/EBITDA vs a 2016-2025 year-end range of 12.1-24.2x — a decade high. The tell that it is recognition rather than full euphoria: the whole group still LAGS SOXX (+76.2% YTD), and VSH fell 14.6% on 2026-08-05 despite beating with a 1.32 book-to-bill, i.e. good news no longer pays.

Why the payoff is shaped this way. Real earnings runway remains — Microchip's 61.6% non-GAAP gross margin is still ~6pts below its FY23 peak, Vishay's 22.6% adjusted GM is ~5pts below 2022-23, STM's 34.8% is ~14pts below peak — so a full margin normalisation is not yet in the P&L. But multiples already discount it, and July's 22% SOX drawdown only took TXN back to ~28x forward. You are paid for margin recovery, not for the cycle turn, which is consensus.

Bear case. The restock may be a bullwhip, not a recovery. 32-bit industrial MCU and PMIC lead times have stretched to 20-40+ weeks (auto/industrial families to 52 weeks) precisely because AI infrastructure is eating mature-node capacity — that is the exact 2021 setup that induced panic double-ordering and produced the 2022-23 bust. Order books look strong because customers are hedging scarcity, not because sell-through improved: global light-vehicle build is -0.4% in 2026 and China passenger-car volumes fell ~20% YoY in January. Meanwhile the multiple leaves no room — TXN at 29x EV/EBITDA is a decade high, and VSH falling 14.6% on a beat with a 1.32 book-to-bill shows the marginal buyer is already fully positioned.

Mature-node foundry utilisation & pricing cycle

EUPHORIApricing5.0/20
phase 1.0aware 0.5asym 1.0runway 2.5
phase late-cycleclock ~4-5y utilisation/pricing cycle nested inside a 7-10y foundry capex cycle. Price hikes lag utilisation by two to three quarters; the down-leg is set by when Chinese greenfield mature-node capacity actually lands.asymmetry low

Where on the clock. UMC ran 85% utilisation in Q2 2026 with gross margin up 3.3pts QoQ to 32.5%, and guided Q3 above 90% with firm ASPs and mid-30s margins. Vanguard grew revenue 21.8% YoY with gross margin at 32.3% versus 28.0% a year earlier and guided ASPs +2-4% with utilisation moving from 80% to 85-90%. TrendForce has top-10 8-inch utilisation at ~88% in 2026 heading to ~90% in 2H26, with foundry prices already up 5-15% between Q1 and Q2 2026 and a third hike round extending into 2027. Utilisation cannot go much above 90% — from here the cycle is priced off ASP alone.

Who already knows. UMC is +145.4% YTD 2026, Vanguard +70.5%, Tower +79.8%, SkyWater +78.7%, GlobalFoundries +41.6% — all live-verified. UMC trades at 3.43x book, above its 2021-mania peak and roughly triple its 2010-2020 typical 0.7-1.5x range, at 18.2x TTM P/E for a business whose long-run earnings power is mid-cycle single-digit-margin. When a foundry that spent a decade at sub-2x book gets to 3.4x on a pricing cycle, awareness is not the bottleneck.

Why the payoff is shaped this way. The upside left is one more ASP round already telegraphed by TrendForce and the companies themselves; the downside is a double reversal — AI capex digestion frees 8-inch capacity at the same moment Chinese greenfield capacity lands. Book multiples at decade highs on a cycle whose driver (supply cuts) can be reversed by decision rather than by demand is a poor payoff structure.

5347.TWOVanguard International SemiconductorPure 8-inch specialty foundry (PMIC, display driver, power); the single most direct beneficiary of 8-inch scarcity pricing+70.5%Gross margin 32.3% is still ~8pts below its 2022 peak, but the stock at NT$157 sits 30% below its 2026 high of NT$223 — the cheapest way to own the ASP leg
GFSGlobalFoundriesWestern specialty/mature-node capacity with auto and RF exposure; a geopolitically-favoured second source as customers dual-source away from China+41.6%$49.46 versus a 2026 high of $92.55 — down 47% from the high, the deepest drawdown among the foundry cohort
TSEMTower SemiconductorSpecialty analog foundry (SiGe, RF, power management, silicon photonics) — the highest-mix, highest-margin mature-node asset+79.8%Fell 11.9% on 2026-08-05 alone; $211 versus a 2026 high of $319.94 — a 34% drawdown that has already de-rated the pricing narrative
0981.HKSemiconductor Manufacturing International (SMIC)The largest mature-node foundry in China — the entity whose capacity additions determine when this pricing cycle ends-6.2%The only major foundry DOWN in 2026 (HK$67.00 vs HK$71.45 at 2025 year-end) despite the same utilisation tailwind — a pure geopolitical-discount anomaly worth understanding before owning the cycle
Bear case. This is a supply-cut cycle wearing a demand-cycle costume. Utilisation is near 90% because TSMC and Samsung retired 8-inch lines and AI-server power ICs absorbed what remained — TrendForce and Vanguard both say so explicitly. Both legs are reversible: if AI capex digests, the PMIC pull disappears overnight, and the shuttered capacity was a management decision that can be un-made. Underneath sits the real overhang — China at roughly half of global mature-node capacity heading to over 31% of 28nm by 2027, an LCD/solar-shaped setup where aggressive expansion meets softening demand in late 2026 or 2027. UMC at 3.4x book, above its 2021 peak multiple, prices none of this. And SMIC being down 4.8% YTD while UMC is up 145% is a warning that the market is paying for the geopolitical premium, not for the wafers.
Best risk/reward in this segment. The automotive semiconductor leg: it is the only sub-cycle where the fundamental turn is confirmed (NXP revenue +19% YoY with automotive +12%, STM automotive +16% YoY) while the equities have not re-rated at all — NXPI is +6.5% YTD against SOXX +76.2%, trading at 13.5x EV/EBITDA versus TXN's 29.0x, meaning you are paid roughly a 55% valuation discount to own the same restock mechanics four to six quarters earlier in their arc.
AI-infrastructure overlap — excluded from this scan. Roughly 25-40% (est., not directly disclosed) of this segment's 2026 revenue growth is AI-infrastructure demand wearing an ex-AI label, and it is heavily concentrated. Hard evidence: onsemi's entire Q2 2026 growth came from Power Solutions +19% YoY on AI datacenter while its Analog & Mixed-Signal segment (auto/industrial) was -2%, with 2026 AI datacenter revenue guided to more than double; Infineon expects over EUR1.6B of AI datacenter revenue against ~EUR16.3B FY26 guidance (~10% of revenue) and it was the swing factor in its Q3 record; ADI's Communications segment was $554.7M (15.3% of fiscal Q2 revenue) up 79% YoY "driven significantly by data center", and its fastest-growing industrial sub-segments were ATE and electronic test — which is AI-chip test equipment; TI named "data center" as a growth driver alongside industrial and automotive; STM guides Q4 2026 above $4B "driven mainly by engaged customer programs in AI data centers". The mature-node foundry sub-cycle is the most contaminated of the four: TrendForce attributes the ~88-90% 8-inch utilisation and the 5-15% price hikes directly to AI-server power ICs plus TSMC/Samsung 8-inch capacity cuts, and Vanguard explicitly credited AI-server PMIC demand for its Q2 2026 beat — so arguably half of that sub-cycle's driver is AI-infra, which is why I staged it euphoria/low-asymmetry rather than counting it as an ex-AI recovery. AI also shows up as a NEGATIVE overlap that is genuinely ex-AI in its effect: AI memory scarcity has pushed automotive-grade memory prices up over 150%, compressing Chinese OEM margins to ~2.9%, and AI cannibalisation of mature-node capacity has stretched 32-bit MCU and PMIC lead times to 20-52 weeks, which inflates order books with hedge-buying that is not end demand. The cleanest AI-free reads in this segment are Melexis, indie Semiconductor, Elmos, X-FAB and Vishay's passive components — I weighted those most heavily when staging awareness.

If you only do one thing. Stop hunting for undiscovered stories. This scan found exactly one latent cycle in 52 — but 16 scoring 14 or better, most of them cycles the market has already recognised, priced for continuation of the bad news, and stopped re-underwriting. The highest-value habit is the disaggregation that Analog Devices' own quarterly makes free: when one segment inside a company grows 56% and another grows 2%, the market usually prices the average. That spread is where the remaining edge lives.

查核top

Every load-bearing number in this report was produced with a two-source requirement and an as-of date, then a set of independent adversarial auditors re-checked the most consequential claims against primary sources they had to find themselves. Corrections were applied to the data before this report was rendered, not footnoted around.
